MEETING: Sine Die Meeting and First Regular Meeting | DATE: 2019-01-07 | CLIP_ID: 696 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 696

 
  RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
 OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 Office of City Clerk 
 
 January 7, 2019 
           
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in “Sine Die” Session on this date at 
2:30 p.m. in Council Cha mbers.  Those present were: MAYOR:  Marian J. Orr ; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS: Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, M r. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn , Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. 
Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White .  Also present were Kylie Soden , Deputy City Clerk, and 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. 
 
 Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Orr stated the purpose of the session was to 
ceremonially recognize outgoing the Councilman Mr. Johnson (whose term of office is expiring) 
for his years of service on City Council.   
 
 At Mayor Orr’s request, Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk, read a ceremonial resolution in 
its entirety honoring outgoing Council member Richard Johnson , entitled as follows: “A 
Ceremonial Resolution in Recognition of the Honorable Richard Johnson for his Dedication and 
Outstanding Contributions to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Brian Olson, 
Department Commander , Military Order of the Purple Heart , Chapter 800 , and Barry Gasdek,  
Regional Commander, Military Order of the Purple Heart, expressed appreciation to Mr.  Johnson 
for supporting the organization  and Veterans, and for his effort  and involvement related to the 
Resolution proclaiming Cheyenne a Purple Heart City, and presented him tokens of appreciation. 
Mr. Johnson commented on his years of service as a City Council member, fundraising efforts and 
projects he has been involved with, and expressed appreciation to his  colleagues and to his parents 
for their support.  Mr. White expressed appreciation to Mr. Johnson and presented him with a 
Stetson hat on behalf of the City Council. (#5969) 
 
 Mayor Orr announced the First Regular Meeting of the governing body  would convene in 
City Council Chambers immediately following the closure of the “Sine Die” session. 
 
 There being no further business previously noted for the “Sine Die” Session, Mayor Orr 
adjourned the meeting at 2:42 p.m. 
 
       Submitted by, 
 
 
       Kylie Soden 
       Deputy City Clerk 
 
 This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
PUBLISHED: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  January 12, 2019 
    RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
 OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 Office of City Clerk 
            
 January 7, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne assembled for its First Regular Meeting for 
2019 on this date at  2:46 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Purpose of the First Regular Meeting was 
to recognize and administer oaths of office to those individuals who were elected, or re -elected, 
at the most recent general election to serve as City Council members, as well as pro vide for 
certain administrative functions. 
 
 Mayor Orr  welcomed those in attendance and introduced Diane Sanchez, Clerk of 
District Court , who was present to administer oaths of office to officials.  Ms. Sanchez  
administered the oath of elected office to the following newly elected, or re-elected, City Council 
members: Scott D. Roybal, Ward I; Mark D. Rinne, Ward II; and Ken A. Esquibel , Ward III.   
Mr. Esquibel and Dr. Rinne introduced family in attendance. 
 
 Mayor Orr officially called the First Regular Me eting of the governing body to order at 
2:50 p.m.  Those present were: MAYOR – Marian J. Orr ; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Case, 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor  and Mr. 
White.  Also present were Kylie Soden , Deputy City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl , City Attorney.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Election of  City Council President for 2019 .  Mayor Orr announced, pursuant to City 
Code, the mayor conducts the election for Council President but does not cast a vote .  Mr. Cook 
moved to nominate and elect Mr. Case as City Co uncil President for 2019 , seconded by Mr. 
Luna.  Mr. Case accepted the nomination.  There being no further nominations, Dr. Rinne moved 
that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote.  Motion to nominate and 
elect Mr. Case as City Council President for 2019  carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote. 
 
 Election of City  Council Vice -President for 2019 .  Mayor Orr announced, pursuant to 
City Code, the mayor conducts the election for Council Vice -President but does not cast a vote.  
Mr. Luna  moved to nominate and elect Mr. Cook as City  Council Vice -President for 2019, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Cook accepted the nomination.  There being no further nominations, 
Mr. Luna moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -  
all members of the gove rning body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote.  Motion 
to nominate and elect Mr. Cook as City Council Vice-President for 2019 carried.  Voting “yes” -  
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote. 
 
 Approval of Appointment of City officials (pursuant to Title 2 of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne) as follows:  Amy Allen , City Engineer ; Charles W. Bloom , Planning and 
Development Director; Ronald Downey, City Treasurer; Sylvia Lee Hackl, City Attorney; Randy 
K. Hays , Assistant City Treasurer (Board of Public Utilities ); Greg Hoggatt Sr. , Chief of the 
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department ; Ronnald J. Jeffrey, Juvenile Court Judge; K ristina F. 
Jones, City Clerk/Assistant City Treasurer; Brian N. Kozak, Chief of the Cheyenne Police 
Department; Teresa Moore, Community Recreation and Events Director ; Mark T. Moran, 
Municipal Court Judge, and  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director .  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve the appointments “en masse”, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral 
appointments).  Following announcement of the vote, Diane Sanchez, Clerk of District Court,  
administered the oath of appointed office to the City officials.   
 
 Mayor Orr announced light refreshments would be served in the Council annex adjoining 
Council Chambers immediately following the meeting. 
 
 There being no further business previously noted for the First Regular Meeting, the 
meeting was officially adjourned at 2:56 p.m. 
 
       Submitted by, 
 
 
       Kylie Soden 
       Deputy City Clerk 
 
 This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
   
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  January 12, 2019

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 14, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Scott Roybal. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 20, 2018.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Sine Die Session of the Governing Body on January 7, 2019 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 7, 2019 .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MR Medium Density Residential to

Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present. (#4256)

Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main m otion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4258) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Creating Chapter 12.28, Downtown Graffiti Abatement

12-103(b) to the Director of the City of Cheyenne Planning and Development Department  –

(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 10 th

[CA] RESOLUTION – Designating local banks as de positories for municipal funds.  Motion

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to execute State Historic

Project, Cheyenne Façade  Improvement Program Project)  – referred to Finance Committee.

[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events

Constructors of Wyoming, LLC, to add the Guaranteed Maximum Price for Phase I abatement and 
demolition of existing site for the Municipal Court Building Project. (1% Specific Purpose Sales 
Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-10-19 for furnishing one truck for the Fleet Division and one truck 
with a plow for the Facilities Division (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax and general fund).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-11-19 for furnishing one new 2018-2019 cab & chassis with 45-
foot aerial device body for the Forestry Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Dillinger’s, 
LLC d/b/a Dillinger’s, 1601 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by One 
East Sixteenth, LLC).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Bar and grill liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for DBS Napoli’s 
Cheyenne, LLC d/b/a Napoli’s, 1901 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming.   Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. 
 
  
Appointments of Diane Gonnelly to the Cheyenne Housing Authority Board, and Michelle 
Garcia to the Board of Adjustment.   Mr. Laybourn moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments). 
 
[CA] Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the February 21, 2019 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Winter Conference Business 
Meeting to be held in Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King Jr. Holiday on Monday, 
January 21, 2019, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in 
the Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil A venue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: 
Finance Committee will meet Tuesday, January 22, 2019 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services 
Committee will meet Wednesday, January 23, 2019 at Noon. 
 
Under other business: 
 
 Noel Griffith, Jr. and Jaime Winters, Noel Griffith, Jr., & Associates Architects, provided 
a presentation including handouts (on file with City Clerk’s office)  of the exterior design for  the 
proposed Municipal Court Building Project.  Mr. Winters and Mr. Griffith provided information 
related to materials that will be used for the project and explained the design will include features 
that pay homage to the original building design.  They advised they are working with the Planning 
and Development Department on the streetscape design.  During discussion and inquiry related to 
the possibility of including historical design elements, Mr. Griffith advised that can be considered 
in the proposed design.    
 
 Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, provided information relating to 
issues involving the recent holiday sanitation pick-up process.  
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:24 p.m.         
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-01-28 | CLIP_ID: 700 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 700

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 28, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Also present: Kris Jones , City Clerk, and 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Luna and Mr. Cook, Agenda Item #18(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 14, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Creating Chapter 12.28, Downtown Graffiti Abatement 
Pilot Program, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne,  Wyoming, and providing procedures for the operation thereof . Vicki Dugg er, 
Executive Director, Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority, provided information on the  
current graffiti abatement program process  which is an “opt -in” approach , advising letters and 
abatement forms  were sent to property owners that were affected  by graffiti  with only 10 .5% 
returned the forms. The DDA is establishing a pilot program in conjunction with the ordinance to 
take a more proactive approach  and has approved a $5,000 contribution until end of the current 
fiscal year and a $15,000 commitment for next fiscal year. Ms. Dugger explained graffiti 
abatement work under the proposed program, noting it would be contracted out and spoke in favor 
of it being an “opt-out” program.  She provided information on properties in the downtown district 
advising that many owners are not local.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provide d 
additional information on the current program, explaining volunteers paint over graffiti after a 
waiver is submitted by the property owner.  Richard Johnson spoke about attendance at a public 
meeting regarding the program  and spoke in support of  an “opt-in” provision and asked for 
clarification on how possible citations would affect those who chose to “opt -in”. Reporting for 
Public Services Committee,  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading , seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Dr. Rinne  moved to amend by  substitute dated January 24, 2019, which reflects an 
  
amendment made at the January 23, 2019 Finance Committee meet ing to Section 12.28.040 to 
reflect the program as “opt-in” instead of “opt-out”, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion included: 
Support for an “opt -out” program,  lack of success of the current process, spread of g raffiti if 
untreated, unresponsive property owners, benefits of both an “opt -in” or “opt -out” program, 
concerns with altering property without the owner’s consent and the need for an accurate database 
of property owners in the downtown area.  Upon inquiry related to charging individuals for graffiti, 
Chief Kozak explained there are few security cameras in the downtown alley areas with only two 
individuals being charged in the last several years, explaining those who do appear on camera are 
hard to identify.  He stated convictions typically occur when the Police Department receives a tip 
and reported upon inquiry  that currently white and grey paint is used due to cost. Motion to amend 
by substitute failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor.  
Voting “no” – Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr.  White.    Main motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, and Mr. 
White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, and Mr. Shanor.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Delegating the review authority authorized by W.S. 
§34-12-103(b) to the Director of the City of Cheyenne Planning and Development Department .  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), by adding indoor entertainment as a land use category 
permitted by right for Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD, adding a new Section 5.11 to de fer 
to the UDC standards where the PUD is silent on Development Standards, and, pursuant to Section 
2.2.1 of the UDC, amending The Village PUD map to reflect Lot 28 instead of Lots 30 and 31 of 
The Village Subdivision for the subject parcel, Cheyenne, Wyoming, (located west of and adjacent 
to Powderhouse Road, south of Old Town Lane) . Alessandra McCoy, Assistant City Attorney,  
explained a draft substitute has been circulated  based on  comments made during the  Public 
Services Committee meeting designating the  property as a conditional use instead of a use -by- 
right as originally proposed by added indoor entertainment. Based on concerns voiced regarding 
the existing Wyoming Downs location, the Police Department provided a memo outlining criteria 
for a safety/se curity plan for the proposed new site and current location .  Ms. McCoy clarified  
three options for the property are use-by-right, voting the ordinance  down or approving the 
amendment by substitute.  Criteria as recommended by the Police Department can be added under 
a conditional use during the site plan process along with staff conditions.  Charles Bloom, Director 
of Planning and Development,  provided information relating to Wyoming Pari -Mutuel 
Commission involving citations issued to establishments.   Frank Lam b, Wyoming Downs,  
explained hours of operation vary based on racing signals, confirmed proposed plan in conjunction 
with the Police Department to address security concerns, and provided informa tion relating to 
security staff and training.  Kelly Johnson spoke in opposition of the ordinance and location of the 
proposed establishment and provided statistics related to gambling and number of Police calls at 
the current Wyoming Downs location .  For the record, Mr. Case  expressed support of his 
neighborhood which is located in the vicinity of the existing business location and voiced concerns 
  
relating to appropriate locations for this type of business.  Upon inquiry, it was confirmed the 
proposed location for Wyoming Downs will be a secondary business location.   Frank Cole 
provided information regarding a proposed development of a new school on land near the property 
(southeast corner of Carlson and Powderhouse) and voiced  opposition relating to the proposed 
business location. Joe Svec, #1 Properties, explained it is anticipated the business will consist of a 
restaurant on the lower level and pa ri-mutuel machines in the upper level.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . White.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by  substitute 
dated January 25, 2019, which includes an amendment made at the January 23, 2019 Public 
Services Committee, meeting making indoor entertainment land use permitted as a conditional 
use, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Comments were made on use-by-right vs. conditional use.  Motion 
to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend 
by adding a site plan condition that states at a minimum, staff will require the six safety precautions 
recommended in the January 14, 2019 Police Department memo, before approving a site plan , 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak explained an officer has been assigned to 
the current location for about 4 months and has made safety improv ement recommendations that 
have not yet been implemented by the establishment and confirmed  if Wyoming Downs applies 
for a bar and grill liquor license, compliance checks w ould take place.  Mr. Bloom stated zoning 
violations can be reported by citizens  and any conditions placed on the site plan could be posted 
on the premise.  Mr. Case requested maps of the current and proposed Wyoming Downs locations 
including proximity to nearby schools, licensed daycares and churches prior to the February 5, 
2019 Public Services Committee.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Additional discussion included: Use -by-right vs. conditional use and law 
enforcement issues with current location, placing limitations on businesses if statutory 
requirements are met, site plan requirements, providing data and mitigating issues moving forward 
if ordinance is approved .  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Wiese Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat of Lot 3, Block 1, Wiese Subdivision, and a portion of Tract 
79, Allison Tracts, 2 nd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to E. 
Prosser Road, east of S. Greeley Highway).  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt with revised map, seconded 
by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5972)  
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Destin Heights, a plat of the W 1/2 NE 1/4, and the N 1/2 SE 1/4 NE 1/4 of Section 24, Township 
14N., Range 66 W. of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located southwest of the 
intersection of Beckle and Christensen Roads) .  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development 
Department, advised the intent was to include the revised map in the motion  at the recent Public 
Services Committee meeting but it was inadvertently omitted, noting staff continues to recommend 
approval with t he revised map and staff condition #1.  Mr. Lloyd stated for the record  staff 
condition #1 includes connection to the west subdivision, specifically Lupine Trail.  Tom Mason, 
MPO, spoke in favor of condition staff condition  #1 and reported a Planning Commission 
compromise was made for road dedication for the Lupine Rd. connection.  Upon inquiry by Donn 
Edmunds, Mr. Lloyd explained the property is in the county (within one mile from City) and there 
is no anticipation of  connection to city water or sewer services. Mr. Edmunds voiced concern  
  
relating to potential depletion of the Ogallala Aquifer.  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services, 
agent for the applicant, explained a geologist has been hired to conduct a water study due to  the 
number of lots and results will be available to the public.  Mr. Palma advised the property owner 
has reluctantly agreed to staff condition #1,  as long as there isn’t any construction  considered at 
this time and confirmed a historic farmhouse is not located on this property but is instead located 
on a separate property east of the City.  Mr. Palma confirmed the property consists of 19 five-acre 
lots which meets the county requirement for individual water wells and septic sewer systems.  He 
stated the road will be an “s” curve ending in a cul -de-sac, and a reason to keep the road going 
may be for long range planning or if the land becomes part of the City.  It was confirmed that prior 
to January 1, 2019, City approval was required for plats within a mile of the City limits.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to adopt with revised map, seconded by Mr . Case.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by adding  
staff condition #1, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  It was noted for the record the r ationale for staff 
condition #1 is to convey the importance of connectivity.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Roybal voting “no”. Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#5973)

 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Vortex International 
Aquatic Play Solutions, Carrolton, Texas, for the design and construc tion of a splash pad at the 
Depot Plaza ( General Fund and Visit Cheyenne fundraising donations)  – referred to Finance 
Committee.  Mr. White declared a conflict of interest and vacated council chambers .  Reporting 
for Finance Committee, Mr. Case advised that due to timing, the item was reviewed under other 
items for the agenda at the January 22, 2019 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, provisions of 
Title 2 of City Code had been met.   Mr. Case moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Ben 
Hornok, Project Manager, TC Solutions , presented splash pad literature (on file with Clerk’s 
office) and explained the main contract change is to extend the  completion date to November, 
noting a targeted July 4 th opening date.  Upon inquiry, Teresa Moore, Director of Community  
Recreation and Events, explained construction could impact Fridays on the Plaza and other plaza 
events including the horse and carriage staging area during the summer, noting fencing and signage 
may be necessary.  Michelle Vaughn, Vortex Aquatic Structures, explained the project completion 
date is dependent upon the planning and design process and construction start date , noting 
construction and completion of the project is anticipated to take approximately five to six months.    
Upon inquiry, Amy Allen, City Engineer, and Ms. Moore explained that due to unknown electrical 
power availability, it may not be possible to temporarily relocated some 2019 summer Depot Plaza 
events to the Civic Center Commons location, noti ng any proposed event use would need to be 
evaluated for power requirements. Additional comments included support of the proposed project. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna 
voting “no”.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. White returned to council chambers.  
(#6933)

 Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6918 between the City of Cheyenne and FCI 
Constructors of Wyoming, LLC, to add the Guaranteed Maximum Price for Phase I abatement and 
demolition of existing site for the Municipal Court Buildin g Project. (1% Specific Purpose Sales 
Tax).  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry,  Ben Hornok, Project 
Manager, TC Solutions, explained the guaranteed maximum price for the project has not yet been 
established and noted the proposed contract modification amount is the amount for abatement and 
demolition for the project.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works,  confirmed the contract 
modification price adds the Phase I cost to the original contract price of $21,485 .00 with FCI 
Constructors of Wyoming, LLC , noting t he Phase II  guaranteed maximum price won’t be 
determined until M arch 4, 2019 , and e xplained the original contract amount was for pre -
construction services.  Mr. Hornak explained the pre-construction services.  Upon inquiry,  Sylvia 
Hackl, City Attorney, explained the original contract has been approved by the governing body in 
which phases of the project will be presented for approval .  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6918)

Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects for Architectural and Engineering Services for finish of the 
second floor of the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax and

Corporation for partial release of retainage for the Civic Center Commons Park Project (1 % 
Specific Purpose Sales Tax, SLIB, FEMA and Parks funds) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-11-19 for furnishing one new 2018-2019 cab & chassis with 45-
foot aerial device body for the Forestry Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. 
 
 Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the February 21, 2019 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Winter Conference Business 
Meeting to be held in Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to delegate Dr. Rinne as Official 
Voting Delegate, and Mr. Shanor as Alternate Delegate, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Esquibel voting “no”. 
 
 Appointment of Allan Cunningham to the Board of Public Utilities.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments). 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 11, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in 
the City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether 
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for Lot 4, 
Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park Third Filing, and a Portion of adjacent Tundra Drive right-of-way, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek 
Parkway). 
  
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 11, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in 
the City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether 
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Lots 6 and 
7, Block 1, and all that 16’ alley located in Block 1, except east 8’ adjacent to Lots 3 and 4, Block 
1 Countryside Addition, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest intersection of 
Countryside Avenue and Whitney Road). 
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:04 p.m.  
 
Under other business: 
 
Donn Edmunds inquired about possible traffic resolutions at US 30 and Whitney Road and 
voiced concerns relating to construction of the intersections involving the Christensen Road 
project. Amy Allen,  City Engineer, advised  WYDOT and MPO a re working together on a 
masterplan to improve that intersection and reported the City is moving forward with relocating 
the sidewalk for pedestrian use.  Mr. Case encouraged attendance at the public meetings for the 
masterplan and advised discussions regarding project funding options are on-going. 
 
Richard Johnson spoke in support of the “opt-in” amendment that failed for the ordinance 
related to the Downtown Graffiti Abatement Pilot Program, spoke about electrical conduit 
locations at the Depot Plaza, provided information on the school zone at Taft and 13 th St., and 
voiced concerns regarding pedestrian walk-ways covered in ice near the Depot Plaza.  
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body,  the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:13. p.m.         
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-02-11 | CLIP_ID: 701 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 701

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 11, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne (arriving at 6:06 p.m.) , Scott Roybal and Dicky Shanor. Absent: Jeff White. Also present: 
Kris Jones, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 28, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated council chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as 
presented, seconded by Mr. Case. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to 
Chambers. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a  public hearing to determine whether compliance of  annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for Lot 4, Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail 
Park Third Filing, and a Portion of adjacent Tundra Drive right -of-way, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clea r Creek Parkway).  Tawn 
Hillenbrand, Planning and Development Department, advised the annexation request was 
submitted by the landowner, provided information on the property and reviewed requirements of 
and compliance with annexation conditions contained in  state statutes.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
   
Mayor Orr opened a  public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Lots 6 and 7, Block 1, and all that 
16’ alley located in Block 1, except east 8’ adjacent to Lots 3 and 4, Block 1 Countryside Addition, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest intersection of Countryside Avenue and 
Whitney Road).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, advised the applicant and 
City staff feel the annexation is not ready to proceed at this time, noting the ordinance has not been 
submitted to the City. Mr. Lloyd advised a second public hearing will be held on March 11, 2019, 
  
and noted it is anticipated the annexation will be considered at that time.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Creating Chapter 12.28, Downtown Graffiti Abatement 
Pilot Program, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and providing procedures for the operation thereof . Vicki Dugger, 
Executive Director, Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority,  spoke in support of the 
ordinance, addressed a concern related to distributing information to property owners and advised 
the DDA has hired help to obtain property owner information from the Secretary of State’s office 
which will be maintained in a database.  She provided ownership information on properties in the 
downtown district, stating 28% of owners are absentee. Ms. Dugger explained letters will be sent 
to owners giving  the option to “opt -out” of the program. Ms. Dugger advised, upon inquiry, 
program funds will be taken out of the mill levy budget line item.  Upon inquiry by Richard 
Johnson, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, advised property owners will not receive citations 
for graffiti.  Upon inquiry related to the course of action for those who “opt -out” and are affected 
by graffiti, Mayor Orr explained  Code Enforcement may become involved .  It was noted the 
program is a two -year pilot  program. Mr. Johnson discussed graffiti culture and suggestions 
provided by downtown property owners including public education and an area where graffiti is 
allowed.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dugg er explained initial letters were sent 
through registered mail.  Discussion followed and included: Proposed graffiti removal  process, 
methods utilized to communicate with property owners and the importance of an updated property 
ownership database. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present 
with the exception of Mr. Shanor voting “no”. (#4259) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Delegating the review authority authorized by W.S. §34-
12-103(b) to the Director of the City of Cheyenne Planning and Development Department.  Charles 
Bloom, Planning and Development Director, advised the County determined they will not  enter 
into an MOU at this time and explained the review process going forward noting the City’s ability 
to comment on County plats and the review process does apply to stormwater.  It was reported that 
County Commissioners are required to submit written notice if they disagree with City comments.  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion topics 
included: Concerns related to the ordinance and the way state statute is written, potential for future 
annexation reque sts that involve land that hasn’t met City standards,  possible impact of not 
approving the ordinance and future discussions with legislature on the topic. Amy Allen, City 
Engineer, clarified when annexations occur, City standards must be met.  Motion carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne and 
Mr. Roybal voting “no”. (#4260)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), by adding indoor entertainment as a land use category 
permitted by right for Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD, adding a new Section 5.11 to defer 
to the UDC standards where the PUD is silent on Development Standards, and, pursuant to Section 
  
2.2.1 of the UDC, amending The Village PUD map to reflect Lot 28 instead of Lots 30 and 31 of 
The Village Subdivision for the subject parcel, Cheyenne, Wyoming, (located west of and adjacent 
to Powderhouse Road, south of Old Town Lane). Eric Nelson, Principal Owner, Wyoming Downs, 
provided history of Wyoming Downs  business operations including community tax revenues, 
noted average age of customers,  explained the compan y’s interest in the  proposed location, 
provided information on calls responded to by Police Department at their existing location (report 
on file with Clerk’s office)  and stated their first priority is customer and employee safety .  Mr. 
Nelson discussed a future open house to be held at the proposed location to provide  public 
information and, upon inquiry, explained the difference between gambling and par i-mutuel 
waging.  He advised it is his understanding that other nearby and adjacent properties are correctly 
zoned for indoor entertainment . Joe Svec, #1 Properties, explained th e process of acquiring the 
property and reported beginning in 2018, signatures were collected for the  zone change petition 
from nearby property owners and provided information relating to petition requirements . Upon 
inquiry, Mr. Svec advised the former Jackson’s building has been vacant for 2 years with minimal 
interest in the building .  Eric Cook, Chief Operations Officer, Wyoming Downs, provided and 
reviewed handouts (on file with Clerk’s office ) discussing: Business customer base, proposed 
restaurant environment including alcohol and reported a recent decline in business at the existing 
location.  Upon request for c larification relating to a letter included in  the handout, Mr. Cook 
confirmed the letter  is undated  and stated  it was prepared on Novembe r 9, 201 8.  Mr. Cook 
provided additional information relating to desire to expand, details of the proposed building 
layout and operation, incident report involving Police Department calls for service at exiting 
location, projected tax revenues, and potential for ordinance postponement to allow for additional 
public input at a future open house.   Upon inquiry, Mr. Nelson explained  physical altercations 
occurred outside of the facility according to an internal control report.  Speaking in opposition of 
the ordinance were:  Mike Konar, Frank Cole, Evan Asher, Paul Wood, Scott Weaver,  Gloria 
Milmont, Laramie Ringolsby,  Maresa Hilton, Candis Pickard, CRMC Davis Hospice Center, 
Crystal Lewis, Trisha Smedley, Bryan Young,  Joe Fiedler, Holly Fiedler and  Kim and Conner 
Shumway.  Comments and concerns discussed included: Proposed location proximity to homes , 
churches, schools, daycares, hospice center, and other businesses, notification to nearby property 
owners required for zone change, incidents occurring in the existing location parking lot vs. inside 
the establishment,  importance of a safe neighborhood, potential long term financial impacts 
relating to current and future residents and businesses, Police Department recommended criteria, 
data provided by Wyoming Downs, client base, future alcohol service at the proposed location,  
zone change petition process and zoning criteria . Mr. Case advised there are six lots that are 
appropriately zoned for the business directly north and northwest of the proposed location.  Donn 
Edmunds spoke in favor of a secondary location with a six-month provision.  
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:20 p.m. Jeff Dierksen spoke 
about neighbor’s concerns and voiced support for discontinuation of problematic behavior.  Dr. 
Rinne reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Discussion topics included:  Neighborhood 
safety, community need for entertainment options, incidents at existing location isolated to the 
immediate area of the business , and potential positive  financial revenue impact. Mr. Case 
distributed and discussed a zoning map of the area (on file with Clerk’s office).  Mr. Case moved 
to postpone to February 25, 2019 , seconded by Mr. Luna.   Motion to postpone carried.   Voting 
  
“yes” – all members of the governing body present  with the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. 
Laybourn voting “no”. (City Clerk note: Dr. Rinne left the meeting due to a previous commitment 
at 8:33 p.m., prior to the vote). 
 
ORDINANCE – 1ST READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Lot 4,

 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Mineral Royalty  Capital 
Construction Account Grant Application to the State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the 
Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne for the purpose of funding the finish of the second floor 
of a new Municipal Courthouse .  Ron Downey, City Treasurer, provided information on the 
amount requested and  the grant application due date (2/21/2019) . Upon inquiry , Mr. Downey 
confirmed the proposed grant is to finish the second floor of the new Municipal Court Building 
and advised if the grant is awar ded and the second floor is not completed, half of the amount 
awarded would need to be  returned.  Joe Dougherty  inquired as to the specified government 
purpose of the proposed second floor space.  Mayor Orr advised the use hasn’t been determined to 
date and will be specified in the near future.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey explained project funding 
(general fund reserves  and 6th Penny fund overages), current general fund amount and 60 -day 
reserves requirement.   Reporting for Finance Commit tee, Mr. Luna reported there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. Shanor abstained from discussion and voting  (business affiliation with 
Wyoming State Loan and Investment Board) and vacated council chambers. Comment was made 
related to the affiliation of the resolution and the upcoming contract modification and the intended 
use for the space .  Mayor Orr advised for the purpose of the grant, specifics are not  currently 
  
needed for space.  Upon inquiry, Ben Hornok, TC Solutions, explained evolving space utilization 
of the second floor involving d iscussions with architects and governing body input on the use of 
the second floor.  Upon inquiry related to a possible postponement of the resolution, Mr. Downey 
advised a postponement would interfere with  the grant submittal deadline.  Mayor Orr explained 
her representation of the governing body before the State Loan and Investment Board and voiced 
concern about missing a  grant opportu nity.  Comments were made re lated to  the resolution ’s 
positive impact on the grant application and determining what will be located in the space.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present in Council Chambers.  
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Chambers. (#5976)

  Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6892 between the City of Cheyenne and Noel 
Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects for Architectural and Engineering Services for finish of the 
second floor of the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax and 
General Fund reserves) . Joe Dougherty commented on the elements of the  project and voiced 
concern regarding costs affiliated with the project .  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works,  
explained the amount of the modification is approximately $95,000.  Ben Hornok, TC Solutions, 
stated a work session for utilization of the second floor will be scheduled immediately, as this is a 
time sensitive aspect of the project.  Comment was made regarding determining priorities for  the 
space. Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Luna moved to postpone to 
February 25, 2019, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Mr. Laybourn advised the postponement is t o 
allow for further discussion related to space utilization.  Mr. Hornok advised upon inquiry, that a 
postponement could potentially delay the project. Jaimie Winters, Noel Griffith & Associates, 
provided a review of the project to date , explained the only component approved for the second 
floor is the  shell and provided suggestions for second floor use such as office spaces required, 
conference room space, etc.   Comments were made and included potential uses for  the second 
floor and opposition of the postponement. (Clerk’s note: Dr. Rinne returned to the meeting at 9:03 
p.m.)  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Cook.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.   Discussion 
continued relating to the following topic s:  Future City department occupancy  and possible 
  
renovations relating to proposed second floor of the new Municipal Court Building and the 
Municipal Building located at 2101 O’Neil Ave and status of general fund reserves.  Upon inquiry, 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, suggested language for amending Section II of the contract 
modification relating to second floor occupancy involving specific city departments.   Mr. 
Laybourn moved to amend Section II of the contract modification by striking the following: “The 
second floor will be designed to accommodate the following suites of offices: Mayor’s Office, 
City Attorney’s Office, and City Council” and replacing with: “The second floor will be designed 
to accommodate such o ffices as may be designated by Council.”, seconded by Mr. Roybal .  Mr. 
Hornok clarified  that temporary housing of City departments can occur if the second floor is 
constructed as general office space if future Municipal Building renovations are made.  Motion to 
amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Comment was made 
regarding the potential expenditure of additional funds on the project and the possibility of having 
just the shell for the second floor.  Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor 
voting “no”. (#6892) 
 
 [CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6839 between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corporation for partial release of retainage for the Civic Center Commons Park Project (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales Tax, SLIB, FEMA and Parks funds) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6839) 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 5 to Contract #5930 between the City of Cheyenne and Kone, Inc. for the 
addition of 2 Kone traction elevators located at the Timothy E. Wilson Board of Public Utilities’ 
Administration/Engineering Building, 2416 Snyder Avenue (BOPU funding) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract No. 6932 between the City of Cheyenne and Asbestos 
& Technical Services for asbestos abatement services for 2247 East 19 th Street to extend the 
completion date – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract No. 6743 between the City of Cheyenne and River 
Oaks Communications Corporation for drafting and negotiation of Charter Communications Cable 
Franchise Renewal and CenturyLink Cable Television Franchise Agreements to extend the 
completion date – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-13-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering 
Department, and Reiman Corp. for the Christensen Railroad Overpass & Corridor Project  (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase a mobile stage from Stageline Mobile Stage, Inc., Quebec, 
Canada, for the Community Recreation & Events Department – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase traffic paint from Vogel Paint and Wax Co., Orange City, Iowa, 
per WYDOT Bid #18-260CS (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
 Appointments of Lisa Kindel, Justine Whitacre and Jared Dixon to the Mayor’s Council 
for People with Disabilities.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (mayoral appointments).  Mayor Orr commented on the amount of applications submitted for 
the Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities.  
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 25, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in 
the City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal applications 
for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant,  limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, 
microbrewery, satellite distillery and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s 
office.   
 
Announcement was made that due to the President’s Day Holiday on Monday, February 
18, 2019, regula rly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance 
Committee will meet Tuesday, February 19, 2019 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee 
will meet Wednesday, February 20, 2019 at Noon.   
 
Under other business: 
 
Rod Stone  voiced concern with flooding issue s in the Summit Drive area relating to 
development of the Thomas Heights addition  and inquired on the status of previous discussions 
with City and County staff regarding initiating a review and analysis of the drainage plan from a 
third-party engineer. Upon inquiry, Mr. Stone advised he has been in contact with an engineer that 
has agreed to provide review and analysis services and confirmed the individual is not licensed in 
the State of Wyoming.   Mr. Laybourn voiced concerns relating to flooding in that area and  the 
need to resolve the draining issues. 
    
Jeff Dierksen distributed information related to a recent Police Department high visibility 
patrol on Pinto Lane (on file with Clerk’s office)  and upon inquiry, advised he hasn’t been in 
contact with the new City Traffic Engineer. Ms. Allen reported the Traffic Engineer has looked at 
some data concerning traffic in the area. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 10:03. p.m.         
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
         
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
  
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  February 23, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-02-25 | CLIP_ID: 703 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 703

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 25, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones , City Clerk, and 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda. (Clerk’s note: Council President Case assumed duties of Chair 
during a brief absence by Mayor Orr at 6:02 p.m.) Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to 
support received by Mr. Luna and Mr. Cook, Agenda Items #12 and 13 were removed from the 
Consent Agenda. Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook 
and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item #21 (b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved 
to approve remaining Consent Agenda items , seconded by Mr.  White. Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present in council chambers. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 11, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Esquibel moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. 
Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in council 
chambers. 
 
Council President Case opened a public hearing for renewal applications for existing City 
liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, 
satellite distillery and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s office.  Kris Jones, 
City Clerk, advised the resort liquor license renewal application has been withdrawn for JPK TR 
Cheyenne, LLC d/b/a Cheyenne Staybridge Suites due to non -compliance with State Statute 12 -
4-101(b)(2) as determined during a  recent Wyoming Liquor Division inspection , and was not 
included in those annual licenses involved with the public hearing.  She explained the application 
would also not be included in the renewal licenses that will be considered for approval at the next 
Finance Committee and City Council meetings .  There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Council President Case closed the hearing and referred 
the applications to Finance Committee. (Clerk’s note - Mayor Orr returned to council chambers 
following the public hearing at 6:06 p.m.) 
   
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, whic h established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), by adding indoor entertainment as a land use category 
permitted by right for Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD, adding a new Section 5.11 to defer 
to the UDC standards where the PUD is silent on Development Standards, and, pursuant to Section 
2.2.1 of the UDC, amending The Village PUD map to reflect Lot 28 instead of Lots 30 and 31 of 
The Village Subdivision for the subject parcel, Cheyenne, Wyoming, (located west of and adjacent 
to Powderhouse Ro ad, south of Old Town Lane) . Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr 
Rinne announced the applicant has requested to withdraw the application to re-zone.  Hearing no 
objections, Mayor Orr advised the item has been withdrawn.  
 
ORDINANCE – 2ND READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Lot 4, 
Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park Third Filing, and a portion of Tundra Drive, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek Parkway).  Dr. Rinne 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2ND READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County Swan Ranch PUD Planned 
Unit Development to City Swan Ranch PUD Planned Unit Development (Ordinance No. 3989) 
for Lot 4, Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park Third Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (at the 
southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek Parkwa y).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2ND READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to LI Light 
Industrial for Hutchins Corner 2nd Filing Lots 4 & 5, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of 
and adjacent to Campstool Road, west of Hutchins Drive).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Capital Construction loan application to the 
State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to 
fund the replacement of Fire Station #3 .  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Shanor  announced he would be abstaining from discu ssion 
and voting on the current and following items (business affiliation with Wyoming State Loan and 
Investment Board) and vacated council chambers. Upon inquiry, Mayor Orr explained funding for 
repayment of the loan has not yet been determined  and provid ed possible options for loan 
repayment.  Ron Downey, City Treasurer, provided loan interest rates available through the State 
Loan and Investment Board and explained  it is highly advisable to apply for a loan through this 
mechanism.  Concern was expressed for possible utilization of general fund reserves and future 6th 
Penny Sales Tax monies for this type of project.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey provided information 
  
on 6th Penny Sales Tax overage fund amounts.  Mayor Orr provided further information on the 
loan program stating during the 2017 legislative session, money was set aside for cities and towns 
to apply for loans for certain types projects and noted the loan application can go before the State 
Loan and Investment Board monthly .  She advised if funds are left unexpended, the money may 
not be available moving forward.   Motion failed.  Voting “yes” Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Orr, Mr. 
Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Dr. 
Rinne.   
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Capital Construction loan application to the 
State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to 
fund the replacement of Fire Station #5. Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
failed.  Voting “yes” Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne.   Following announcement of the vote, 
Mr. Shanor returned to council chambers. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Directing the availability of  the production of Tchaikovsky’s The 
Nutcracker Ballet at the Cheyenne Civic Center. Dr. Rinne and Mr. Esquibel declared conflicts of 
interest and vacated council chambers.  Barbara Sandick, Executive Director of Ballet Wyoming, 
explained their mission and spoke in favor of the resolution , explaining it will allow only local 
production companies to perform Tchaikovsky’s The Nutcracker Ballet during even numbered 
years. Ms. Sandick explained the struggle of competing with large national production companies 
and expressed the importance of supporting local arts groups. Stephanie Meisner-Maggard, Ballet 
Wyoming, Board of Directors, expressed appreciation for the resolution, provided information on 
Wyoming Ballet’s goals and their composition and voiced support for local arts groups.  Reporting 
for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case. David Soules, Events 
Coordinator, Community Recreation and Events,  provided information  upon inquiry related to 
attendance numbers for non -local productions of The Nutcracker  and advised he is unaware if 
notification of the resolution has been provided to Moscow Ballet .  Mr. Soules noted Moscow 
Ballet currently has a performance planned for 2019. Upon inquiry, Mr. Soules advised in his past 
work experience, a governing body had not interjected in facility renting policies. Ms. Sandick 
further elaborated on the difficulties involved with competing with non -local production 
companies, and that a non-compete clause was suggested with Moscow  Ballet but they couldn’t 
come to an agreement.   She advised Wyoming Ballet contracts with the  Cheyenne Symphony 
Orchestra for this production which costs a significant amount of money. Teresa Moore, Director 
of Community Recreation and Events, clarified Moscow Ballet has had 2019 dates on hold for 
some time.  Ms. Moore advised upon inquiry, customers have not been asked if they prefer local 
or national entertainment for events, but they can work on creating a survey.  Ms. Meisner provided 
information on a recent F orward Greater Cheyenne initiative that resulted in  2,800 responses of 
which indicated citizens would like to see local entertainment by Cheyenne Symphony Orchestra 
and Ballet Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case. Comments  were made 
relating to obtaining survey data and the importance of receiving input from Moscow Ballet.  Mr. 
Shanor moved to postpone to March 11, 2019, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Further comment was made 
on the intent of the resolution with respect to financial competition with larger scale entertainment 
entities performing similar shows.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” all members of the 
governing body present in council chambers with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White 
  
voting “no”.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Esquibel and Dr. Rinne returned to council 
chambers. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Directing all revenue from Contract No. 5902 to be earmarked to plan, 
build and maintain recreation facilities at the Belvoir Ranch – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the hiring of three (3) additional uniformed officers for the 
Cheyenne Police Department – referred o Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submittal of a request to the Wyoming State Loan and 
Investment Board to amend Drinking Water State Revolving Fund #133 from $3.0 million to $4.9 
million for the City of Cheyenne Hydroelectric Generation Facility Project and authorizing the 
Mayor, City Clerk and the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to sign all loan documents – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Calling for a drainage study to be conducted for and around the Thomas 
Heights Development – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Terracon Consultants 
for landfill leachate pump maintenance (Solid Waste fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(amount not to exceed $36,000.00). (#6955) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County 
Conservation Dis trict, Rotary Clubs of Cheyenne and Frog Creek Partners, LLC, Casper, 
Wyoming, to install and maintain gutter bins at identified locations in the Capitol Basin to trap 
waste in stormwater and reduce pollutants entering Crow Creek .  Motion (per Consent Agen da) 
carried. (#6956) 
 
 Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming Cattle Co., LLC, Pine 
Bluffs, Wyoming, for cattle grazing on the Belvoir Ranch .  Craig La Voy, Deputy Director of  
Public Works, advised five proposals were received and Wyoming Cattle Co., LLC was selected. 
He explained the contract is for three years  with an annual revenue of  $187,477.50.  Mr. LaVoy 
explained, upon inquiry, that historically the contract was for three years with occasional one year 
contract extensions.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6957) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Short Elliott 
Hendrickson Inc. for engineering design services for the Miller Lane and Sunset Drive 
Reconstruction Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount 
not to exceed $50,260.31). (#6958) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Short Elliott 
Hendrickson Inc. for engineering design services for the Airport Parkway Pavement Rehabilitation 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$46,463.60). (#6959) 
  
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AVI, P.C. for 
engineering design and construction management services for the Allison Road and Cribbon 
Avenue Street Improvements Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Amendment No. 5 to Contract #5930 between the City of Cheyenne and Kone, Inc. for the 
addition of 2 Kone traction elevators located at the Timothy E. Wilson Board of Public Utilities’ 
Administration/Engineering Building, 2416 Snyder Avenue (BOP U funding) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (amount of $6,872.16 annually).  (#5930) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract No. 6932 between the City of Cheyenne and Asbestos 
& Technical Services for asbestos abatement services for 2247 East 19 th Street to extend the 
completion date.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6932) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract No. 6743 between the City of Cheyenne and River 
Oaks Communications Corporation for drafting and negotiation of Charter Communications Cable 
Franchise Renewal and CenturyLink Cable Television Franchise Agreements to extend the 
completion date .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $15,000.00) .  
(#6743) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6951 between th e City of Cheyenne and Ramaker 
& Associates, Inc. for data conversion and software maintenance for the City’s Cemetery records 
(Information Technology Division Maintenance Funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 4 to Contract No. 3911 between the City of Cheyenne and Bresnan 
Communications, LLC, l/k/a/ Charter Communications to extend the term of the contract until 
May 31, 2019 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #S-13-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering 
Department, and Reiman Corp. for the Christensen Railroad Overpass & Corridor Project  (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales Tax) .  Sam Berta , Staff Engineer, Engineering Department,  explained 
Reiman Corp. was the lowest bidder, noting  HDR Engineering, Inc.  (HDR) supported 
recommendation for approval of the bid.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Berta advised the bid  doesn’t 
incorporate all project components and charges,  explained easements are in place with nearby 
landowners and stated HDR will be bringing forward a contract modification for bridge material 
testing. Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $10,557,155.01, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Comment was made regarding the methodology of the project.  Scott Larsen, Benchmark 
Engineers, advised, upon inquiry, Benchmark Engineers was not involved in the bid process which 
he believes resulted in incorrect answers to bid questions  and commented it is his belief that  his 
signature on previous documentation for the project is null and void.  Amy Allen, City Engineer, 
commented on the validity of drawings completed by an unauthorized party and suggested further 
review of the issue.  Comments were made relating to a similar project where the City became the 
project manager and the importance of competition of engineering services in the community . 
Mayor Orr raised a point of order stating discussion was not germane to the motion on the floor.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Larsen explained additional companies have not had involvement with the 
project to the best of his knowledge.  Ms. Allen provided additional information relating to the 
  
project’s bid process. Logan Sharpe, Assistant City Attorney, advised he is unaware of another 
scenario where a construction company participated in a bidding process  and stated it is his 
understanding it didn’t happen in this case.  Mr. Larsen explained bid information was adjusted 
based on information received by Reiman  Corp. and others.  Comments were made regarding : 
Potential conflict of interest, cost of the project and Reiman Corp.’s involvement as a subcontractor 
in the bid process .  Upon inquiry,  Mr. Larsen  advised Benchmark Engineers would not be 
refunding any payment received by the City relating to previous project services. Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn 
and Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#6960) 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase a mobile stage from Stageline Mobile Stage, Inc., Quebec, 
Canada, for the Community R ecreation & Events Department. Jason Sanchez, Assistant, Deputy 
Director, Community Recreation and Events, requested consideration for approval, explained due 
to the upcoming construction of the recently approved Depot Plaza Splash Pad the City will need 
to change the location of events that require a stage, advised the mobile stage could be utilized for 
other departments and upon inquiry, reported could possibly generate revenue through rentals and 
advertising.  Mr. Sanchez explained upon further inquiry, the maintenance building area at the 
Depot Plaza can accommodate power for a stage but isn’t intended to be used as one.  Mayor Orr 
and Mr. Downey provided information on 6 th Penny Sales tax overage funds, which could 
potentially be utilized to purchase the stage. Mr. Sanchez explained, upon inquiry, the mobile stage 
should be able to be used for 15 -20 years  and confirmed the purchase of the stage was not 
previously budgeted for in the Community Recreations and Events budget.   Mr. White moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $185,541.00, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Comments were made 
regarding: Capital needs, utilizing 6th Penny Sales Tax overage funds and other potential funding 
options.  Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, explained, upon inquiry, an amendment could be made to 
identify a funding source, or the purchase consideration could be approved with a future resolution 
identifying the revenue source.  Mr. Laybourn moved to amend by utilizing 6th Penny Sales Tax 
overage funds as the funding so urce, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Com ments continued and 
included: Lack of identified funding prior to purchase request, request for Community Recreation 
and Events to determine a funding source,  desire for additional entertainment options , potential 
for revenue through rentals and advertising and appropriateness of funding options.  Mr. Downey 
explained funds could re-appropriated from various accounts.  Mayor Orr explained the possibility 
of a future process for expending 6 th Penny Sales Tax overage fund s.  Motion to amend failed.   
Voting “no” - all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “yes”.  
Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.  
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase traffic paint from Vogel Paint and Wax Co., Orange City, Iowa, 
per WYDOT Bid #18-260CS (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-13-19 for the Surplus Sale of “United Rotary Brus h, United Pro 
Strip Brooms”, for the Street & Alley Division (Revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 11, 2019 at 6:00  p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyomi ng, to determine whether 
  
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for or all of 
Lots 6 and 7, Block 1, and all that 16' alley located in Block 1, Countryside Addition,  Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located southwest of th e intersection of Countryside Avenue and Whitney 
Road). 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 11, 2019 at 6:00  p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether 
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for all of FW 
Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection  of College Drive 
and Southwest Drive). 
 
Under other business: 
 
Mr. Cook noted that at the February 11, 2018 City Council meeting, the motion to approve 
Contract Modification No. 2 to City Contract No. 6892 between the City of Cheyenne and Noel 
Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects for architectural and engineering services for finish of the 
second floor of the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project passed.  Mr. Cook, advising he had voted 
on the prevailing side, and pursuant to City Code Chapter 2.04, moved to reconsider the vote taken 
on the main motion, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comments were made on the process involving a 
motion to reconsider to allow for public input. Kris Jones, City Clerk, explained the item can be 
voted on or postponed allowing future public input.  Motion to reconsider failed (tie vote).  Voting 
“yes” - Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” - Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White. 
 
Mr. Case advised he has received citizen complaints regarding debris from the chip sealing 
and restriping programs.  Sam Berta, Staff Engineer, explained he will look in to remedying the 
issue.  
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:54 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
