MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-03-11 | CLIP_ID: 704 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 704

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 11, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones , City Clerk, and 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda. Mr. Luna moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda items , 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Shanor who abstained. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 22, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 25, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 27, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 28, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
Mayor Orr  opened a Public Hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for or all of Lots 6 and 7, Block 1, and 
all that 16' alley located in Block 1, Countryside Addition,  Laramie County, Wyoming (located 
southwest of the intersection of Countryside Avenue and Whitney Road).  Seth Lloyd, Planning 
and Development Department, advised the annexation request was submitted by the landowner,  
provided information on the property  and reviewed requirements of and compliance with 
annexation conditions contained in state statutes and advised this is the second public hearing due 
to the project being postponed.  Mr. Lloyd advised a genda item #14 is the affiliated annexation 
ordinance.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
  
 
Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for all of FW Subdivision, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of College Drive and Southwest Drive) .  
Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, advised the annexation request was submitted 
by the landowner,  provided information on the property  and reviewed requirements of and 
compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes  advised the annexation request 
was submitted by the landowner,  and a dvised agenda item #17 is the affiliated annexation 
ordinance.  Dr. Kay Sheehan, adjacent property owner, advised she received a letter regarding the 
proposed annexation, stating meeting information was not included and voiced concerns relating 
to required development signage. D r. Sheehan read a letter into the record advising it is her 
understanding county staff was unaware of the proposed annexation and provided information on 
how the annexation would affect her land and adjacent property owners.  She expressed concerns 
regarding: City improvements that would have to be reimbursed if her property is sold, potential 
for increased traffic and crime if additional businesses were to open in the area and requested 
consideration to postpone or deny the proposed annexation.  Glen Connor spoke in opposition of 
the proposed annexation and voiced concerns relating to development improvements and required 
reimbursements and noted previous estima te amount for improvements . Mr. Connor requested 
confirmation in writing that he and other area property owners w ouldn’t be responsible for 
reimbursement of developer fees. Mayor Orr advised reimbursement requirement s will be 
researched.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lloyd advised the Planning Department followed state statu te 
notification requirements for annexation petitions reporting notification was sent to nearby 
property owners within 300 ft.  It was noted  UDC development signage requirements w ere 
followed, but weather conditions may have contributed to signs blowing down. D evelopers must 
make a good faith effort to ensure the notices remain posted.  There being no further comments or 
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing.

 RESOLUTION – Directing the availability of the production of Tchaikovsky’s The 
Nutcracker Ballet at the Cheyenne Civic Center  (Postponed from February 25, 2019).  Dr. Rinne 
  
and Mr. Esquibel declared conflicts of interest and vacated council chambers. Stephanie Meisner-
Maggard, Ballet Wyoming, Board of Directors, expressed support for the resolution and articulated 
this will allow the ballet to conduct the production with local organizations.   She explained she 
distributed survey information obtained from a Forward Greater Cheyenne  initiative she spoke 
about at the last council meeting .  Barbara Sandick, Executive Director of  Ballet Wyoming, 
advised there are many professional dancers locally and provided information on professional 
training companies that local dancers have attended.  She explained the concept of a “routed tour” 
which involves many performances along a route in a certain timeframe and provided information 
relating to a radius clause.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Sandick advised  Ballet Wyoming  has funding 
sources necessary to conduct The Nutcracker production every other year.  Julie  and Gracie Fogg 
provided information about Ballet Wyoming and local dancers and spoke in favor of the resolution.   
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Shanor 
moved to amend by adding at the end of the “Be it further resolved ” clause: “This resolution 
sunsets January 1, 2023”, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comment was made relating to community spirit 
and the governing body taking an arbitrary approach.  Ms. Sandick spoke in support of the 
amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
in council chambers. Jason Sanch ez, Deputy Director of Community Recreation and Events, 
explained upon inquiry , contact was made with Moscow Ballet .  It was noted there are many  
professional traveling production companies.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present in council chambers.  Following announcement of the 
vote, Mr. Esquibel and Dr. Rinne returned to council chambers. (#5979) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Directing all revenue from Contract No. 5902 to be earmarked to plan, 
build and maintain recreation facilities at the Belvoir Ranch .  Todd Thibodeou, Belvoir Master 
Plan planning committee, explained wind generation will have a significant impact to the property.  
Mr. Thibodeou voiced support for wind energy revenue going towards recreational improvements.  
Speaking in support of recreational development on the property were: Barb Gorg es, Cheyenne 
High-Plains Audubon Society, Peter Skram, Wyoming Pathways, Cheyenne Mountain Bike Club 
and Overland Mountain Bike Association, Jean Sh iverdecker, Gr anite Cano n, Caroline Esch, 
Cheyenne Mountain Bike Club and  highschool Mountain Bike Team coach and  Jodee Pring , 
highschool Mountain Bike Team coach.  Topics discussed included: Opposition of utilizing so 
much of the land for wind turbines, maintaining recreational value of property and mountain biking 
trails, racing and riding opportunities on the property .  Brad Brooks, Director of BOPU, spoke 
about water studies on the property, City and Board contributions for purchasing costs, and noted 
any BOPU revenue from the wind energy project w ill go towards water development expansion. 
Mr. Brooks explained funding options relating to water development projects, stating the Board 
will apply for state loan if they don’t receive enough project revenue and explained sewer loan 
limitations. Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, referenced a memo distributed previously 
regarding F inance Committee comments stating the solid waste fund  could utilize additional 
revenue to supplement maintenance on the ranch.  Ms. Nemecek provided landfill design costs 
and explained development was halted due to cost estimates of the project. Comments were made 
regarding possible future uses for the Belvoir Ranch and ranch management during windfarm 
development. Mr. Roybal advised a financial plan must be submitted for the project and there is a 
10-year sunset date on the resolution. Teresa Moore, Community Recreation and Events Director; 
advised public access contributed to delayed recreation al development on the  property.  She  
  
discussed maintenance and affiliated costs for the property.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated March 8, 2019, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Ms. Nemecek confirmed wind farm revenue amounts and provided cost inf ormation 
associated with the landfill expansion.  Comment was made regarding concern for not fully 
utilizing the land for recreational us e and how long maintenance will  be needed for the wind 
turbines.  Mayor Orr explained turbines are eventually removed . Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek 
explained the need for cattle grazing on the property and how cattle are fenced in areas throughout 
the grazing period.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Dr. Rinne a nd Mr. Luna  voting “no”. Comments were made on the 
postponement.  Alessandra McCoy Fakelman , Assistant City Attorney,  explained proposed 
revenue distribution amounts are undetermined and suggested addressing the matter in the annual 
city budget ordinance.  It was noted the master plan for the Belvoir Ranch is available for review.  
Comment was made related to wind farm  revenue utilization for recreation in other areas of the 
city.   Mr. Case moved to postpone to March 25, 2019, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to postpone 
carried. Voting “yes” all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. White  voting 
“no”. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the hiring of three (3) additional uniformed officers for the 
Cheyenne Police Department.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5980) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submittal of a request to the Wyoming State Loan and 
Investment Board to amend Drinking Water State Revolving Fund #133 from $3.0 million to $4.9 
million for the City of Cheyenne Hydroelectric Gen eration Facility Project and authorizing the 
Mayor, City Clerk and the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to sign all loan documents .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5981) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Calling for a drainage study to be conducted for and around the Thomas 
Heights Development. Speaking in support of the resolution were: Rod Stone, Larry Bechtoldt and 
Bill Spillman.  Topic discussed included: C oncern with flooding related to the development  of 
Thomas Heights, current stormwater drainage language in the UDC, significant flooding events in 
the area caus ing considerable damage, water drainage in Sunrise Heights  and money spent by 
property owners on water mitigation. Mr. White moved to a dopt, se conded by Mr . Laybourn .  
Discussion topics included: mitigating flooding issues, subdivision  development contributing to  
flooding neighboring areas,  benefit of hiring a 3 rd party consultant for a drainage review, storm 
water utility charges, funding mechan ism to pay for  a drainage study, preventative measures for 
future developments, stormwater drainage maps and 2009 groundwater drainage plans. Mr. Case 
moved to amend by adding a new line 1 to read: “Direct the City Engineer to develop a detailed 
scope for the project, and to put the project out to bid pursuant to the procedures set forth in Part 
III of the City of Cheyenne’s Purchasing Policy and Procedures Manual, Professional Services – 
Architects, Engineers and Surveyors”, renumber section 1 to 2 , strike the words “an honest” and 
replace with “ a thorough” in section 2, renumber section 2 to 3 and insert “this Governing Body” 
after the word “residents” in section 3, seconded by Mr. Layb ourn.  Amy Allen, City Engineer, 
suggested a SOQ (consultant services – Statement of Qualifications) or bid process for the study.  
Mr. Stone advised he  has attempted  to resolve the issue with Guardian Homebuilders. Donn 
Edmunds spoke about a floodwater si tuation he had been involved with in a different area of the 
  
city. Ms. Allen and Sylvia Hack, City Attorney provided further information on the SOQ, RFP and 
bid processes.  Motion to amend failed. Voting “no”  - all members of the governing body.   
Discussion continued and included: R esolution language, city and county involvement, 
establishing responsibility for resolution of  the drainage issue, funding source for potential 
drainage evaluation by a third -party and concern with prior analysis’. Upon inquiry, Ms. Allen 
explained drainage pond requirements in the area, drainage engineer review of original plans and 
the need for review of City code regarding flooding. Chris Lovas, AVI Engineering, P.C., provided 
information on their analysis and the 3-pond plan for the area stating the plan included expansion 
of an existing drainage pond and  infrastructure installment in the Granite Hills Subdivision .  
Further discussion continued on information contained in a report from Benchmark  Engineers.   
Motion failed. Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White. 
Voting “no” - Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Naming the Cheyenne Botanic Gardens Grand Conservatory the Shane 
Smith Grand Conservatory – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr. 
Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White.

 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AVI, P.C. for 
engineering design and construction management services for the Allison Road and Cribbon  
Avenue Street Improvements Project (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax). Wes ley Bay, Deputy City 
Engineer, provided a staff report for the project.  School Resource Officer Becky Juschka, Johnson 
Junior High, reported traffic is a concern in the area of the school due multiple factors including – 
confusing traffic pattern, busses causing lack of visibility and crosswalk placement.  Coley Maurer, 
spoke in support of the proposed improvement project and stated her daughter was recently injured 
by a vehicle in the intersection. Jeff Dierksen spoke in support of the agreement.  Mr. White moved 
to approve in an amount not to exceed $69,500.00, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry, Anissa 
Gerard, Traffic Engineer, explained in her experience the practice has been  to not to reduce the 
speed limit in junior and senior high school zones. She believes a plan to revise the traffic flow in 
the intersection will naturally reduce speeds and advised temporary measures will be implemented 
until construction starts on June 4 th.  Ms. Gerard advised signs will be installed with median and 
  
flashing lights.  Comments were made regarding the importance of crosswalks and crosswalk 
safety. Ms. Gerard explained she meets with the school district monthly to discuss safety.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6963)

 Appointment of Robin L. Lockman as City Treasurer for the City of Cheyenne. Mr. Luna 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments).  Mayor 
Orr expressed appreciation to Ms. Lockman. 
 
 Appointment of Kimberly Skoutary-Johnson to the Laramie County Community Juvenile 
Services Joint Powers Board and Kevin Yarbrough to the Mayor’s Council  for People with 
Disabilities.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral 
appointments). 
 
Under other business: 
 
 Donn Edmunds commented on the plans for U .S. Highway 30 and Christensen Road 
project. 
 
 Jeff Dierksen voiced concern relating to failure of the resolution related to a drainage study 
in Thomas Heights and requested consideration to continue working on a possible solution.  Mayor 
Orr advised further discussions can be held related to the concern.   
 
 Amy Allen, City Engineer, reported on information provided to members of the governing 
body related to the 26th Street Interceptor Project as previously requested, and advised the RFP for 
a consultant for the project is moving forward.  Mayor Orr provided information on presentation 
of the project to the State Loan and Investment Board, noting the grant is awarded as 
reimbursement.  Support was expressed for the project. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:12 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-03-25 | CLIP_ID: 705 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 705

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 25, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item #16 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon 
request by Mr. Case, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor, Agenda Item 
#24 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Case moved to approve remaining Consent 
Agenda items , seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 11, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meetings of the Governing Body on March 12, March 14, March 16 
and March 19, 2019.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Case moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of Lots 
6 and 7, Block 1, and all that 16’ alley located in Block 1, Countryside Addition, Laramie, County, 
Wyoming, (located southwest of the intersection of Countryside Avenue and Whitney Road) .  
Reporting f or Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning District Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential 
to CB Community Business for all of Lots 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, and 8, Block 1 and all  of Lots 2, 3, and 4, 
Block 2, Countryside Addition, Laramie County Wyoming, (located south of Countryside Avenue, 
west of Whitney Road).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of FW 
Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming ( located northeast of the intersection of College Drive 
and Southwest Drive) .  Dr. Kay Sheehan  commented on annexation requirements and spoke in 
opposition stating the annexation does not benefit  nearby property owners ’ health, safety and 
welfare due to increased traffic and potential decrease of property value.  Glenn Connor thanked 
Planning and Development staff for clarifying developer reimbursement  fees and stated concern 
with flooding in the annexation area .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry, Gene McDonald, agent for the applicant, provided information on 
an existing retention pond that will be doubled in size and explained it is proposed the land will be 
replatted into seven lots with one designated for drainage.  He advised the land won’t be developed 
for several years, but it must be annexed first which will include Broken Arrow Rd.  Mr. McDonald 
further explained there will be 50 ft. of public right -of-way.  Mr. McDonald stated annexation of 
all land  is proposed , not just the land  that Arby’s occupies. Comments were made r egarding 
drainage concerns in the area.  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development  Department, provided 
information on county land sub-division requirements upon inquiry, and advised it is not 
recommended due to the land being located partially in the city and county which can be 
problematic.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Case voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning District Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County CB Community Business to 
CB Community Business for all of FW Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast 
of the intersection of College Drive and Southwest Drive).  Dr. Rinne announced the applicant has 
requested the zone change application be withdrawn.  Hearing no objections, Mayor Orr advised 
the item has been withdrawn. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning District Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Unified Development Code, amending the 
zoning classification for a portion of the southeast quarter of Section 11 and the northeast quarter 
of  Section 14 in Township 13 North, Range 67 West from the Overland Trails Planned Unit 
Development (PUD) to the CFD Park -N-Ride PUD for the 27.69-acre Lot 1 of the CFD Park -N-
Ride Subdivision in the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of Southwest 
Drive, north of College Drive, and adjacent to I-25).  Glenn Connor expressed interest in assistance 
with the project as a nearby property owner .  Donn Edmunds commented on road conditions in 
the area and expressed concern related to the proposed Park-N-Ride location. Brad Emmons, AVI, 
agent for applicant, provided information on the Park -N-Ride operational scenario during CFD  
stating volunteers will be at  the south entrance where all  traffic will be funneled  and advised a 
temporary traffic signal will be located at Southwest Drive and College.  Comments were made 
regarding traffic barrels and placement during the event. Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated March 19, 2019, 
seconded by Mr. Case. Dr. Rinne explained the substitute provides clerical revisions, noting no 
substantial changes were made.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes ” – all members of the 
  
governing body.  Further comments included road improvement suggestions in the area and land 
being donated for the project by adjacent land owners.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 5.48, Detective, Patrol and Guard 
Service Businesses, 5.52, Massage Establishments, and 5.72, Public Transportation, within Title 
5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided information on the amendment stating  although 
finger printing services will no longer be administered to business license applicants , local 
background and national warrants checks will still be conducted.  He stated there has not been any 
license application denials based on fingerprint ing results alone in the last several years . Chief 
Kozak explained individual detective guards will no longer be  required to obtain a license.  This 
process change will streamline the application process allowing applicants to start employment 
sooner. Donn Edmunds voiced opposition and provided background information on the Detective, 
Patrol and Guard business and individual application process.   Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 10.56, Handicapped Parking Spaces, 
within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic,  of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Directing all revenue from Contract No. 5902 to be earmarked to plan, 
build and maintain recreation facilities at the Belvoir Ranch.  Caroline Esch, Cheyenne Mountain 
Bike Club and high  school Mountain Bike Team coach , commented on utilization of revenue 
funds, lease language that states lessee and owner should cooperate on how the land will be used  
and committees that determined appropriate placement of the turbines.  Cari Cuffney commented 
on utilization of revenue and City Department reimbursements. Reporting for Finance Committee, 
Mr. White advised there is no recommendation on the item. Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Roybal explained the last postponement didn’t allow for a work session and 
requested an additional postponement. Discussion topics included: interest in postponing the item, 
difficulty in scheduling a work session prior to the next Finance Committee meeting, desire for 
multiple work sessions, funding analysis to determine utilization of revenue, placement of turbines, 
and conservation easement.  Mayor Orr stated NextEra Energy agreed to removing turbine #8 but 
are hesitant to remove others as it would put them below production capacity for the project.  
Discussion continued relating to updating the masterplan and trails on the land including potential 
amenities and additional work sessions.  Mayor Orr advised upcoming budget planning sessions 
can provide for additional discussions regarding contract revenue.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone 
to April 8, 2019 , seconded by Mr. Cook.   Motion to postpone failed .  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” - Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, 
Mayor Orr,  Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor .  Discussion continued on bringing the resolution  in a 
revised form back before the governing body  after budget discussions and funding analys es are 
complete.  Main motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn and M r. 
Roybal.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White. 
 
  
 RESOLUTION – Naming the Cheyenne Botanic Gardens Grand Conservatory the Shane 
Smith Grand Conservatory.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White. Members of the 
Governing Body expressed their appreciation to Mr. Smith and his contributions to the community 
and to the Botanic Gardens program .  Concern was expressed that the City does not have any 
criteria in place for naming buildings after individuals.  Mayor Orr commented on the importance 
of these types of resolutions. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5982) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Providing for the budgeting of excess 1% Specific Purpose Sales and 
Use Taxes collected for the August 21, 2012 voter-approved specific purposes.  Mr. White reported 
the motion to adopt failed at the Finance Committee, therefore, there is no recommendation on the 
item. Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case. Mr. Shanor stated the primary purpose of 
the resolution is to provide transparency for the approximately $3.2 million in remaining overage 
funding from the 2012 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax .  He explained the resolution 
determines the funding will be used for projects that were on the ballot  and further advised the 
governing body would  determine how to allocate the excess funding during the budget process.  
Comments were made on the importance of the integrity of the election and utilizing excess 
funding for projects the funding was intended for.  Mr. Shanor noted the resolution sets for th 
requirements of fund distribution for maintenance and operations for related 2012 voter approved 
projects.  Discussion topics included: statutory requirements relating to use of specific purpose tax 
funding, recent legislation amending the statute on spending requirements and limitations relating 
to voter approved sales tax funding , concerns regarding proposed resolution language being  
restrictive, general support for the resolution, previous 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax 
project funding overages, operations and maintenance costs  and the 17th Street Lighting project.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes”  - all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. 
Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, and Mr. Roybal voting “no”.  (#5983) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the Wyoming Retirement System Deferred Compensation Plan 
for the benefit of full -time employees of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and allowing for 
automatic enrollment of new full-time employees – referred to Finance committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Case)

 Preliminary Plat for US 30 Business Plaza, Second Filing, a replat of Lots 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 
and 8, Block 1 and all of Lots 2, 3, and 4, Block 2, and all of Tim Tam Street, and all of that 16’ 
alley located in Block 1, Countryside Addition, together with Lots 7, 8, a nd 9, Block 1, US 30 
Business Plaza, Cheyenne, Laramie County Wyoming, (located south of  and adjacent to 
Countryside Avenue , west of Whitney Road) .  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development  
Department, reported the applicant provided information on an option of fulfilling condition #1 at 
the last Public Services Committee  meeting.  Mr. Lloyd expressed concern for proposed staff 
condition language as provisions  aren’t included for  utilities and requested consideration to 
approve the original proposed staff condition #1.  Anissa Gerard, City Traffic Engineer, reported 
engineering staff supports original verbiage for condition #1, supports reserving land as right-of-
way near Tim Tam street, and advised the revised language  amendment doesn’t allow authority 
for access on property to the south as it is redeveloped in the future.  John Sa yers, agent for the 
owner, requested vacation of Tim Tam Street noting if the original staff condition #1 is approved, 
future land development may be difficult. Dr. Rinne moved to approve with staff conditions #1 & 
2, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend staff condition #1 to read  “Should 
necessary access to the unplatted land south of US 30 Business Plaza Second Filing need 
ingress/egress access through Lot 1, Block 1, the property owners will work together to determine 
the location and prepare a Memorandum of Understanding to include easement and maintenance”, 
seconded Mr. Roybal.  Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes”  - all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes” 
- all members of the governing body. 
 
 Preliminary PUD for Harmony Valley, a portion of the S½ of Section 7 and the SE¼NE¼ 
of Section 7, T.13N., R.66W., 6 th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of 
and adjacent to W. College Drive, west of Walterscheid Boulevard) .   Kelly Hafner, CivilWorx, 
LLC, agent for owner, provided information on the  project including topography, involvement 
with the City and Plan Cheyenne and public input received from the public and the governing 
body. Mr. Hafner explained many zoning aspects are e ncompassed in the project with the 
preliminary PUD which allows flexibility for parks, open space and trails planning. He voiced 
concerns relating to  staff and Planning Commission  conditions F-Greenway width, G-attached 
sidewalks, and I-street tree planting exceptions.   Dillon Ohrt, representing residents of Harmony 
Hills, read a letter into the record related to concerns with certain elements of the development  
including the following:  impact contribut ing to potential diminished property value, drainage 
issues, proposed greenway location, increased traffic and population in the area , plans for 
intersections and population influxes in area schools.  James O’Brien voiced concerns relating to 
drainage, parking and obstruction of current  views. Mr. O’Brien  requested project development 
height restrictions and provided pictures of recent flooding in the area.  Mr. Hafner acknowledged 
drainage issue concerns  and advised they are looking into project elements to address draining.  
Information was provided on traffic and drainage studies, future placement of traffic signals and 
Laramie County School District #1 determin ing the need for additional schools.   Mr. O hrt 
requested consideration in preserving  existing view sheds . Dr. Rinne reported there was no 
  
recommendation on the item  from the Public Services Committ ee. Dr. Rinne  moved to 
acknowledge the Planning Commission’s recommendation to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal. 
Discussion included: conditions F-Greenway width, G-attached sidewalks, and I-street tree 
planting exceptions and tree and greenway requirements in the area .  Mr. Lloyd reported staff 
recommends approval with conditions A-J and suggested an amendment to condition A. Dr. Rinne 
moved to amend to include conditions A-J, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
condition A by amending Section 1.K.2 to satisfaction of the Engineering Department by deleting 
the text in quotations, seconded by Mr. Shanor. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the 
governing body .  Motion to amend as amended carried. Voting “yes”  - all members of the 
governing body.   Main motion  as twice amended carried. Voting “ye s” - all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between  the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for the 
housing of juvenile detainees in the Laramie County Juvenile Services Center located at the Archer 
Complex.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6967) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie Cou nty Sheriff’s Department and 
Laramie County for housing City Prisoners at the Laramie County Detention Center.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6968) 
 
 Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Country Club for 
continued use of the premises located on Stinner Road for a period of 100 years .  Mayor Orr, Dr. 
Rinne and Mr. Cook recused themselves from discussion and voting  (Cheyenne Country Club 
membership affiliation) and vacated council chambers. Mayor Orr relinquished duties of the chair 
to President  Case.  Bill Hibler,  attorney representing  Cheyenne Country Club, provided 
information on the  lease history noting there have been  many amendments over the years.   He 
advised he collaborated  with the City A ttorney’s office to revise the contract  and they envision 
long term  capital improvements for the community.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Hibler provided 
information on club memberships and facility utilization options for members and non -members.  
Tawn Tempel, Cheyenne Country Club Board, provided information on taxes and utilities paid  
and events hosted at the facility , noting funding has been contributed to a walking path that is 
utilized by WYDOT employees.  Brian Pedersen expanded on events hosted by the club .  Mr. 
White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of 
the governing body.  Following announcement of the vote, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Cook 
returned to council chambers. (#6969) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Goat Green, LLC, Wellington, Colorado, for 
vegetation management along Dry Creek and Crow Creek (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
 Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 6660 between the City of Cheyenne and WYCO 
Recycling LLC for recycling services to increase the tipping fee for recyclables, monthly minimum 
payment, and annu al not -to-exceed cost (Solid Waste fund).   Vicki Nemecek,  Public Works 
Director, advised the modification is in response to the current local , national and international 
recycling crisis .  She provided information involving recycling co ntamination rates, WYCO’s 
operational status and the City’s desire to continue to provide recycling services to the community. 
  
Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek provided information on the potential for additional limitations being 
placed on accepted  recyclable items, marketable recyclable items and explained the use of  an 
incinerator would not be an economical option.  Brian Heuer, WYCO Recycling LLC, provided 
recycling information regarding single stream recycling products and explained upon inquiry, that 
glass isn’t accepted due to cost restraints.  Mr. White moved to approve , seconded by Mr. Case.   
Ms. Nemecek explained a proposed 5% increase to the sanitation fee schedule which will cover 
increased WYCO fees.    Comments were made  related to public awareness regarding recycling 
requirements and recycling contamination in the community.  Ms. Nemecek provided the contract 
expiration date upon inquiry.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#6660) 
 
 Contract Modification #1 to Contract  #6785 between the City of Cheyenne and HDR 
Engineering Inc. for additional construction management services for the Christensen Road 
Overpass Project (6th Penny Sales Tax).  Wes Bay, Deputy City Engineer, requested the contract 
modification not be approved to allow for revisions to the construction management portion of the 
contract.  Mr. White advised the motion to approve failed at the Finance Committee and there is 
no recommendation on the item.  The item died due to lack of a motion. 
 
[CA] Amendment to Contract #6905 between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Laramie County School District No. 1 for school resource officers for the 2018-
2019 school year and summer school 2019.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6905) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -14-19 for furnishing one new ¾ ton crew cab full size pick -up 
truck (command vehicle), one new extended cab mid-sized pick-up truck (support truck), and two

Department (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried ( accept bid

[CA] Consideration of Bid # S-10-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Kaiser

Carpet Replacement Project (Capitol Imp rovement Plan (CIP) funding) .  Motion (per Consent

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-16-19 for the surplus sale of traffic controllers and traffic control

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-12-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, and Reiman 
Corp., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Evers Boulevard Reconstruction Project (2015-2018 1% Sales 
Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-14-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, and American 
Contracting of Wyoming LLC, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Jr. Leag ue Dugout Roof 
Replacements and Masonry Repairs Project (Reserves carryover) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Appointments Robert Korber to the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint 
Powers Board, Scott Homar to the Fire Civil Service Commission, James Johnson to the Building 
Code Board of Appeals and Lee Filer to the Personnel Commission. Mr. Roybal moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments).   
 
Under other business: 
 
 Mr. White expressed appreciation to first responders and snow removal crews who 
provided assistance during the recent  snow storm and crews who are working to repair potholes 
due to the storm.  Mayor Orr commented on a recognition ceremony held in honor of a citizen who 
provided assistance during the storm.   
 
Mr. Laybourn complemented parties involved in the  recent Collective Labor Bargaining 
process.   
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:44 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-04-08 | CLIP_ID: 706 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 706

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 8, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal and Jeff White. Absent: Dicky Shanor.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr.  Case, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna, Agenda Item #16 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Mr. Case, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna, Agenda Item #22  (a) 
was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve  remaining Consent 
Agenda items , seconded by Mr . Luna .  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on March 20, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 25, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of Lots 
6 and 7, Block 1, and all that 16’ alley located in Block 1, Countryside Addition, Laramie, County, 
Wyoming, (located southwest of the intersection of Countryside A venue and Whitney Road) .  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Roybal. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present. (#4264) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning District Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential 
to CB Community Business for all of Lots 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, and 8, Block 1 and all of Lots 2, 3, and 4, 
Block 2, Countryside Addition, Laramie County Wyoming, (located south of Countryside Avenue, 
  
west of Whitney Road).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4265) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of FW 
Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming ( located northeast of the intersection of College Drive 
and Southwest Drive).  Dr. Kay Sheehan requested postponement of the annexation due to water 
retention concerns in the area  and expressed concern related to flooding and maintenance of  
Broken Arrow Rd.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, provided information regarding gravel 
road maintenance in the annexation area.  Glenn Connor expressed concerns regarding flooding in 
his neighborhood which is within a mile of the requested annexation area.  He noted there has been 
an increase in flooding over the years  and expressed the importance of addressing the concern.   
Mr. Connor explained , upon inquiry , Lindbloom Ct. drainage hasn’t been addressed.   Upon 
inquiry, Dr. Sheehan stated flooding occurs on Broken Arrow Rd. and the  road doesn’t seem to 
receive as much maintenance as it did previously.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Gene McDonald, agent for the applicant, explained his  
professional background upon inquiry, stating he has spent significant time working in hydrology 
and he believes issues brought forth regarding storm drainage in the area are valid.  He stated 
requirements for peak discharge are in place and may help alleviate drainage concerns. Discussion 
topics included: designation of d rainage area, future development of the land, City and County 
involvement to address drainag e concerns and long-standing drainage issues in the area.  Mayor 
Orr voiced concern for  inheriting drainage issues that would become the City’s responsibility to 
maintain.  Mr. McDonald advised the annexation won’t contribute to the existing drainage issues 
as they are south of the proposed development and that the area of concern is in a county pocket.  
Dr. Sheehan voiced concern of the project b eing piecemealed and not knowing the impact until 
completion of the development and requested Broken Arrow Rd. be made into a gravel road. Upon 
inquiry, County Commissioner Amber Ash advised the road  receives limited maintenance due to 
allocation of resources based on use, traffic and other factors. She noted water tables tend to be 
higher on the south side of town and advised she will research the drainage concerns.  Further 
comments included: Condition of Southwest Drive and lack of funding to address problem areas, 
drainage in the annexation area, maintenance of Broken Arrow Rd., annexation impact not directly 
related to flooding and maintenance concerns, support for the annexation, platting of the property, 
piecemeal annexations and the need to develop south Cheyenne.  Upon inquiry, Amy Allen, City 
Engineer, explained there are n’t any plans for drainage studies in the annexation area, however, 
areas near Crow Creek are being reviewed for utilization of FEMA grants in conjunction with the 
County to improve flooding.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mayor Orr voting “no”. (#4266) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning District Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Unified Development Code, amending the 
zoning classification for a portion of the southeast quarter of Section 11 and the northeast quarter 
of  Section 14 in Township 13 North, Range 67 West from the Overland Trails Planned Unit 
Development (PUD) to the CFD Park -N-Ride PUD for the 27.69-acre Lot 1 of the CFD Park -N-
Ride Subdivision in the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of Southwest 
Drive, north of College Drive, and adjacent to I -25).  Glenn Connor offered suggestions to help 
  
alleviate traffic concerns in the area of the Park-N-Ride and spoke in support of the ordinance. Dr. 
Kay Sheehan requested landscaping requirements in the area .  Brad Emmons, AVI  Engineering, 
P.C., agent for applicant, explained letters requesting feedback relating to landscaping were sent 
to adjacent property owners  and that no  responses were received.  He expressed concern for 
maintaining landscap ing and explained they are looking into working with  the Conservation 
District to acquire trees for the area . It was noted zone change applicants can write their own 
requirements for a PUD.  Mr. Emmons explained landscaping wouldn’t be visible as it would be 
behind houses.  Mary Garcia voiced concerns over various aspects of the project.   Upon inquiry, 
information was provided regarding entrance and exit locations  and a future easement for 
emergency vehicle access.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the 
exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#4267) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapters 5.48, Detective, Patrol and Guard 
Service Businesses, 5.52, Massage Establishments, and 5.72, Public Transportation, within Title 
5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4268) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.56, Handicapped Parking Spaces, 
within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming . 
Joe Chenchar, Assistant City Attorney, explained the  amendment is to  clarify the location of 
application submittal.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the Wyoming Retirement System Deferred Compensation Plan 
for the benef it of full -time employees of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and allowing for 
automatic enrollment of new full-time employees.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5984) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
CFD Park-N-Ride Subdivision for a portion of the southeast quarter of Section 11 and the northeast 
quarter of Section 14, Township 13 North, Range 67 West, situated in Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located east of I -25, west of Southwest Drive, south of Swan Ranch Road, and north 
of College Drive) .  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt , seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5985) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor to enter into a Memorandum of Understanding 
with the Governing Bodies of other local governments that are affected by the proposed 
Roundhouse Wind Energy Project at the Belvoir Ranch pursuant to the Wyoming Department of 
Environmental Quality Industrial Siting Regulations. Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, Assistant City 
Attorney, advised the resolution is to request certain monetary reimbursements  for unmitigated 
costs for City services  related to development of the Wind Energy P roject at the Belvoir Ranch .  
Upon inquiry, Ms. McCoy Fakelman explained  monetary reimbursement w ill not come from 
revenue funds from the project.  County Commissioner Amber Ash stated meetings have been held 
with Laramie County entities who are anticipating unmitigated monetary impact from the project. 
  
She provided information on the proposal that will be presented to the Industrial Siting Council. 
Upon inquiry, Ms. Ash explained signatures  and letters detailing the request from participating 
entities are being obtained and advised requests will include monetary reimbursement, equipment, 
fire trucks, communication towers and lodging taxes. Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
Esquibel.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5986) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Proclaiming the two -block radius around the City’s east parking lot at 
17th Street and Warren Avenue as Cheyenne’s Downtown Restaurant District . Dr. Rinne 
announced there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee. Dr. Rinne 
moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  White.  Mr. Laybourn provided a presentation related to 
downtown restaurants, merchants and various sidewalk, curb and gutter areas downtown that are 
in need of repair. He explained there isn’t a funding mechanism in place to address maintenance 
downtown and provided information on the purpose of the resolution noting there are no expenses 
related to the resolution.  Concern was expressed related to the intention of the resolution and not 
highlighting restaurants in all areas of town. Mr. Case moved to amend by designating in Wards 2 
and 3  - Dell Range  and the  Sportsman’s Warehouse and Gold’s Gym strip mall areas  as 
commercial zones and the areas of Sunrise and  Sun Valley as residential  districts for curb and 
pavement repairs, seconded by Mr. Luna. Discussion followed and included the following topics: 
need for downtown improvements and revitalization, opposition of the amendment, responsibility 
of curb and gutter repairs downtown, shared concern and appreciation for a vital downtown area, 
resolution providing a tool that can be used with restaurant tours and downtown property owners, 
6th Penny overage funds being utilized for downtown maintenance and the C ity’s maintenance 
responsibilities.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case and Mr. Luna.  Voting “no” – 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.   
Further discussion included a ppreciation for the resolution as a statement of support for the 
downtown area, funding required for maintenance, City’s possible participation in future repairs  
and the possibility of utilizing state grant funding for downtown maintenance.  A point of order 
was raised related to voicing support for the resolution  without first turning over duties of the 
Chair.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal and 
Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne. (#5987) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne supports 
the application of the Town of Burns to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board by 
the Board of Commissioners of Laramie County for 2015 -2016 Countywide Consensus Block 
Grant funds in the amount of $18,739.00 to replace the Community Center roof -top heating and 
air conditioning unit – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Christensen Park, situated in a portion of the SW¼ NE¼, Section 36, Township 14 North, Range 
67 West of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the 
southwest corner of Talbot Court and Cribbon Avenue) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Calling for an ADA Compliance Evaluation and Transition Plan for the 
City of Cheyenne Municipal Building – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
  
 
[CA] Preliminary Plat for Harmony Valley, 1st Filing, a plat of a portion of the S½ of Section 7 
and the SE¼NE¼ of Section 7, T.13N., R.66W., 6 th P.M., Cheyenne Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located north of and adjacent to W. College Dr., west of Walterscheid Blvd.) – referred to Public 
Services Committee. 
 
 Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Goat Green, LLC, Wellington, Colorado, for 
vegetation management along Dry Creek and Crow Creek (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Sam Berta, 
Staff Engineer, requested a postponement due to another vendor expressing interest in the project.  
Mr. Berta explained, upon inquiry, the City has only been aware of one vendor that provides this 
type of service in the community and postponing the item won’t negatively impact the service start 
date as the schedule allows for flexibility.  TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager, City Treasurer’s 
Office, explained the contract has been advertised in the newspaper once as a sole source project 
and if the contract is disputed, the bid process can be utilized.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Berta explained 
the following: Possible licensing requirements of the vendor, formal bid process was not utilized 
as it was considered a sole source contract, sole source contract publication requirement was not 
done initially and that no further communication with Goat Green, LLC has occurred.  Mr. White 
moved to approve in the amount of $100,000.00, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
postpone to April 22, 2019, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Berta explained the contract 
has been a dvertised once and will be advertised again  meeting the requirement of advertising 
twice.  Mr. Berta  noted he advised the additional interested vendor could attend the upcoming 
Finance Committee meeting .  Comments were made related to the importance of utilizing a 
qualified business and in o pposition of the postponement.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting 
“yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – 
Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr and Mr. Roybal. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Plan One / Architects, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for City Municipality Re -Roofing Services for the Aquatic Center, Fleet 
Maintenance Facility and Parks Mainten ance Shop (Capital Improvement Plan (CIP) funding) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Frontier 
Days, Inc., for the use of city-owned land and buildings for parking, camping, and other activities 
during the 2019 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne Police Department and the 
Laramie County Sheriff’s Department for EOD, SWAT and CNU Operations and law enforcement 
extraterritorial assistance and activities – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract No. 6932 between the City of Cheyenne and Asbestos 
& Technical Services , Cheyenne, Wyoming, for asbestos abatement ser vices for 2247 East 19 th 
Street (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid # E-15-19 for furnishing 95 gallon and 65 gallon automated refuse 
carts for the Sanitation Division (Solid Waste fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept 
  
bid submitted by Otto Environmental Systems North America, Charlotte, NC, in an amount not to 
exceed $132,034.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid # S-11-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, and Simon 
Contractors, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Smalley Park Greenway Reconstruction Project 
(Greenway Maintenance fund) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid submitted by 
Simon Contractors, Cheyenne, WY, in an amount not to exceed $119,190.30). (#6975) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-12-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, and Reiman 
Corp., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Evers Boulevard Reconstruction Project (2015-2018 1% Sales 
Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid submitted by Reima n Corp., Cheyenne, 
WY, in an amount not to exceed $7,453,668.50). (#6976) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-14-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, and American 
Contracting of Wyoming LLC, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Jr. League Dugout Roof 
Replacements and Masonry Repairs Project (Reserves carryover).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (accept bid submitted by American Contracting of Wyoming, LLC, Cheyenne, WY, in an 
amount not to exceed $60,800.00). (#6977) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-19-19 for furnishing bituminous asphalt hot mix, tack oil and cold 
mix on an annual basis (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held April 22, 2019, at 6:00 p.m. in City Council 
Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action 
Plan Fiscal Year 2019 prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Dr. Rinne noted that the motion to adopt the Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City 
Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the CFD Park -N-Ride Subdivision for a portion of the southeast 
quarter of Section 11 and the northeast quarter of Section 14, Township 13 North, Range 67 West, 
situated in Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located east of I-25, west of Southwest Drive, 
south of Swan Ranch Road, and north of College Drive) passed earlier in the meeting without the 
recommended motion in its entirety.  Dr. Rinne, advising he had voted on the prevailing side, and 
pursuant to City Code Chapter 2.04, moved to reconsider the  motion of the resolution, seconded 
by Mr. Luna.  Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director, explained the appropriateness 
and importance of reconsidering the resolution with the recommended motion. Motion to 
reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Dr. Rinne moved 
to adopt with the condition that the revised plat show a southern access easement from Southwest 
Drive prior to the City signing the final plat, seconded by Mr. Case. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present. 
 
Upon inquiry, Amy Allen, City Engineer, provided information on the single source 
contract process with Goat Green, LLC for vegetation management.  She adv ised staff will 
  
recommend the vendor that is most qualified for the project.   Logan Sharpe, Assistant City 
Attorney, provided information on legal advertising publishing requirements. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:57 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-04-22 | CLIP_ID: 708 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 708

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 22, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor  and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Councilman White recognized members of C ub Scout Pack #124 and Boy Scout Troup 
#102 who were present to observe government in action. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Luna and Mr. Case, Agenda Item # 13 was removed from the Consent Agenda . Mr. Shanor 
declared a conflict of interest (business affiliation with agenda item #11) and vacated council 
chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Following announcement of 
the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council chambers. 
 
[CA] Minutes from  Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 8 , 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan Fiscal 
Year 2019 prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office.  Deanne 
Widauf, Housing and Community Development, provided a brief overview of the history and 
purpose of the Community Development Block Grant program , noting the proposed plan is 
consistent with the 5 -year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan  developed in 2015, and stated the 
anticipated allocation funding amount is $433,947.00 .  Ms. Widauf explained the Tier system 
being utilized and reviewed information on the grant application process; public notifications and 
opportunities for input; role of the Advisory Council in reviewing and recommending projects to 
be funded  and explained the projects that are being proposed.  She explained Housing & 
Community Development is anticipating $85,000.00 in recaptured funding.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 10.56, Handicapped Parking Spaces, 
within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming . 
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governin g body.  
(#4269) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule – referred to Finance Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County CB Community Business to 
CB Community Business for all of FW Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast 
of the intersection of College Drive and Southwest Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne supports 
the application of the Town of Burns to be su bmitted to the State Loan and Investment Board by 
the Board of Commissioners of Laramie County for 2015 -2016 Countywide Consensus Block 
Grant funds in the amount of $18,739.00 to replace the Community Center roof -top heating and 
air conditioning unit.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5988) 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Christensen Park, situated in a portion of the SW¼ NE¼, Section 36, Township 14 North, Range 
67 West of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the 
southwest corner of Talbot Court and Cribbon Avenue) . Dr. Rinne moved to a dopt, seconded by 
Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne explained there were two conditions recommended that include: Provide 
required proof of ownership and provide a written access agreement with the City.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to postpone  to May 13, 2019,  seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Support for postponement was 
voiced by members of the governing body and it was expressed that there is an administrative need 
to fulfill n ecessary requirements, such as property ownership, prior to seeking governing body  
approval.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 RESOLUTION – Calling for an ADA Compliance Evaluation and Transition Plan for the 
City of Cheyenne Municipal Building .  Patti Riesland, Mayor’s Council for People with 
Disabilities, spoke in favor of the resolution.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved 
to adopt, seconded by Laybourn.  Upon inquiry regarding funding, Eric Fountain, Chief of Staff, 
advised current ly a funding source has not been identified and reported the purpose of the 
resolution is to identify ADA shortcomings and develop a compliance plan for the future.  He 
explained this approach has been taken with certain Community Recreation and Events facilities, 
the Municipal Court and the Public Safety Center currently meet ADA compliance requirements, 
total cost for ADA updates has not yet been established and the City has not been contacted by the 
Department of Justice ( DOJ) as of yet.  Mr. Fountain added that a timeline hasn’t been clearly 
defined and that once a complaint is received by the DOJ an analysis will be initiated regarding 
federal requirements which will require a compliance timeline.  Concern was expressed related to 
  
status of Municipal Building ADA compliance including existing barriers involving access for 
people with disabilities.  It was suggested the Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax be considered 
as a funding source.  Further discussion included current compliance process being com plaint 
driven, funding source options and  deficiencies in the building including  the HVAC system and 
modern infrastructure issues. Support was voiced relating to the resolution concept and a proactive 
approach to meet compliance.  Mr. Shanor moved to lay the main motion on the table, seconded 
by Mr. Case.  Mr. Laybourn raised a point of order related to the motion and process requesting 
clarification.  Mayor Orr advised 5 th Penny Sales Tax is referred to as a capital improvement tax 
in city code.  Ales sandra McCoy Fakelman , Assistant City Attorney,  upon inquiry, explained a 
motion to lay on the table is an acceptable and proper motion according to Robert’s Rules of Order.  
It was advised the motion is not debatable or amendable.  Motion to lay on the ta ble failed (tie 
vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” – 
Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.   Mr. Fountain provided 
information on the following upon inquiry: it is his under standing ADA grants have been 
unsuccessful in the past and there ha ve not been any recent applications submitted , compliance 
efforts could be addressed one issue at a time,  funding needs would be identified once the study 
has been completed and noted the rule of thumb is compliance updates cost 10% of the price of a 
building remodel.  Jason Sanchez, Community Recreation and Events Deputy Director, reported a 
2012 analysis was conducted in an effort to be proactive and identify areas of non-compliance for 
Community Recreation and Events facilities. He explained the method of addressing the non -
compliant areas, stating corrections are made annually in conjunction with other improvements in 
an on-going effort to become closer to being compliant. Upon inquiry, Mr. Sanchez advised cost 
estimates are done as each improvement is addressed . Comments were made related to  
understanding federal requirements and timeframes, the need for addressing federal requirements, 
fiscal responsibilities and a proactive approach.  Vicki Nemecek , Director of Public Works , 
explained upon inquiry, that previous attempts to acquire Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax (6th 
Penny) funding were unsuccessful regarding renovation of the Municipal Building during the last 
two 6th Penny ballots.  Motion failed (tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, 
Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Shanor.  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne Annual Action Plan 2019 prepared by 
the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submission of an application to the Wyoming Business 
Council, Business Ready Community Program, for a $1,500,000.00 Community Readiness Grant 
for the Happy Jack Road/F.E. Warren Air Force Base Enhanced Use Lease Infrastructure Project 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Preliminary Plat for Harmony Valley, 1st Filing, a plat of a portion of the S½ of Section 7 
and the SE¼NE¼ of Section 7, T.13N., R.66W., 6 th P.M., Cheyenne Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located north of and adjacent to W. College Dr., west of Walterscheid Blvd.) .  Holli Welch, 
Woodhouse, Rod en and Nethercott  LLC, represent ing an  adjacent landowner, advised area 
landowners have concerns relating to access, noting  access has been changed once prior  to the 
  
proposed development and advised the development shouldn’t be problematic as long as access 
doesn’t change.  Kelly Hafner, CivilWorx, LLC, agent for the applicant, reported development 
plans include maintaining rights-of-way and granting access easements on current property on the 
final plat .  Dr. Rinne moved to acknowledge the Planning C ommission’s recommendation to 
approve with staff conditions #1-14, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Goat Green, LLC, Wellington, Colorado, for 
vegetation management along Dry Creek and Crow Creek (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (in the amount of $100,000.00). (#6980) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Plan One / Architects, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for City Municipality Re -Roofing Services for the Aquatic Center, Fleet

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $50,155.00). (#6981) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Frontier 
Days, Inc., for the use of city-owned land and buildings for parking, camping, and other activities

(#6982) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne Police Department and the

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Josh Cossitt to provide tennis services and

[CA] Memorandum of Understan ding between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County

[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract No. 6932 between the City of Cheyenne and Asbestos 
& Technical Services , Cheyenne, Wyoming, for asbestos abatement services for 2247 East 19 th

[CA] Renewal of Contract #6837 between the City of Cheyenne and Delta Dental for group 
dental benefits for City employees – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract No. 6924 between the City of Cheyenne  and TC

Court (1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #E-19-19 for furnishing bituminous asphalt hot mix, tack oil and cold
