(accept bid from JTL Group Inc. d/b/a Knife River, Cheyenne, WY, in an amount not to exceed 
$284,265.00).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase two Lifepak defibrillator devices for the Cheyenne Fire and 
Rescue Department (General fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Appointments of Brandon VanTassell to the Fire Code Board of Appeals and Eric Prior to 
the Housing and Co mmunity Development Advisory Council .  Mr. Luna moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments). 
 
Reappointment of Nicholas A. Zahner to the International Fire Code Board of Appeals .  
Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral 
appointments). 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 13, 2019, at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public comment 
regarding the intent of the City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming 
Business Council’s Business Ready Community, Community Readiness Grant Program, to assist 
in funding the F.E. Warren Enhanced Use Lease Infrastructure Project. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 13, 2019, at 6:0 0 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a social club liquor 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Mary Godfrey Playhouse, 2706 E. 
Pershing Blvd., Cheyenne, WY. 
  
Announcement was made of a Public He aring to be held May 13, 2019, at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a restaurant liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Adeli, LLC d/b/a Railyard Café – Coffee Haus & 
Eatery, 1620 E. Pershing Blvd., Cheyenne, WY. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Rocky Bolin, Knights of Pythia s Lodge, provided Lodge formation and location history 
which is located near the property of the new Municipal Courthouse project.  He expressed concern 
related to losing access to their kitchen windows which are on the east side of the building during 
construction of the project and provided information regarding contact he has had with the project 
architect and manager.  Ben Hornak, TC Solutions, Project Manager, expressed appreciation to the 
Knight’s of Pythias for their accommodation  during the project and reported there are fire codes 
relating to separation between the two buildings that will need to be complied with . A ruling will 
be requested from the City Building Official regarding what is required for a fire separation wall.  
  
Comments were made related to assisting in other ways to provide natural lighting in the building 
that houses the Lodge. 
 
Joe Ramirez, Chair of Wyoming Alliance Non-Partisan Group, provided a report related 
to community relations and law enforcement.  Mr. Ramirez asked for feedback from the Governing 
Body and advised the group has worked with Police Chief Brian Kozak. 
 
Jeff Geyer, Laramie County Conservation District, and Dena Egen hoff, BOPU , g ave a 
presentation on the Crow Creek Revival  stream restoration project  stating the purpose is  to 
promote, enhance, restore and revitalize the ecological values and functions of Crow Creek.   The 
presentation included information on: Creek history and patte rn, realignment of Crow Creek, 
details of project phases  and goals , project funding  and grants received , importance of public 
access and water quality and current and prior master plans. Ms. Egenhoff stated a community 
Crow Creek clean-up day is scheduled for May 11, 2019.  Further information was provided related 
to Crow Creek history, a 1993 master plan and how the project is related to an enhanced use lease 
resolution and public hearing that will be held on May 1 3, 2019. Support for the project was  
expressed and comments were made  related to  the relationship between Crow Creek and the 
Greenway system. It was noted that a Memorandum of Understanding was approved two years 
ago related to the project. 
 
John Moore, Wyoming Goat Farm , provided informatio n relating to his company and 
desire to be considered for the  goat grazing services contract which was approved earlier on the 
agenda.   Amy Allen, City Engineer,  advised the contract was awarded as a sole source project 
with all advertising requirements having been met. There was an option to discuss the contract at 
the Finance Committee meeting held on April 15, 2019.  Ms. Allen explained the contract will be 
reviewed again next year.  
 
Mr. Esquibel noted that at the April 8, 2019 City Council meeting, th e motion to approve 
Resolution #5987 proclaiming the two-block radius around the City’s east parking lot at 17th Street 
and Warren Avenue as Cheyenne’s Downtown Restaurant District  was adopted.  Mr. Esquibel, 
advising he had voted on the prevailing side , and pursuant to City Code Chapter 2.04, moved to 
reconsider the vote taken on the  motion, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Esquibel explained 
following adoption of the resolution,  the need to address city -wide curb and gutter concerns and 
funding options were discussed.  Motion to reconsider carried. Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, 
Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” – Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr, Mr. 
Roybal and Mr. White.  Comments were made in support of addressing city-wide curb and gutter 
repairs, looking into funding mechanisms for such and how the resolution only pertains to a 
specific area of town.   Amy Allen, City Engineer , advised the 1% Construction Department is 
reviewing community curb and gutter  concerns and working on a proactive approach for 
addressing them.   Further comments were made in support of the original resolution as the 
intention is to establish a downtown restaurant district and bringing forth resolutions for additional 
restaurant districts.  Motion failed. Voting “yes” – Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal and Mr. 
White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.   
 
  
Mayor Orr provided information on  the Cheyenne Day of Giving to be held on May 10, 
2019 and the Civic Center Commons ribbon cutting ceremony and Cheyenne Day of Service to be 
held on May 11, 2019 at 9:00 a.m. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:38 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

  May 2, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-05-13 | CLIP_ID: 709 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 709

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 13, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor  and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda items , seconded by Mr . Luna .  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 22, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. Shanor.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive public comment regarding the intent of the 
City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s 
Business Ready Community, Community Readiness Grant Program, to assist in funding the F.E. 
Warren Enhanced Use Lease Infrastructure Project.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, provided 
grant application process information stating a public hearing and resolution are requirements of 
the grant.  Ms. Schneider advised the City was awarded  a grant and loan last year related to the 
same project and that a dditional infrastructure is needed.  There being no further comments or 
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a  public hearing for an application for a social club liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Mary Godfrey Playhouse, 2706 E. Pershing Blvd., 
Cheyenne, WY.  Brenda Lyttle , C heyenne Little Theatre Players Board of Directors , provided 
information on the application and establishment and stated the Atlas Theater has had a social club 
liquor license since 2014 . She explained minimal alcoholic beverages would be available for 
purchase at certain shows.   Upon inquiry, Ms. Lyttle explained all bartenders will receive TIPS 
training, alcohol sales will not take place  during school hours due t o the proximity of East High 
School and that alcohol will be locked up when not for sale .  John Lyttle added information 
regarding the type of alcohol consumption that typically occurs du ring theater productions and 
  
explained the Mary Godfrey Playhouse has a positive working relationship with East High School.    
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr 
closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr op ened a  public hearing for an application for a restaurant liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Adeli, LLC d/b/a Railyard Café – Coffee Haus & 
Eatery, 1620 E. Pershing Blvd., Cheyenne, WY.   Freddy Flores-Salieb, Railyard Café – Coffee 
Haus & Eatery, advised she was available for questions.  There being no further comments or input 
and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule. Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, provided information related to an in-house 
cost accounting analysis and fee increase needs. She explained commercial and contractor loads 
will now be charged by weight, cost of recyclable material has been increased and there is now a 
standardized scattered trash collection fee.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised last year was the 
fourth year in a series of consultant recommended increases and has been 5% even though an 8% 
increase for 5 years was recommended in order to meet landfill maintenance needs.  She explained 
a 5% rate increase is sufficient due to a vertical landfill expansion and there being time to collect 
funds for a new expansion in the future.  Ms. Nemecek provided information on Note 2 on the fee 
schedule, upon inquiry, explaining it is related to strip malls or buildings with multiple businesses 
that share a waste container.  Fees are based on volume and frequency of pickup, and the basic rate 
fee is shared by all participating businesses. Ms. Nemecek provided further information related to 
cost savings seen by not taking trash to Ault, CO, stating savings are being used for fuel, equipment 
maintenance, employee overtime if necessary, litter fences and potentially m ore employees.   
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend fee schedule Note 2, sentence 2 to read “Buildings will 
be assessed based on the type and quantity of containe rs and frequency of pickups as outlined in 
this Solid Waste Fee Schedule, and by deleting the third and  following sentence starting with 
“Multiple collocated…”, s econded by Mr. Case. Mr. Shanor  provided information on prior fee 
schedules stating customers weren’t charged for the service, but rather arbitrary metrics such as 
how many trash receptacles were located at a building. That method of billing was updated to 
charge based on size and frequency of pickups.  The amendment will be revenue neutral and the  
fee schedule will be adjusted to be consistent with the proposed amendment.  Ms. Nemecek further 
explained Note 2 stating it applies to businesses like the refinery that have multiple receptacles in 
various locations with multiple pickups.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Shanor voting “yes”.  Main motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna 
and Mr. Shanor voting “no”.

 Announcement was made that due to the Memorial Day holiday and City offices being 
closed on Monday, May 27, 2019, the regularly scheduled City Council meeting will instead be 
held on Tuesday, May 28, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in the Municipal Building, City Council Chambers, 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Under other business: 
 
City Clerk Kris Jones explained agenda item #21 (c) was inadvertently referred to Finance 
Committee, and that it will instead be heard at the May 21, 2019 Public Services Committee 
meeting to be held at 12:00 p.m. in Municipal Building Room 104. 
 
Mr. White commented on the Big Boy 4014  steam locomotive  event which attracted 
thousands of attendees to the community and expressed appreciation to all who assisted with the 
event and also the C ivic Center Commons ribbon cutting ceremony, Crow Creek clean-up event 
and the ground breaking for Blue Federal Credit Union world headquarters at Cole Square. 
 
Mr. Laybourn expressed appreciation for budget work sessions that were held and spoke 
about continuing funding of the Downtown Development Authority.  He stated, for the record, that 
he had received a bill for almost $1,000 .00 for a public records request for 18 months worth of 
minutes and vouchers  from the D DA. Mr. Laybourn  further commented on fairness and 
transparency for tax payers, possible public meetings to provide information on how the agency 
operates and the purpose of requesting documentation is to see how funds are being utilized.  
 
  
Mr. Esquibel provided information on the Union Pacific Steam Shop and Roundhouse 
located in Cheyenne and advised a functional steam shop would likely boost tourism  in th e 
community. 
 
Mr. Case advised the Wyoming Public Records Act allows for  organizations to charge  
certain fees for researching information for public records requests.   Concern was expressed 
related to Governing Body decorum, process and how business is conducted. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:47 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-05-28 | CLIP_ID: 711 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 711

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 28, 2019 
 
 Due to the Memorial Day holiday, t he Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne  instead 
met in regular session on this date beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:10 
p.m.), Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  
Absent: Ken Esquibel.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
EXPRESSION OF APPRECIATION TO THE 2018-2019 MAYOR’S YOUTH 
COUNCIL AND STATE OF THE YOUTH ADDRESS. Jan Spires, Youth Alternatives and staff 
advisor to the Mayor’s Youth Council , expressed appreciation to Youth Council members, 
recognized their year in service and introduced Julia Horst, chair for the Mayor’s Youth Council, 
who presented the State of the Youth Address.  Ms. Horst reviewed projects and programs the 
group participated in throughout the year.  She expressed appreciation to Mayor Orr , Ms. Spires 
and fellow Youth Council members.  Mayor Orr expressed appreciation to the Youth Council and 
expressed the importance of the Unaccompanied Students Initiative. Ms. Spires introduced and 
presented awards to the following members: Alexis Arp, Maggie Black, Ashen Doty, Darby 
Downham, Raegan Downham, Connor Fertig, Dylan Fertig, Julia Horst, Marie Lucas, Emily 
Lucero, Lars Quinlivan, Kenitra Shanks, Dominick Singleton and Marty Zhang.   
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Cook and Mr. Case, Agenda Item #21 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Case, Agenda Item #26 (e) 
was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda 
items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  
 
Mr. Case moved to suspend the rules by  bringing forward Agenda Item # 25 (g) before 
Agenda Item #6 to allow for consideration of the Cable Franchise Agreement between the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Spectrum Pacific West, LLC l/k/a Charter Communications , seconded 
by Mr. Shanor. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body present. 
 
Cable Franchise Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Spectrum 
Pacific West, LLC l/k/a Charter Communications.  Bob Duchen, River Oaks Communications , 
provided a review of the franchise agreement explaining it is a non-exclusive contract which would 
allow other cable providers to come into Cheyenne. Mr. Duchen advised they are working to find 
solutions for the City and Charter Communications and areas of the agreement that are being fine-
  
tuned are the government ac cess channel including updating the return line and aspects of the 
customer service center. Mr. Duchen advised Charter isn’t agreeable to amendments at this time  
and provided a substitute amendment to the governing body.  Ben Rowland , Davis & Cannon, 
LLP, reported details are being finalized with ongoing negotiations and suggested approval of the 
substitute amendment.  Mr. Rowland explained if the agreement is postponed, a month to month 
agreement will occur due to the current agreement lapsing.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. 
White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Case moved to postpone for two weeks, 
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  It was noted the amendment was not distributed to all members of the 
governing body.  Comments were made related to postponing the item vs. approving a substitute 
amendment.  Mr. Rowland clarified the procedures of postponing the agreement and approving a 
substitute amendment. Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 13, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. Case. 
Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer and Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director, Community 
Recreation and Events provided information available on vouchers payable to the following: JM 
Rodent Control (rodent control at Dinosaur Park – area around Pride Park) and Sundahl, Powers, 
Kapp and Martin LLC (professional legal services for Fight the Blight Initiative funded from the 
general fund).  Responding to an inquiry related to the voucher payable to Hansen, Allen & Luce 
Inc., Amy Allen, City Engineer,  explained the City is under  contract for review of ordinances 
pertaining to drainage issues and confirmed she received directive to enter into the contract.  Ms. 
Allen reviewed the scope of work  as it pertains to the voucher in question (College and Storey 
(Summit) Drainage Investigation ) and advised a final solution based report  will contain 
recommendations. Discussion included: setting precedence for reviewing issues in subdivisions,  
relation of the contract and a recently failed resolution calling for a drainage study in the area and 
the importance of City/County relationship moving forward to address drainage .  Upon inquiry, 
Ms. Allen explained the drainage investigation will review pre and post development of the area. 
Mayor Orr advised the intent is to bring c hanges before the governing body.  Mr. Shanor moved 
to divide the question to allow for separate action on the voucher payable to Hansen, Allen & Luce 
Inc. in the amount of $5,972.84 for College and Storey (Summit) Drainage Investigation, seconded 
by Dr. R inne. Mayor Orr noted the motion was no t debatable.  Motion to divide the question 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present  with the exception of Mr . 
Laybourn voting “no”.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order related to making a neg ative motion 
(motion to deny the voucher).  Comment was made related to  circumventing the governing body 
approval process.  Ms. Allen clarified a contract with Hansen, Allen & Luce Inc. has been executed 
and the voucher is for a portion of work that has bee n conducted.  Mayor Orr commented on the 
decision to enter into the contract, explained it isn’t her intention to circumvent the governing body 
approval process and requested review of prior City Council proceedings related to the drainage 
study. Upon inquiry, Alessandra McCoy-Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, provided information 
on the following: breech in contract if affiliated bills aren’t paid, termination clause allowing the 
contract to be terminated and the voucher process, most contracts are for items that have  been 
authorized by the governing body , contracts under $35,000 .00 can be executed administratively, 
  
possibility of revising purchasing requirements and if the contract is terminated, invoices will have 
to be paid for work that has already been completed.  Comments were made in support of the 
contract and how it relates to recent severe weather events, the importance of having a third-party 
review of drainage concerns and support for paying the voucher. Ms. Allen advised recommended 
solutions for drainage issues will show affiliated repair costs.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and 
Mr. Shanor voting “no”.   Motion to approv e remaining vouchers carried.   Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
 Mayor Orr opened a  public hearing for an application for a winery liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for GrapesNwine, LLC d/b/a Vintages, 309 North 
American Rd., Cheyenne, Wyoming. Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, provided information 
related to the proposed business stating the  applicant could manufacture wine and dispense the 
wine for on -premises and limited off -premises personal consumption.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing and 
referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule. Vicki Nemecek Public Works Director, recommended approval of the proposed annual 
5% rate increase.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Support was voiced for straight fees for services instead 
of a basic rate fee schedule.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor voting “no”. (#4270)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County CB Community Business to 
CB Community Business for all of FW Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast 
of the intersection of College Drive and Southwest Drive).  Glen Connor, adjacent property owner, 
advised Dr. Kay Sheehan , fellow adjacent property owner,  could not be present to voice her 
opposition and provided a letter from her to the governing body.  Mr. Conn or explained he has 
concerns with area  flooding that is  occurring, noting Ms. Sheehan ’s property to the north is 
currently flooded.  Mr. Connor requested  consideration of  drainage issues before future 
development takes place.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Shanor.  Gene MacDonald, agent for  the applicant, 
provided drainage information regarding the current and proposed detention pond locations for 
FW Subdivision, stated there isn’t much drainage in the current location and there are no signs of 
erosion on Broken Arrow Rd. from water runoff from FW Subdivision.  Mr. MacDonald advised 
the property was previously annexed into the City and it is required to be City zoned, noting the 
zoning classification will remain CB - Community Business.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present.  (#4271) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities. Randy 
  
Hays, Board of Public Utilities, explained there is a recommended 3% water rate increase that will 
allow for multiple projects over the course of the next several years  and loan payoffs which will 
in turn generate more revenue .  Mr. Hays advised a 5% increase for sewer  rates is proposed to 
assist with funding a Dry Creek project and several other revenue generating projects. He provided 
approximate increase amounts for residential and commercial customers.  Per inquiry, Mr. Hays 
explained the BOPU master plan is updated every 10 years and is due to be updated in 2023.  Mr. 
White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2019 and ending June 30, 2020.  Stephanie 
Lowe, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA),  voiced support for the 
contract with Novo Benefits for negotiation assistance on the employee health insurance policy 
with Blue Cross Blue Shield of Wyoming  resulting in no premium increases.  Joe Dougherty 
requested information on the newly proposed Compliance Division, projected revenue reductions, 
number of City employees and voiced concerns on not offering employees wage increases.  Upon 
inquiry, Eric Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of Staff, explained the Compliance Division will consist of 
18.4 positions from Risk Management, Nuisance, ADA Compliance and the Building Department.  
He explained it is not a requirement that the nuisance officer be uniformed Police Department 
position.  Robin Lockman, City Treasurer,  explained upon inquiry  certain revenue line items 
haven’t seen losses, but instead were over-estimated for the 2019 fiscal year.  Mr. Fountain spoke 
about the previous 3% pay for perform ance incentive and reported it wasn’t included in the 
proposed current budget for fiscal year 2020. Reporting for Committee of the Whole,  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by amendment dated 
5-24-19, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor explained the amendment moves $18,000 out of the 
Police Patrol budget line item into Cheyenne Fire & Rescue budget relating to Cheyenne Frontier 
Days overtime expenses and by moving the previously budgeted 9% health insur ance increases 
from each department into a miscellaneous account intended for funding a proposed self -insured 
line item.   Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by a n “omnibus budget amendment” containing 11 separate 
amendments, seconded by Mr.  Luna. Mr. Shanor provided an overview of the proposed 
amendment which includes the following changes:  Subtract $140,000.00 from City Council Line 
item (#1), place $90,000.00 into the Engineering line item for right -of-way infrastructure 
improvement program (#2), place $50,000.00 in the Engineering line item to crea te a fund for 
necessary studies evaluating prospective 6th Penny projects to be approved by the Governing Body 
(#3), subtract $20,000.00 from Public Works (#4), subtract $4,000.00 from Community Recreation 
and Events (#5), subtract $130,566.00 form Community Recreation and Events and eliminate two 
new proposed positions (Aquatics and Maintenance Tech)  (#6), place $297,500.00 i nto the 
Economic/Community Development line item  - $190,000.00 to the Downtown Development 
Authority (DDA) and $107,500.00 to the Cheyenne Animal She lter for Animal Control services  
(#7), place $24,000.00 into Community Recreation and Events for professional services (#8), place 
$40,000.00 into Special Projects for an art project in partnership with Arts Cheyenne  (#9), freeze 
all general fund positions from hiring of any current vacant positions and any future vacated 
  
positions until the savings of the proposed amendments ($206,934.00) is met (#10) and cap overall 
full-time employees at 578.1 for FY 2020 (#11).  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, advised 
leaving 5-6 Street and Alley positions vacant would restrict community services.  Greg Hoggatt, 
Chief of Cheyenne Fire and Rescue, inquired about the proposed hiring freeze and how it pertains 
to Firefighters. Mr. Shanor explained Firefighters would be excluded due to the City’s contractual 
agreement with the Fire Union.  Teresa Moore , Community Recreatio n and Events Director,  
provided information on current Aquatics staff and a proposed new position and a request for a 
new Parks maintenance employee.  Astrid, Plains Hotel, expressed appreciation for the proposed 
Downtown Development Authority (DDA) funding.  Alessandra McCoy-Fakelman, Deputy City 
Attorney, expressed concern with the proposed hiring freeze and how it relates to the currently 
vacant City Attorney position.  Anthony Ortiz, local business owner, expressed concern regarding 
current opera tions of the DDA, explained he requested information related to DDA funding 
sources and requested  more program oversight.   Charles Bloom, Planning and Development  
Director, expressed concern regarding the proposed hiring freeze relating to a proposed Planner II 
position in his department, explaining it is budgeted to begin in October.  Ms. Lockman provided 
information on projected time frame to recuperate the proposed $206,934.00 needed to lift the 
hiring freeze which could include contingency funds from  the Mayor’s budget and the cost of 
proposed new City website.  Vicki Duggar, DDA Director, expressed appreciation for the proposed 
DDA funding and advised they recently approve d moving DDA reserves into  the façade 
improvement program. Carman Hess, Wyoming Home, spoke in favor of the DDA program  and 
funding.  Brian Kozak, Chief of Police, expressed concern regarding the proposed amendment and 
how it relates to freezing the department’s positions.  Mr. Shanor explained the intent is to freeze 
all positions until savings is realized.  Chief Kozak provided information on current and proposed 
vacancies and absences due to deployments and expressed the need to hire officers immediately 
as the hiring and training process takes a year.   Aaron Wilmarth, President, Police Protective 
Association, expressed further concern related to an urgent projected staff shortage that will affect 
community safety and noted the City of Casper recently approved a new officer pay scale that will 
result in competition for maintaining and recruiting officers.  Ms. Lowe spoke in opposition of the 
proposed cap on employees as it prevents growth and urged trust in directors to fund  positions 
based on their budgets.  Ms. Moore provided information on budgeted adjustments that were made 
in or der to fill new needed positions.   Amy Allen, City Engineer,  requested clarification on  
proposed budget amendments relating to a right-of-way infrastructure improvement program and 
a fund for studies to evaluate prospective 6 th Penny projects.  Mr. Shanor  advised they are 
generalized to give discretion to Ms. Allen to administer and when proposals are submitted for the 
right-of-way infrastructure program,  a match must be provided.   Ms. Nemecek advised 
miscellaneous concrete funds are available in the 1% budget  line item.  Comments were made 
related to the proposed fund for studies to evaluate prospective 6 th Penny projects being a new 
concept, and the importance of a committee to review the projects.   Ms. Lockman  requested 
detailed dollar amounts related to the proposed freezing of positions in order to balance the budget.  
Comments were made related to meeting the proposed $206,934 .00 in savings with various 
funding mechanisms including reserves, miscellaneous concrete funds and funding designated for 
The Hole property . Discussion continued and included:  DDA proposed funding, remaining FY 
2018-2019 budgeted funds that can potentially be rolled over, Youth Alternatives vacant position 
and how it relates to grant requ irements, savings utilized to fund DDA and one -time Animal 
Shelter improvements and alternative mechanisms to fund one-time improvements.   
  
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:12 p.m.   Further 
discussion continued and included: Support and opposition of the proposed amendments and 
possibility of further amendments.  Mayor Orr expressed concern relating to how the proposed 
amendment may send a message to candidates for employment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mayor 
Orr voting “no”.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by  applying $90,000.00 from line item 1% sales 
tax 2019 -2022 towards a right -of-way infrastructure improvement program, leaving remai ning 
language (#2), adding “full-time” to #10, 3rd line and by adjusting #12 by subtracting $90,000.00 
from $206,934.00 which is $116,934.00, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Ms. Nemecek commented on 
the proposed right -of-way infrastructure improvement program and how it relates to City code 
requirements stating curb, gutter and sidewalk must be maintained by adjacent property owners.  
Chief Kozak advised he will delay a  Captain promotion that would result in other promotions 
resulting in a $70,000.00 savings and requested the hiring freeze be removed from police officers.  
Comments were made related to the proposed right -of-way infrastructure program and how it 
relates to City code provis ions and  required adjustments to the amendment can be made 
accordingly. Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. L aybourn voting “no”. It was noted the  minimum revenue guarantee 
(MRG) for air service wasn’t included in the budget, with Ms. Lockman confirming the exclusion 
of the revenue. Mr. Case moved to amend by moving $650,000.00 from reserves to the MRG line 
item for air service, seconded by Mr. Shanor. Friendly amendment was offered by Dr. Rinne and 
accepted by Mr. Case and Mr. Shanor, as the maker and second to the motion to amend, to add 
“up to” before $650,000.  Ms. Lockman provided information on unassigned and unrestricted 
general fund reserves amounts and explained $250,000 has been spent to date and doesn’t include 
the current quarter billings. Upon inquiry, Ms. McCoy-Fakelman advised a resolution rescinding 
the original resolution  outlining funding for The Hole would be needed to utilize the funding 
differently. Main motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. L aybourn voting “no”.  Motion as amended four times carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 5.6.2 
PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Unified Development Code, changing the 
zoning classification from CB  Community Business and County A -1 Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to Harmony Valley Planned Unit Development for portions of the S1/2 of Section 7 
and the S1/2 of the NE1/4 of Section 7, Township 13 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., City 
of Cheyenne Laramie County, Wyoming (as described in Exhibit A and generally located northeast 
of the intersection of West College Drive and Parsley Boulevard. Kelly Hafner, CivilWorx, agent 
for the applicant, provided information on the reques t stating a PUD is desirable as aspects of 
different zone types can be added into one document.  Mr. Hafner advised there are concerns 
relating to placement of required landscaping (trees) involving underground utilities and the 
applicant is proposing 5’ attached sidewalks instead of 6’ detached sidewalks.  He explained they 
can provide cash-in-lieu instead of required landscaping and could place trees in open space areas 
or on lots and proposed l ower volume residential roads  involving an interval curb and gutter 
section.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading  with Planning Commission 
recommendations # 3 & 4 to be renumbered as # 1 & 2 and updating Exhibit B prior to 3rd reading 
  
on June 10, 2019, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Comments were made related to street landscaping 
requirements and concern for proposed attached sidewalks relating to pedestrian safety. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the exception of  Mr. 
Luna, Mayor Orr and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.

 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown 
Development Authority .  Astrid, DDA and Plains Hotel, spoke in favor of the MOU .  Vicki 
Duggar, DDA Director, spoke in support of the MOU that has framework she believes is beneficial 
to all parties.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
by substitute dated 05-24-19 #1 which changes the deadline, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne 
advised rationale for change in date is to provide enough time to obtain information requested 
which will be available in October.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute #2 which includes the additions 
of Sections 6 and 7 and i ncludes the date amendment approved in substitute #1.  Ms. McCoy -
Fakelman advised Section 6 addresses landscaping services and Section 7 incorporates comments 
based on maintenance responsibilities in the downtown district. Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend Section 5 (a) 
relating to sales tax to baseline year Jan 1- 2019 – Dec. 31 2019, and Section 5 (b) by adding “by 
June 30, 2019”, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present .  Discussion topics included: Support for  the MOU, possible future 
revisions, DDA Board information, concerns regarding meeting financial reporting requirements 
and the importance of providing financial reports, financial reports reviewed at monthly meetings 
and City Council lia ison involvement.  Ms. Dugger explained she was not aware of financial 
reporting requirements and has set a work session with an attorney to review state and city 
requirements.  Mr. White advised he was also not aware of the requirement as City Council liaison 
and will now be forwarding the reports to colleagues. Ms. McCoy-Fakelman advised she believes 
the code requires a report to be forwarded to the full governing body. Inquiry was made related to 
receiving previously requested financial reports for the past 18 months that incurred a fee.  It was 
noted comments were not germane to the topic.  Main motion as twice amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#7002)

 Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the June 13, 2019 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Summer Convention Business 
Meeting to be held in Sheridan, Wyoming .   Mr. White moved to designate Dr. Rinne as Offical 
Voting Delegate and Mr. Laybourn as Alternate Delegate, seconded by Mr. Roybal.     Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
 Core Development Incentivization Program Fiscal Year 2019 Quarterly Report pursuant 
to City Code 15.08.040. Charles Bloom, Planning & Development Director, provided information 
regarding the program and advised the program expires June 30th of this year.  Mr. Bloom reported 
at this time staff is not recommending the program continue as it was utilized in a reactive response 
rather than as an incentive to develop.  He further explained  applicants would request a refund 
after their development was complete instead of prior to, and reported development is happening 
downtown whether  the program exists or not.   Ms. Lockman advised this program was not 
accounted for in proposed FY 2020 budget.  Mr. White moved to acknowledge receipt  of the 
report, seconded by Mr. Shan or.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.  
 
Re-appointments of Allan Cunningham and James Murphy to the Board of Public Utilities.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  V oting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present  with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote 
(mayoral appointments). 
 
     
Appointments of Marsha Dial and Charity Henning to the Mayor’s Council for People with 
Disabilities.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (mayoral appointments). 
  
Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held June 10, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming for a proposed sale of undeveloped 
public property described as a portion, consisting of approximately 1.46 acres, of Lot 2, Block 1, 
Pat Griffin Park, containing 5.5 acres, more or less, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, 
with an appraised value of $111,000.00, to Moreland Properties, LLD.    
 
Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held June 10, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming for proposed budgets for the City 
of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2020.      
 
Under other business: 
 
 Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, advised the property referenced in 
agenda item #29 (e) is located along Missile Drive and is currently vacant. 
 
  
 Mayor Orr provided clarification on the drainage study for the Thomas Heights area.  Upon 
inquiry, Amy Allen, City Engineer, reported she was directed by the Mayor to have the study 
conducted and advised the purpose of the study is to evaluate potential options for drainage issues 
in the Thomas Heights area and to provide information th at would assist the City in revising the 
current code requirements to help with  eliminating future issues involving drainage with new 
developments.  Comments were made regarding a previous failed attempt to address the drainage 
issues associated with the Thomas Heights area relating to City and County involvement.   
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:31 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-06-10 | CLIP_ID: 712 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 712

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 10, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal, Dicky 
Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Ken Esquibel and Mike Luna.  Also present: Kris Jones, City 
Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Case, and pursuant to support received two 
other members, Agenda Item #20 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda .  Upon request by 
Mr. Case, and pursuant to support received by two other members , Agenda Item #2 1 (b) was 
removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda 
items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 28, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from  Special Meeting of  the Governing Body on June 5, 2019 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Dr. Rinne voting “no”. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a proposed sale of undeveloped public property 
described as a portion, consisting of approximately 1.46 acres, of Lot 2, Block 1, Pat Griffin Park, 
containing 5.5 acres, more or less, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, with an 
appraised value of $111,000.00, to Moreland Properties, LLD.  Brendan Ames, Chief Economic 
Development Officer, provided a staff report on the property noting the economic importance of 
developing the land for the west corridor of the City. He advised the property is currently vacant 
and blighted , the land will be used as a large car dealership  and the interested purchaser 
understands the importance of the Crow Creek Revival (CCR) Project near the property.  Concern 
was expressed related to how the sale and development of the property may impact the CCR 
Project.  Mr. Ames explained there isn’t a site plan or total configuration of the proposed business 
yet, and the potential owner can collaborate with the CCR Committee.  Jeff Geyer, Laramie County 
Conservation District, presented a map of the CCR Project, provided information on Committee 
  
composition which is made of agencies and members of the public and advised the committee has 
been developing a master plan and implementation for 2020. Mr. Geyer advised the  CCR 
Committee is in support of the land acquisition and it’s private/public partnership  and is looking 
forward to  working with adjacent Crow Creek prope rty owner s.  The y requested the City to 
maintain ownership of the 20’ swath of land paralleling the Crow Creek floodway (1,300 linear 
sq. ft.) on top of the floodway terrace to be used as a Greenway trail for the following reasons: 
Greenway vision for the area has been in the masterplan for the area since 1992, ideal flat 
topography and location for a trail, customer exposure for the new proposed business, great trail 
overview as opposed to placement in the floodway corridor and the suggested placement would 
be less likely to sustain damage from peak storm runoff events . Mr. Geyer explained the p roject 
requires land outside of floodway to be included in the excavated area  and requested to maintain 
public access for parking (enough for a school bus to maneuver) and foot and bike traffic as it is 
important for the project. He noted the CCR Committee w ould like to be able to provide input 
during the early planning process.  Comments were made related to timing of the CCR Project and 
when it was brought before City Council, development of the land  and the importance of Crow 
Creek revitalization and development of the land.  Nancy L oomis, project leader for  the 
Community Wildlife Habitat Project for the W est Edge district, provide d information on the 
project, goals and project areas .  She explained 4 City properties have been certified  through the 
National Wildlife Federation as wildlife habitats  and the property being discussed isn’t included 
in the Community Wildlife Habitat Project area but has direct impact downstream and requested 
the developer to work with Laramie County Conservation District and the CCR Committee. Tom 
Annear, spoke in support of the project and suggested coordination between involved parties.   
Brent Lathrop, South East Wyoming Director for the Nature Conservancy, commented on livable 
cities, spoke in support of the CCR Project and requested a deliberate property agreement and sale.  
Further comments were made related to Pat Griffin Park history and Mr. Griffin’s intent to develop 
the land, donating property purchase price dollars to Greenway path construction  and the 
importance of the CCR Project.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing.  
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing  for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and 
Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2020.  There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utili ties.   
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal. Board of Public Utilities  (BOPU) City Council representative Mr. 
Laybourn provided information on the proposed increases  and explained  BOPU is  diligently 
looking at future resource needs as the community grows.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#4272) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
  
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2019 and ending June 30, 2020.  Molly 
Bennett, Cheyenne Public Employees Association ( CPEA), requested removing the cap on 
employees and provided information on the importance of not capping the number of full -time 
employees stating it is essential to continue to provide services as the City grows.  Ms. Bennett 
requested support of the governing body to allow Department Directors the ability to efficiently 
determine how to staff their departments  to provide department and p ublic service functions.  
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, confirmed an email sent during the week of June 3 rd correctly 
reflected the current number of employees at that time was 562.  Mayor Orr advised the City is 
obligated to hold military employee full t ime positions while on deployments.  Darrin Hass, 
Human Resources Director, reported an additional full -time employee has been hired increasing 
the employee number to 563(including deployed full-time employees) and advised currently there 
are 12 open positions . Ms. Lockman explained 5 of the open positions are funded through the 
General Fund and the others are f rom other funding sources and th e number does not include 3 
proposed new positions for FY 2020.  JJ  Chen, expressed appreciation to  City employees and 
spoke in support of the Downtown Development Authority (DDA).  Reporting for Committee of 
the Whole, Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Mr. 
Shanor moved to amend by substitute dated 6/10/19, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Shanor advised 
the amendment incorporates footnotes as recommended at the 06-05-19 Committee of the Whole 
meeting into line items and provides for clarification of intended footnotes.  Mayor Orr clarified 
the proposed substitute dated 06/10/19 does not include a previously approved hiring freeze and 
further clarified proposed footnotes.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by add ing Footnote #3 that adds  
$100,000.00 to reserves from FY 2019 reversions, seconded by Mr. Cook.   Motion to amend 
carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Mr. White moved to amend 
Footnote #2 to change  the employee cap number to 580.1, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. White 
explained this is an effort to give staff a little more flexibility for adjusting department staffing 
levels.   Motion to amend carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
Mr. Laybourn m oved to amend  the Economic/Community Development line  item by moving 
$30,000.00 from the DDA to the Police Department budget for the Downtown Operation Change 
Patrol Program, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr . Laybourn spoke  in support of and provided 
information on the program.   Captain Nathan Buseck, Police Department, provided information 
on the previous success and focus of the Downtown Operation Change Patrol Program which 
included providing the homeless/transient popula tion with tools to improve their current living 
situations.   Captain Buseck explained the program was previously funded through grants which 
are no longer available, noting the program is not currently budgeted for in FY 2020, and reported 
when there is a police officer presence downtown, there is less crime in the area.  Vicki Duggar, 
DDA Director, advised upon inquiry, the proposed DDA funding from the City will require further 
DDA Board action due to a proposed reduction in the funding total.  Comments were made on the 
importance of the downtown patrol program and the proposed budget of the DDA including staff 
salaries.  Captain Buseck provided information on the graffiti abatement program and budget line 
item upon inquiry . Ms. Lockman provided  further information on graffiti  abatement program 
funding line item and available balance.  Comments were made  in support of continued funding 
for the graffiti abatement program.  Motion to amend failed.   Voting “no” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “yes”.  Mr. Laybourn moved 
to amend by moving $10,344 .00 from the General Fund reserves revenue category and adding it 
  
to the Police Department budget for the Downtown Operation Change Patrol Program, seconded 
by Mr. Cook. Captain Buseck requested caution regarding removing all funding for the graffiti 
abatement program as it is still needed for that purpose .  Upon inquiry, Ms. Lockman provided 
information on funds expended from the graffiti abatement line item over the last 3 years.  Motion 
to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as 
amended four times carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   The 
following is a summary of the proposed budget , including the aforementioned amendments : 
General Fund, $ 56,073,809; Weed and Pest, $ 515,683; Youth Alternatives Grants, $ 574,762; 
Development Impact Fees, $ 151,200; Recreation Programs, $1 ,337,339; Community 
Development Block Grant, $ 506,474; Law Enforcement Grants, $ 171,032; Transportation 
Planning, $870,097; FTA Transit Grant, $ 2,432,324; Juvenile Justice, $ 80,409; Special Friends, 
$76,363; Specific Purpose Option Tax, $325,158; One Percent Sales Tax 2019 -22, $10,899,926; 
Youth Activities/Ropes, $18,000; Golf Facilities, $201,000; Fleet Maintenance, $4,322,249; Solid 
Waste Management, $ 18,015,303; Civic Center, $ 2,348,249; Ice & Events Center, $ 685,503; 
Perpetual Care, $2,000; Total City, $ 99,606,880; Water Enterprise, $ 30,186,375; Sewer 
Enterprise, $17,348,745; Total Appropriation, $147,142,000. (#4273)

 Contract Modification # 1 to Professi onal Services Agreement #6785 for the Christensen 
Railroad Overpass Corridor Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) .  Wes Bay, Deputy City 
Engineer, provided an overview of what the modification entails. Mr. Bay, upon inquiry, provided 
information relating t o the history of  the project design, primarily involving the design specific 
modifications to the intersection of Christensen and Pershing Blvd. He explained existing grades 
in the location of the previously proposed roundabout intersection are not conducive for 
construction of a roundabout, noting a 4-lane signalized intersection is currently be considered for 
that location.  Mr. Bay further reported on national design standards involving various intersection 
designs.  Council President Mr. Case provided information relating to the intersection design plans 
  
dating back to February 2014 which includes  ordinances approved by the governing body and 
associated design mapping relating to the project, noting public input for design modifications 
should be considered.  Upon inquiry, Logan Sharpe, Assistant City Attorney, confirmed the design 
map approved in relation to  Ordinance #3279 (03-25-1996) is the mos t recent map approved by 
the governing body.  Mr. Sharpe further reported Ordinance #3279 does not require construction 
of a roundabout intersection and advised he will provide additional information relating to ability 
to construct a skewed intersection upon further review.   Comments were made relating to original 
project design, public input, project expectations and concerns with recent accidents within and 
around skewed intersections.  Mr. Bay provided additional information involving design options  
and evaluations and research, noting the proposed intersection would function much like the 
intersection of Pershing Blvd. and US Highway  30, noting additional review of a signalized 
skewed intersection is being considered.  Mr. Bay explained use of force ac count funding, noting 
the force account is to cover  issues that may arise to keep the project on track .  Discussion and 
comments were made regarding intersection design, public input on design modifications, force 
account funding and construction managemen t.   Mr. Bay confirmed  the project design is not 
complete and a public forum involving proposed changes  can be arranged.  Mr. White moved in 
an amount not to exceed $710,983.20, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Bay, upon inquiry, advised 
the proposed modificat ion funding would come from current project funding (2012 6 th Penny).  
Mayor Orr advised the project is currently below the $15 million budget.  Mr. Case moved to 
postpone to 06-24-19, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Sharpe confirmed he will provide the governing 
body with clarification on questions pertaining to previously approved project related ordinances, 
associated design mapping and traffic study report.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 24, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor  and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.    Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 10 , 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.    
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne for fiscal year 2019 -2020.  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by M r. 
Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4275) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
sworn officers of the Police Department for fiscal year 2019-2020.  Mr. White moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by increasing the 
number of sworn officers from 111 to 112, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion  to amend carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Main motion  as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4276) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4277) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (“UDC”), replacing UDC Section 5.8.1 in response to the rapidly 
changing technology and regulatory environment for wireless communication facilities, including 
  
the deployment of new, smaller wireless communication facilities .  Charles Bloom, Planning & 
Development Director, provided a staff report and advised staff supports approval of the ordinance 
which establishes standards and guidelines.  M r. Bloom explained the purpose is to allow for 
deployment small cell wireless communication facilities and advised  in January 2019, a Federal 
Communications Commission ( FCC) ruling was implemented that City’s cannot  prohibit the 
deployment of small cell wireless communication facilities.  Mr. Bloom advised staff has worked 
with multiple providers to come to an agreement on the proposed ordinance, noting  an item of 
concern is current restrictions on light poll height and indicated a proposed substitute will be forth 
coming to address the issue.   He further noted  a request has been made for an exemption for 
microcell facilities located on existing wirelines  and reviewed the required shock clock review 
period.  Bob Duchen, River Oaks Communications, explained the proposed ordinance provides 
for best practices not only for Wyoming, but around the country as well.  Mr. Duchen reported the 
FCC order implemented strict compliance relating to review periods and limits amount of revenue 
communities can receive for facilities placed within public rights -of-way.  He provided 
information on a recent cost study which indicates the revenue is below actual costs for 
communities.  Mr. Duchen advised the FCC order is currently in the Unites States Court of Appeals 
9th Circuit.  Erik Rasmussen, Charter Communications, advised there will  be a few 
recommendations of change for the ordinance to come before the Public Services Committee  on 
July 2, 2019.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 2.04.160(D), Readings-Committee 
Reports-Amendments, of Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee.  
(Sponsor – Mr. Case)  Upon inquiry, Mr. Case, sponsor of  the ordinance, explained the item 
involves City sponsorships and other special revenue s, therefore is being referred to the Finance 
Committee.

 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held July 8, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance 
of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for a portion of Tract 119, 
all of Tract 120 and portions of Tracts 135 and 136, and adjacent vacated rights-of-way, Sunnyside 
Addition, Fourth Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of the intersection of Fillmore 
Avenue and Charles Street).   
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held July 8, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance 
of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for the three hundred 
(300’) wide right-of-way of U.S. Highway No. 30, situated in a portion of Sections 26, 27, and 34, 
Township 14N, Range 66W of the 6th PM, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between Cleveland 
Avenue and Hayes Avenue).    
 
Announcement was made that two Calcuttas, benefitting Cheyenne area charities, will be 
held as follows:  Thursday, July 18, 2019 beginning at 7:00 p.m. at the Redwood Lounge, 2105 
East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the 2019 Cheyenne Frontier Days 
Wild Horse Races, and Saturd ay, July 27, 2019 beginning at 5:30 p.m. in the Cowboy Bistro 
located beside the Rodeo Committee Cabin, Frontier Park, Cheyenne, Wyoming (10% of proceeds 
to bona fide charities). 
  
 Under other business:  
 
 Jamie Davidson requested consideration on rehabilitation efforts within her neighborhood 
(Fremont and 11th Streets) due to drainage issues causing personal property damage and reported 
flooding at her residence.  Ms. Davidson advised it is her understanding from contact with the City 
Engineer’s office, flooding remediation hasn’t occurred  due to lack of  funding.  Anthony 
Gonzales, area resident, provided comments regarding  on-going flooding and property damage 
concerns.  Upon inquiry, Amy Allen, City Engineer, provided information relating to a recent visit 
to the area of concern, lack of available funding and efforts to minimize citywide drainage issues 
resulting from previous developments.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, explained upon 
inquiry that drainage projects are eligible for 5 th Penny funding, however, the amount that is 
allowed is usually used for grant funding matches.   Ms. Allen explained current UDC and City 
Code requirements are being reviewed for potential amendments to address drainage issues and 
  
support of enforcement.  Discussion included citywide drainage complaints /issues, lack of 
available funding, current development standards, storm water drainage, and FEMA funding and 
drainage master plans.  
 
 Upon inquiry, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided information relating to 
recent crime data published relating to big box stores, specifically both local Walmart stores, 
noting shoplifting raises the City’s overall crime theft rates.   
 
 Donn Edmunds requested restricting heavy truck traffic on Pershing Blvd. relating to the 
construction on the Christensen Road Overpass Project.   
 
 Kris Jones, City Clerk, requested, due to timeliness involving  an application for a resort 
liquor license and per Mayor Orr’s permission, the following item be announced: Announcement 
of a Public Hearing to be held on July 8, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a resort liquor license application filed with the  City Clerk’s 
Office for Driftwood Special Servicing, LLC d/b/a Red Lion Hotel Cheyenne & Conference 
Center, 2014 Fox Farm Rd., Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:59 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
         
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  July 2, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-07-08 | CLIP_ID: 716 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 716

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 8, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, 
Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Dr. Mark Rinne.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Esquibel, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Luna and Mr. White, agenda item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Shanor clarified 
he did not vote as he has a conflict with agenda item #16 .  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor who did not vote.  
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 24, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.    
 
Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing  to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for a portion of Tract 119, all of Tract 
120 and portions of Tracts 135 and 136, and adjacent vacated rights-of-way, Sunnyside Addition, 
Fourth Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of the intersection of Fillmore Avenue 
and Charles Street).  Tawn Hillenbrand, Planning and Development, advised the annexation 
request was submitted by the landowner,  provided information on the property  and reviewed 
requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes. She advised 
the annexation ordinance has been delayed and will appear on the council agenda at a later date.  
Upon inquiry, Ms. Hillenbrand advised the land is currently developed with a church and this will 
allow development of the proposed annexation portion of the property.  She further noted  she is 
unaware of a future plan to develop the property, advising, however, upon annexation, it could be 
developed.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing  to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for the three hund red foot (300’) wide 
right-of-way of U.S. Highway No. 30, situated in a portion of Sections 26, 27, and 34, Township 
  
14N, Range 66W of the 6 th PM, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between Cleveland Avenue 
and Hayes Avenue ).  Tawn Hillenbrand, Planning and Development, advised the annexation 
request was submitted by the landowner,  provided information on the property  and reviewed 
requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Ms. 
Hillenbrand advised agend a item #14 is the affiliated annexation ordinance.   Charles Bloom , 
Planning and Development Director, explained upon inquiry , the purpose of the annexation is to 
clarify city and county boundaries, which will place law enforcement under one entity.  He further 
reported the need to be able to provide more efficient traffic control and clarification of emergency 
response.  Ms. Hillenbrand explained there are two different applicants for annexations.  Amy 
Allen, City Engineer, advised meetings were held with multiple agencies relating to traffic control 
along this stretch of Highway 30 and the annexation would allow City officers to patrol the area, 
noting the area is a safety concern.  Ms. Allen clarified the annexation is for WYDOT Right-of-
Way only.  Comment was made related to concern for future development in the area adding 
additional traffic.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearin g having 
been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing for a resort liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Driftwood Special Servicing LLC d/b/a Red Lion Hotel Cheyenne & 
Conference Center, 204 Fox Farm Rd., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Billie Addleman, Hirst Applegate, 
LLP, advised he was present on behalf of the applicant to answer any questions.   There being no 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (“UDC”), replacing UDC Section 5.8.1 in response to the rapidly 
changing technology and regulatory environment for wireless communication facilities, including 
the deployment of new, smaller wireless communication facilities .  Charles Bloom, Planning & 
Development Director, advised a substitute ordinance dated 7/8/19 was emailed to members of the 
governing body which contains final revisions and includes amendments made at the 7/2/19 Public 
Services Committee meeting. Mr. Bloom reported Charter and Verizon are in agreement with the 
proposed revisions.  He explained one of the changes is reducing standalone towers  heights from 
50 ft. to 40 ft.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute dated 
7/8/19, seconded by Mr. Roybal.   Kris Jones , City Clerk,  confirmed the proposed substitute 
contains all amendments made at Public Services Committee.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Shanor explained that in his opinion, this 
is one of the most important economic development and quality of life ordinances that has been 
approved and it is possible due to collaboration of entities.  Mr. Shanor further advised an industry 
partner raised issues that weren’t addressed in the proposed substitute, however, he believes those 
can potentially be addressed in the future.  Main motion  as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (It was noticed on August 22, 2019 that the main motion 
to approve on third and final reading was inadvertently not included in the ori ginal minutes file.  
The minutes have been updated to reflect the addition of the motion) (#4278)

[CA] RESOLUTION – Repealing Resolution No. 5835, as amended, and Resolution No. 5942,

commonly referred to as the Historic Hynds Building .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6001) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Naming the future Municipal Courthouse in honor of Judge Joseph M. 
Carey.  Stephanie Lowe, on behalf of the Historic Preservation Board, advised she was available 
to answer questions.  Comments were made related to Carey Avenue and Carey Jr. High that were 
previously named after Judge Carey and acknowledgement was given to other  individuals that 
have made contributions to the community.  Mr. White, a co-sponsor of the resolution, advised the 
building at this location has always been known as the Carey Building and that Judge Carey was 
a prominent community figure and had held the offices of Municipal Justice of the Peace, governor 
and mayor. Ms. Lowe provided information on the Carey Block and the first and only building at 
that location. She provided further information on the naming of Carey Jr. High and explained the 
Historic Preservation Board  felt it was important to continue with the  Carey name at the new 
building since the prior building has been demolished.  Mr. Shanor moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mr. Esquibel voting “no”. (#6002)
