 Consideration of Bid #E-30-19 for furnishing three (3) new 2019 -2020 twin-screw diesel 
powered cab & chassis, for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund).  Vicki 
Nemecek, Public Works  Director, reported she sent an email to the governing body  with the 
recommendation to accept the bid from Floyd’s Truck Center and the email included minutes from 
2 prior meetings  containing additional bid process information from May and July 2018 .  Ms. 
Nemecek also advised of an additional email sent to the governing body in May 2018 which 
highlighted her thoughts relating to the bidding process that included information on lowest price, 
bid specifications, contact representative  and necessity to be consistent . Upon inquiry, Ms. 
Nemecek clarified that although Peterbilt of Wyoming provided a higher trade-in amount, Floyd’s 
Truck Center still maintained the lowest bid. Upon inquiry, TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager, 
advised he did not receive an explanation from Tri -State Truck & Equipment regarding not 
providing trade-in amounts.  Ms. Nemecek requested the bid be amended to retain trade-in #5790 
for the purpose of recycling, adjusting the total bid price to $391,959.00 . James Balzer, Peterbilt 
of Wyoming, advised he was available for questions and provided  information on their trade -in 
amounts and additional information relating to their bid.  Mr. White moved to accept the bid from 
Floyd’s Truck Center in an amount not to exceed $357,989.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. White 
moved to amend by  removing trade-in #5790 increasing the total amount not to exceed 
$391,959.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Ms. Nemecek explained the Public Works Department would 
like to keep the trade-in tractor due to recent changes involving the City’s ability to outsource 
  
recycling services.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  Comments were made on the bid process and relying on staff for the best interest of the 
city.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Code have been met.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Esquibel. Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
[CA] Request to expand seating area for consumption relating to retail liquor license held by Alf 
in Wyoming Enterprises, LLC d/b/a Alf’s Pub & Package Liquors, 1622 E. 19 th St., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Frank Cole voiced support for naming the new Municipal Courthouse after Judge Joseph 
M. Carey . Mr. Cole  provided history relating to the Carey family including community 
contributions and properties owned within Cheyenne and Laramie County  including sections of 
Eastridge, the Carey Dairy and land south of Dell Range Blvd.    
 
Mr. Laybourn voiced concern relating to aspects of agenda item #17 (Resolution repealing 
Resolution No. 5835, as amended, and Resolution No. 5942, both pertaining to the expedient 
redevelopment of the building located at 1602 Capitol Avenu e, commonly referred to as the 
Historic Hynds Building) and provided history regarding failed attempts to redevelop the Historic 
Hynds Building and the “ Hole” property.  He requested information relating to the H2 Proposal 
  
which was to have produced a fina l report.  Mr. Laybourn suggested prioritizing development of 
the Hynds and “Hole” properties.    
 
Mr. Laybourn provided copies and information related to the Strategic Downtown Action 
Plan and the “Deploy Bucket Brigade” program downtown during Cheyenne Frontier Days (CFD).  
He advised the  report was approved by Downtown Development Authority ( DDA) and is a 
working document.  Mr. Laybourn voiced concerns relating to the CFD parade route involving a 
series of issues with curb and gutte r in areas where people sit including exposed rebar.  He  
suggested correcting these issues and covering windows of vacant downtown properties.     
 
 Mr. Case stated he appreciates comments made relating to funds budgeted for the Historic 
Hynds Building (agenda item #17) and explained the purpose of recouping and redirecting the 
funding was to make up for a budget oversite relating to a previous air se rvice funding 
commitment.   
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:55 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
         
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  July 17, 2019

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 22, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case,  Bryan Cook , Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor  and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 8, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Case. Dr. 
Rinne declared a conflict and recused himself from discussion and voting.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne who didn’t vote. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.04.160(D), Readings-Committee 
Reports-Amendments, of Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Reporting for Finance Committee, 
Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. C ase.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4279) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6, 1.1.7, and 2.4.1 of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 6.5.4.c to modify allowances for 
Electronic Message Centers .  Reporting for  Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING –  Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development - 
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, amending 
Ordinance No. 2975 and Ordinance No. 3297 by modi fying certain standards of the adopted 
district, Planned Unit Development (PUD), for all of Block 596 and the westerly 40 feet of the 
vacated right of way of House Avenue between East 7th and 8th Streets, Original City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of  I-180 between 7th and 8th Streets ).  Dr. Rinne moved to 
  
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating all portions of non -vacated: alley, adjoining 
half-street right of way of 12th Street, and adjoining half -street right of way of Holmes Avenue; 
of Block 168, Interior Heights, 2nd Filing; and all of Center Drive west of the west line of Harmony 
Center 2nd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to 
West College Drive, west of Walterscheid Boulevard) .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 1.4.4 and 5.1.4 of the UDC to clarify 
definitions for entertainment and recreation land uses; to create a new entertainment and recreation 
– Other Land Use; and to allow these uses in various zoning districts as administrative, conditio nal 
or principal permitted uses .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by adding conditional uses to AG, AR, RR zones for 
Entertainment Out Door, Entertainment Venue, Entertainment Amusement and Recreation Other, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the three 
hundred foot (300’) wide right-of-way of U.S. Highway No. 30, situated in a portion of Sections 
26, 27 and 34, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, 
(located between Cleveland Avenue and Hayes Avenue).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, 
within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – 
referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Adopting a Comprehensive Open Data Policy as is Practical .  Vicki 
Nemecek, Director of Public Works, advised the resolution and policy is a product of the StratOp 
action initiative plan Collect and Utilize Data.  She provided information on open data, affiliated 
software and confidentiality of sensitive records. Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City 
Attorney, explained this is  separate from the Wyoming Public Records Act and further advised 
that currently certain data  must be requested from City departments.  She reported that as  this 
initiative progresses, citizens will be able to  search d ata from a n online portal, noting certain  
records requests will still need to be requested through a public records request.  Dr. Rinne moved 
to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Exhibit A by adding a Section 7 to 
read as follows: “Section 7: Budget (a) Council’s approval of this policy does not include funding.  
Specific projects and budgeting will be discussed at a later date”, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion 
to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6003) 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION –  Whereby the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne authorizes a 
special Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District Election to be held October 16, 2019 
as provided by Wyo. Stat. §15-9-217 (5) which, upon affirmative majority vote of the Qualified 
Voters within the District, will authorize a Special Assessment of Twenty (20) Mills upon real 
property within the District, excluding real property used exclusively for residential purposes, for 
a period of four (4) years.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6004) 
 
[CA]  RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Harmony Valley, First Filing, a plat of a portion of the S1/2 of Section 7 and the SE1/4 NE1/4 of 
Section 7, T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, (located north of and 
adjacent to W. College Drive, west of Walterscheid Boulevard) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 Land Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate 
Management LLC, for the purchase of real property described as a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, P.A.S. 
Subdivision, City of Che yenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for the appraised value of 
$329,630.00 for economic development purposes (General Fund Reserves) . Alessandra McCoy 
Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, advised staff has put together presentations for council to discuss 
some of the issues including access, Ice and Events Center signage and a proposed substitute.  She 
discussed the issue relating to direct access outside of a blanket access agreement that was granted 
in 2007 when the Ice and Events Center was purchased, explaining the City is requesting an access 
easement that will better serve the Ice and Events Center and that access was never granted to the 
City as it was between  the H itching Post and Ice and Events Center. She provided further 
information on the Ice and Events Center sign that has not changed as the City doesn’t own the 
property where the sign is located which causes UDC issues that can be addressed with an access 
agreement.  Ms. McCoy Fakelman advised the proposed substitute will clear up clerical errors and 
contains edits including new legal description , moving of the I ce and Events Center sign and 
clarifies other concerns that arose  during negotiations.  Brendan Ames, Chief Economic 
Development Officer, explained the agreement addresses ownership, parking, proper lighting and 
improvements and will provide flexibility for the City to ensure future success of the property.   
Mr. Ames provided information on a future replat of the property, the property being eligible for 
the Brownfields Revolving Loan Fund, land adjacent to the property and economic impact related 
to developing the la nd.  He reviewed similar land purchase scenarios in the state  of which all 
include vacated property purchased by City entities and advised letters of intent from interested 
developers for the property have been submitted.  Upon inquiry, Bruce Trembath, Chief Build ing 
Official, advised he has been in contact with various contractors related to efforts for stabilizing 
the property including parameter fencing, securing buildings and capping drainage for buildings 5 
and 6 and provided cost estimates for each.  He explained upon further inquiry fencing will be 
secured with posts driven into the ground.  Teresa Moore, Director of Community Recreation and 
Events, provided information on developing the land, plans for the area including the Missile Drive 
Corridor plan, need to create a Cheyenne gateway, importance of the project, private investments 
for similar projects, possibility of Greenway connectivity, history of adjacent land and its current 
use and existing concerns.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, reported staff 
has talked to the EPA regarding the Brownfields Revolving Loan and provided info on matching 
grant funds and eligibility for the revolving loan fund to include all properties on the site.  Upon 
  
inquiry, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, explained a separate fund would be required to be used 
only for purchasing property.  Mr. Bloom, upon  inquiry, advised an EPA grant is different than  
the revolving loan fund and only states, communities and tribes are eligible to apply for the grants 
and provided information on grant deadlines. Casey Palma, Steil Surveying, agent for the owner, 
reviewed project aspects including price of property, the importance of access, need to update 
previous access agreements, current signage (which is currently illegal) and negotiation with 
owner for a lien which will go bac k to the City when property sells for  updates. He advised the 
owner has applied to the revolving loan fund to abate the front 2 buildings.  Upon inquiry regarding 
the Hitching Post sign, Mr. Palma explained he has encouraged the owner to be amendable and 
provided different options for the sign.  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$329,630.00, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Dr. 
Rinne.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Lockman provided information on available reserves which includes the second floor 
of the new Municipal Court building and provided information on the Capital Improvement plan.  
Further discussion continued and includ ed: appraised value of Parcel A, costs affiliated with 
remediations needed for the property, paying more than appraised value of the property and 
importance of remediating the property.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend section 7  (c) by replacing 
existing language with: “Seller agrees to contribute $750,000 toward any remediation of Parcel A. 
Seller agrees to a $750,000 promis sory note  and mortgage in favor of the Buyer which will be 
placed on Parcel B currently owned by Seller and not conveyed to Buyer by this agreement. Said 
promissory note and mortgage to be effective immediately upon Seller’s sale of Parcel B.  Other 
terms and conditions of promissory note and mo rtgage to be negotiated with Seller by the City 
Attorney’s office”, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Palma  commented on the amendment and the 
current owner’s financial position. Further comments were made related to abatement estimates 
that were obtained through an RFP process by the current owner .  Upon inquiry, Ms. McCoy 
Fakelman advised the City has not gone out for RFP’s or RFQ’s.  Mr. Palma explained attachment 
C-1 is part of the owner’s 2018 RFP.  Discussion continued. Topics included: costs affiliated with 
remediation and demolition, grant opportunities and letters of intent.  Mr. Shanor raised a point of 
order relating to a question posed regarding the owner’s interest in moving forward with the sale 
agreement after the amendment.  Mr. Palma advised remediations could possibly be paid for by 
alternative mechanisms.  Mayor Orr and Ms. McCoy Fakelman provided information on the sale 
agreement and condemnation proceedings that are currently stayed before the Building Code 
Board of Appeals pending the decision on this land purchase tonight.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mayor Orr an d Mr. 
Laybourn voting “no”.  Ms. Lockman explained upon inquiry the dollar amount available  in 
reserves includes purchase price of th is property.  Further comments included: s upport for the 
amendment, continued negotiations with Seller and appreciation for everyone who has been 
involved with the agreement.  Main motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#7032) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne  and Preserve Historic

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for

[CA] Human Services Agreement with United Way of Laramie County to provide services to

[CA] Human Services Agreement with United Way of Laramie County to provide services to

[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Ch eyenne Housing Authority to provide

[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Safe Harbor to provide a safe and secure

[CA] Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Airport Board  for land

[CA] Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Airport Board for land

 [CA] Consideration to purchase signaling infrastructure power line relocations from the Union

[CA] Request to expand seating area for consumption relating to retail liquor license held by Alf 
in Wyoming Enterprises, LLC d/b/a Alf’s Pub & Package Liquors, 1622 E. 19 th St., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Motion (Per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Appointment of Ashley Miller as Assistant City Treasurer (Board of Public Utilities) for 
the City of Cheyenne .  Mayor Orr congr atulated Randy Hays on his retirement  and provided 
history on the Assistant City Treasurer appointment.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by

members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral

Appointment of Breck Collier to the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority Board.  
Mr. Shanor moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-08-12 | CLIP_ID: 720 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 720

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 12, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn,  Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Ken Esquibel.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Orr and Kris Jones, City Clerk, explained 
that due to a clerical error, agenda items #18 and #19 were inadvertently referred to the Finance 
Committee and are instead refer red to the Public Services Committee.  Mr. Luna moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.    
 
 [CA] Minutes from  Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 22, 2019 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved  to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.   
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6, 1.1.7, and 2.4 .1 of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 6.5.4.c to modify allowances for 
Electronic Message Centers .  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motio n carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”. (#4280) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING –  Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development - 
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, amending 
Ordinance No. 2975 and Ordinance No. 3297 by modifying certain standards of the adopted 
district, Planned Unit Deve lopment (PUD), for all of Block 596 and the westerly 40 feet of the 
vacated right of way of House Avenue between East 7th and 8th Streets, Original City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of I -180 between 7th and 8th Streets ).  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#4281)  
 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Vacating all portions of non -vacated: alley, adjoining 
half-street right of way of 12th Street,  and adjoining half-street right of way of Holmes Avenue; 
of Block 168, Interior Heights, 2nd Filing; and all of Center Drive west of the west line of Harmony 
Center 2nd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to 
West College Drive, west of Walterscheid Boulevard).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4282) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to S ections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 1.4.4 and 5.1.4 of the UDC to clarify 
definitions for entertainment and recreation land uses; to create a new entertainment and recreation 
– Other Land Use; and to allow these uses in various zoning districts as administrative, conditional 
or principal permitted uses.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4283) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the three 
hundred foot (300’) wide right-of-way of U.S. Highway No. 30, situated in a portion of Sections 
26, 27 and 34, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, 
(located between Cleveland Avenue and Hayes Avenue).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4284)   
 
           ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, 
within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Amy Spieker, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, provided statistical information relating to the 
harmful effects of smoking and supported smoke free indoor environments in public places.  Olivia 
Hardy, Jr. High student, reported increased usage of vaping devices with underage students and 
voiced support for banning use in public places.  Responding  to Mayor Orr, Ms. Hardy reported 
she would support changing the age a person is legally able to purchase vaping or smoking 
products to 21 years of age.  R egina Hughes and Amanda Rieke, Straight Line Vapors, voiced 
support for stricter enforcement relating  to underage smoking/vaping violators and provided 
information relating to the positives of vaping which assist smokers to quit  and air quality. They 
further commented on the positive effects vaping has had on regular cigarette smokers supporting 
them to q uit.  B rittney Wardle, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, reported studies show that 
vaping devices emit chemicals into the air exposing non -users which has been associated to 
potential increases in asthma diagnosis.  David A llen, Tobacco Compliance Officer , Cheyenne 
Police Department, advised the department supports the proposed amendment.  J ason Mintzer, 
American Cancer Society Director, reported studies show vaping devices can include up to 31 
ingredients that produce/contain chemicals (some potential cancer causing that are emitted into the 
air whether or not they contain nicotine).  Mr. Mintzer requested consideration to include in the 
proposed amendment: 1-Strengthen the definition of Electronic Smoking Device, 2-Include hotels 
and motels, 3-Include private residences which are licensed child care, adult day care, pre-schools, 
or health facilities and stated he has started a p etition supporting those changes.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Mintzer confirmed he would support enforcement when the d evice is being “carried” and 
lighted or heated.  Mike Morrison, American Heart Association, voiced support and commented 
  
on exposure to secondhand inhalation.   M arguerite Herman voiced support, reported increased 
use among high school users, and reiterated the addictiveness of nicotine.  Krysta Reeve, Straight 
Line Vapors, provided information regarding requiring product labeling and voiced opposition to 
the amendment.  Cheyenne Kise voiced opposition stating vaping products assisted her father in 
quitting nicotine products.  Baily Early, Jr. High student, advised that changing the age to 21 years 
would not necessarily keep youth from obtaining the devices as older friends and siblings purchase 
and provide them to minors.  Dr. Phyllis Sherrard, Cheyenne Reg ional Medical Center, provided 
information regarding the prevention needs assessment for Laramie County and advised teen/youth 
vaping device use has increased by approximately 32%.  Patty Reisland, Mayor’s Council for 
People with D isabilities, voiced suppo rt for the ordinance.  Nick Vaugh , P oder Academy 
Secondary S chool Principle, reported nicotine addiction involving vaping devices is on the 
increase among youth at the school.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case. Mr. White moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Case.  Comment was made 
related to medical necessity of prescription inhalers.  Motion to amend carried.   Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr voting 
“no”.   Further comments were made relating to expressing caution in banning legal substances 
and infringing on rights and lack of substantiated health risks regarding harmful effects of 
electronic device usage.  Mr. White explained the purposed of the amendment is to include vaping 
devices, noting they were not in existence when the original smoking ban was approved.  
Discussion topics included:  Ordinance language, enforcement, secondhand risk health reduction 
and nicotine addiction.  Main motion a s amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the south 
one hundred fifty feet (150’) of Tract 119, all of Tract 120, and those portions of Tracts 135 and 
136 north of the right-of-way for U.S. Hwy. No. 30, and all of vacated Cheyenne Street (60’), and 
the west ½ (25’) of vacated Fillmore Ave., adjacent to said tracts,  Sunnyside Addition Fourth 
Filing, situate in a portion of the SE1/4 SE1/4 of Section 27, Township 14N, Range 66W of the 6th

           Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held August 26, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council 
Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a proposed trade of real property 
owned by the  City of Cheyenne, described as Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1st 
Filing, and Lot 1, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 2 nd Filing, Laramie  County, Wyoming, 
Consisting of 419.57 acres and having an appraised value of $1,004,000.00, to Mariah, LLC, for 
real property owned by Mariah, LLC, described as all of Sections 11 and 14, lying north of the 
Union Pacific Railroad right-of-way, Township 12 North, Range 70 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie 
County, Wyoming, consisting of 543.76 acres and having an appraised value of $1,060,000.00.   
 
           Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held August 26, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council 
Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a proposed sale of undeveloped public 
property described as a 1.46 acre portion of Lot 1, Block 1, Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 1 st 
Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, with an appraised value of $293,0 00.00 to 
John J. Koehler.   
 
Under other business: 
 
Joe Dougherty commented on release of retainage to Reiman Corp. involving the Civic 
Center Commons Project with respect to cracking concrete and requested an update regarding a 
time frame for when the contractor would be repairing or replacing deficient concrete.   Mayor Orr 
advised the contractor was also selected for the Christensen Overpass Project which they are 
currently under construction.  Amy Allen, City Engineer, reported there is a punch list of items  
they will be returning to repair and advised they are currently working on coordinating that time 
frame.  Mr. Dougherty encouraged the governing body to follow up on completion of the 
contractor’s punch list.   
 
Mr. Laybourn  voiced concern relating to failing/cracking concrete throughout the city 
involving city streets and the Wyoming Department of Transportation in relation to state highways 
running through and around the city, use of tax payer funding for these projects and enc ouraged 
members of the governing body to set standards for the future to hold contractors accountable.    
 
 Mr. Case inquired as to the process for street sweeping.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works 
Director, advised currently the department is understaffed and reported difficulty in recruiting 
employees due to limitations on the amount of wages that can be offered.  Ms. Nemecek provided 
a summary of the current process of street sweeping relating to commercial and residential areas, 
noting members of the public are welcome to make requests for services that would be placed on 
a waiting list.        
 
  
There bein g no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:16 p.m.        
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-08-26 | CLIP_ID: 721 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 721

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 26, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook,  Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Ken Esquibel. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Luna and Mr. Case, agenda item #12 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Shanor and Mr. Case, agenda item #22 (c) 
was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded 
by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.     
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 12, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr.  White.   
Upon inquiry, Amy Allen, City Engineer provided information available on vouchers payable to 
the following: Big Al’s Towing (towing of vehicles during chip seal project) and Inberg Miller 
Engineers (monitoring of retainage wall at Civic Center Commons).  Responding to an inquiry 
related to the voucher payable to Hansen, Allen & Luce, Inc., Ms. Allen explained there was a 
contractual increase for professional services for the College and Storey (Summit) Drainage Study, 
as project work is ongoing.  She noted the contract amount will  remain under $35,000.00.   Dr. 
Rinne moved to divide the question to allow for separate action on the voucher payable to Hansen, 
Allen & Luce , Inc. in the amount of $ 875.27 for College and Storey  (Summit) Drainage 
Investigation, seconded by Mr. Case. It was noted the motion is not debatable.  Motion to divide 
the question carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception 
of Mayor Orr and Mr. Laybourn voting “no” .  Motion to approve remaining vouchers carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr voting 
“no”.  A point of order was raised related to the need to discuss and vote on the voucher payable 
to Hansen, Allen & Luce, Inc. separately.  Comments were made related to a resolution that failed 
calling for a drainage study that relates to the voucher.  Ms. Allen explained the work has been 
agreed to and a contract has been signed. The work has been and continues to be conducted and 
the contract was completed within the procurement process  requirements.  Mayor Orr further 
clarified the contract amount with Hansen, Allen & Luce, Inc. is under $35,000.00.   Upon inquiry, 
Michael O’Donnell, interim City Attorney, advised the City doesn’t have authority to no t pay 
  
invoices for work performed.  A contract holder has the right to go to court to collect money due 
as a judgement against the City.  Discussion continued on the voucher approval process  and the 
failed resolution calling for a drainage study related to the voucher.  Motion to approve the voucher 
payable to Hansen, Allen & Luce, Inc.  failed.  Voting “yes” – Mayor Orr, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. 
Roybal and Mr. White. Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a proposed trade of real property owned by the  City 
of Cheyenne, described as Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1 st Filing, and Lot 1, 
Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 2 nd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming, Consisting of 419.57 
acres and having an appraised value of $1,004,000.00, to Mariah, LLC, for real property owned 
by Mariah, LLC, described as all of Sections 11 and 14, lying north of the Union Pacific Railroad 
right-of-way, Township 12 North, Range 70 West of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, 
consisting of 543.76 acres and having an appraised value of $1,060,000.00.   Vicki Nemecek, 
Public Works Director, provided information on the proposed property trade and explained the 
proposal is for a  monetary equal land trade.  Eric Dalton spoke in favor of the proposed trade, 
commented on the Belvoir Ranch wind energy lease and expressed interest in some lease funding 
being dedicated to recreational use.   There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing.   
 
Mayor Orr opened a p ublic Hearing for a proposed sale of undeveloped public property 
described as a 1.46 acre portion of Lot 1, Block 1, Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 1st Filing, City 
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, with an appraised value of $293,000.00 to John J. 
Koehler.  Teresa Moore, Community Recreation and Events Director, provided information on the 
proposed sale and property, stating it is for economic development purposes.  Ms. Moore explained 
the property buyer intends to purchase the motel that is south of the property and that the Wasatch 
mini railroad line that was previously on the parcel  has been removed.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
           ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, 
within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
Jason Mincer, Wyoming Government Relations Director, American Cancer Society Cancer Action 
Network, provided a hand out containing requested ordinance amendments and spoke in support 
of the addition of vaping devices to the smoking ban.  He requested clarifying the definition of e-
cigarettes which could help eliminate the possibility of business owners having to determine if e-
cigarettes contain nicotine.  Mr. Mincer presented a vaping device and explained how the device 
functions.  Mr. Mincer requested consideration of the inclusion of hotels and motels, in-home care 
centers and private offices as smoke free businesses.  He would like to see in-home care facilities 
smoke free at all times, even when customers aren’t present.   He explained there is a p etition 
containing close to 300 signatures in support of the ordinance changes.  Upon inquiry related to 
how the City would require in-home care facilities to remain smoke free 24 hours a day, Mr. 
Mincer advised if a residence is utilized as any type of business (with the exception of a tobacco 
shop), he believes they  should remain smoke free at all times.  It was noted the approved 
amendment from the August 12, 2019 City Council meeting includes banning vaping devices 
whether or not they contain nicotine.  Mr. Mincer explained his national office provided proposed 
language as the amendment may not be clear.  Speaking in support of the ordinance were: Amy 
  
Spieker, Director of Community Health, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center ; Olivia Hardy; Nick 
Vaughn, Secondary Principal, Poder Academy; Marguerite Herman; Josh Hannes, Wyoming 
Hospital Association; Mike Mor es, American Heart Association and Patti Reisland, Mayor’s 
Council for People with Disabilities.  Topics discussed included :  priorities identified in a 
community health assessment including healthy behaviors related to vaping; vape use among 
teenagers; student vaping related disciplinary measures in area schools; public space smoking ban 
restrictions; vape use reaching epidemic levels ; public health risk  associated with vaping;  
importance of including nicotine free vape products in smoking ban; logical extension of existing 
smoking ban ordinance and statistics related to individuals with greatest risk of second hand vaping 
effects.  Mayor Orr asked for everyone in attendance in support of the ordinance to rise.  Astrid, 
Plains Hotel, requested clarification related to enforcing vape free hotel/motel rooms.   Joe 
Chenchar, Assistant City Attorney, advised ordinance language that has been proposed would not 
make it illegal to use a vap ing device in a hotel or motel room.  Reporting for Public Services 
Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Shanor voting “no”. (#4285) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the south 
one hundred fifty feet (150’) of Tract 119, all of  Tract 120, and those portions of Tracts 135 and 
136 north of the right-of-way for U.S. Hwy. No. 30, and all of vacated Cheyenne Street (60’), and 
the west ½ (25’) of vacated Fillmore Ave., adjacent to said tracts, Sunnyside Addition Fourth 
Filing, situate in a portion of the SE1/4 SE1/4 of Section 27, Township 14N, Range 66W of the 6th 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming.  (located south of the intersection of Fillmore Avenue and 
Charles Street.  Dr. Rinne reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services 
Committee.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading , seconded by Mr.  Shanor.  Jack 
Studley, agent representing owner, advised the owner would like to withdraw the annexatio n 
application.  Mr. Studley explained for the purpose of this annexation request he is representing 
his private practice and provided information on the request to withdraw the application.  Motion 
failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 - Planned Development 
Regulating Plan, Section 2.4.1 - Zoning Text Amendment, and Section 5.6.2 - Planned Unit 
Development amendment, amending the Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD) 
Regulating Plan for 2,349.54 a cre Sweetgrass PUD, City of Cheyenne, Laramie, County, 
Wyoming (located south of West College Drive and east of Avenue C) .  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated 
8/19/2019, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Dr. Rinne explained the substitute changes the location of the 
event center and raises the height restriction and would allow for increasing the building heights.  
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Submitting to the qualified property owners of real 
property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authorit y District of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, excluding real property used exclusively for residential  purposes, at a 
Special Election on Wednesday, October 16, 2019, the following Question:  “Shall a Special 
  
Assessment of twenty (20) mills be levied upon the assessed  value of real property within the 
boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District, excluding real property 
used exclusively for residential purposes, for a period of four (4) years pursuant to the provisions 
of Wyo. Stat. §15 -9-217(b) f or the purpose of (1) administrative costs; (2) landscaping or 
maintenance of public areas; (3) planning or management of development or improvement 
activities; (4) promotion of public events; (5) activities in support of business recruitment and 
development?”  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comments were made regarding the importance of taxations of 
businesses in the downtown area, evaluation of the DDA program including how funds are spent 
and how financial records are accessed  in compliance with City code requirements.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the fiscal year 2019 budget of the City of Cheyen ne

 Preliminary Plat for Center Point Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat of Lots 1 through 7, Block 
1, inclusive Center Point Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the 
intersection of Homestead Avenue and Eda Place).  William Alexander, Eda Place resident, spoke 
in opposition of th e preliminary plat and on behalf of several neighbors , voicing their concerns 
related to the possibility of increased traffic, access by Homestead, proposed increased dwelling  
units in the area, existing covenants and compromised safety. Gayleen Wyant, Homestead Avenue 
resident, spoke in opposition of the preliminary plat and commented on existing concerns in the 
area.  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services, agent for the applicant, provided information on the 
following: issues concerning roads are being ad dressed, areas of land  that are covered by 
covenants, review of emergency neighborhood access, areas of concern to be reviewed during final 
plat process, applicable codes having been met for the preliminary plat and the potential of a right-
of-way connecti on being provided to vacant land.  Dr. Rinne reported there was no 
recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Dr. Rinne moved to acknowledge 
receipt with staff conditions 1(a) through (g), seconded by Mr . Shanor. Comments were made 
related to a compaction test with a need to withstand a 75,000 lb. fire apparatus, concern regarding 
fill material that was used during an annexation and how it relates to site stability and issues with 
geotechnical reports for subdivisions.  Amy Allen, City Engineer, explained in conjunction with 
the StratOp process, geotechnical requirements for major projects are currently being reviewed.  
She explained the City is also looking at requiring a third-party engineer to do compaction testing 
for streets and the need to implement these requirements during the design and development phases 
of projects.  She provided information on subdivision issues that could have been addresse d with 
geotechnical reports and advised the City can require a geotechnical report of the property prior to 
development.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Palma advised the intent is to provide  a geotechnical report of 
the subdivision and explained the p roject engineer has recommended fill be removed and 
recompacted.  Ms. Allen advised she would like to be able to add recommendations during the 
final plat process related to geotechnical report requirements. Mr. Shanor moved to amend by 
adding staff condition #1 (h) that reads “Applicant will provide a geotechnical compaction report 
sufficient to satisfy the City Engineer and City Fire Plans Examiner Fire Inspector”, seconded by 
Dr. Rinne.  Motion to amend carried .  Vo ting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Roybal voting “no”.  Comments were made related to UDC requirements 
and the unlikelihood of vacant land in a neighborhood remaining vacant.  Upon inquiry,  Charles 
Bloom, Planning and Development Director,  provided UDC information stating this proposal 
meets UDC and IFC requirements.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mayor Orr, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. 
Luna and Mr. Roybal. 
   
  
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Cheyenne Youth Alternatives, and 
Laramie County for Juvenile Probation Services (revenue to the City) .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried.  (#7057) 
  
 Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Airport Golf Club, Inc.  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#7058) 
 
Grant of access easement between the City of Cheyenne and Joseph M. Dyson for a 60 foot 
wide access easement across City -owned land in the NE1/4NE1/4 of Section 23, Township 13 
North, Range 68 West, 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Jack Studley, City Surveyor, provided information on the location of the 
property, upon inquiry, stating a fragment of the property is part of the Belvoir Ranch and is north 
of Otto Rd.  He explained there is an easement on an existing improved gravel road that has been 
in use for many years by Mr. Dyson and area energy companies and that Mr. Dyson has granted 
access agreements to the City for other purposes. Mr. Studley noted the Dyson’s are anticipating 
selling their home and the title company asked for the easement.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present. (Clerk’s note – agenda item discussed and voted on 
out of sequence) (#7059) 
 
 Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 7:55 p.m. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and OpenCities, Inc., San 
Francisco, California, for the implementation of a Web -Enabled Content Management System 
which includes website re -design, development, maintenance and hosting of the Ci ty’s Website 
(General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $81,900.00).  
(#7060) 
 
[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and 
Yolanda Ronnau for Crime Victim Assistance Services.  (Wyoming Division of Victim Services 
Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in the base salary amount of $10,800.00 plus 
eligible benefits). (#7061) 
 
[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and 
Brenda Johnson for Crime Victim Assistance Services.  (Wyoming Division of Victim Services 
Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in the base salary amount of $35,859.12 plus 
eligible benefits). (#7062) 
 
[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and 
Jessica A. Fritz for Crime Victim Assistance Services.  (Wyoming Division of Victim Services 
Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in the base salary amount of $31,200.00 plus 
eligible benefits). (#7063) 
 
[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and 
Leslie Burch for Crime Victim Assistance Services.   (Wyoming Division of Victim Services 
  
Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in the base salary amount of $48,878.52 p lus 
eligible benefits). (#7064) 
 
 Co-Sponsorship of a Grant Agreement along with Laramie County and the Cheyenne 
Regional Airport Board with the Federal Aviation Administration for Federal Aviation Grant for 
the Planning and Engineering Designing work to r econstruct the main runway at the Airport  – 
referred to Finance Committee.  Mr. White advised due to timing, the item was reviewed under 
other items for the agenda at the August 19, 2019 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, 
provisions of Title 2 of City C ode had been met.  Mr. White moved to approve , seconded by Mr. 
Luna.  Tim Barth, Director of Aviation, Cheyenne Regional Airport, explained funding involved 
with the project noting there is no financial commitment from the City and only airport funds will 
be utilized.  Mr. Barth advised the Cheyenne Airport Board is a joint powers board and it is a 
requirement that the governing body approve the grant agreement as it is a public improvement.  
Mr. White advised staff received a letter from the FAA outlining requirements to receive the grant 
and provided the grant deadline date .  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#7065) 
 
[CA]  Real Estate Purchase Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and John J. Koehler, for 
the sale of undeveloped public property described as a 1.46 acre portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 
Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 1 st Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for the 
appraised value of $293,000.00 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA]  Agreement for the trade of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne, described as Lots 
1 and 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1st Filing, and Lot 1, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivisio n, 
2nd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming, consisting of 419.57 acres and having an appraised value 
of $1,004,000.00, to Mariah, LLC, for real property owned by Mariah, LLC, described as all of 
Sections 11 and 14, lying north of the Union Pacif ic Railroad right-of-way, Township 12 North, 
Range 70 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, consisting of 543.76 acres and having 
an appraised value of $1,060.000.00 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract No. 6839 between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corporation, Cheyenne, for final construction of the Civic Center Commons Project.  (1% Sales 
Tax 2019-2022).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $86,545.52). 
(#6839) 
 
[CA]  Consideration of Bid #S -22-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and American 
Contracting of Wyoming, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Cheyenne Regional Airport Fountain 
Partial Restoration Project. (Certified Local Government Grant, Wyoming Cultural Trust Fund 
Grant, Historic Preservation Board, and Leadership Cheyenne Class of 2016) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (accepted the bid from American Contracting of Wyoming, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming in an amount not to exceed $45,400.00). (#7066) 
 
[CA]  Report of Community Facility Fees Fiscal Year 2018-2019.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. 
 
  
 Appointment of Michael R. O’Donnell as City Attorney for the City of Cheyenne .  Mr. 
Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr . White. Appreciation was expressed to Mr. O’Donnell 
and to current City Attorney staff for their efforts during the absence of a City Attorney.   Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor 
Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointment). 
 
 Mr. Shanor moved to a cknowledge receipt of  revenues as presented, seconded by Mr.  
Luna. Appreciation was expressed to City staff on implementation of revenues  as a reoccurring 
agenda item pursuant to new ordinance requirements. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present. 
 
 Announcement of a Public Hearing was made to be held September 9, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. 
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a transfer of ownership 
for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for AB Cheyenne, WY, 
LLC d/b/a/ Applebee’s Neighborhood Grill & Bar, 1401 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming.    
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Labor Day holiday on Monday, September 2, 
2019, the regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance 
Committee will meet Tuesday, September 3, 2019 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee 
will meet Wednesday, September 4, 2019 at Noon. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Vince Bo diford, The Cheyenne Post,  provided information on  the weekly community 
newspaper that has recently launched in Cheyenne.   
 
Ed Melko spoke  in opposition of $250,000.00 that was recently allocated for repairing 
potholes and the importance of the bid process for this type of project. 
 
Mr. Laybourn commented on the recent  Edge Fest event that was held at  Civic Center 
Commons, voiced concern regarding the former airport terminal building related to its current 
condition and possible future use and expressed appreciation to Police Chief Kozak regarding his 
response to the Community Conversation Report.  
 
Mr. Luna voiced concern regarding observed pothole work near his residence and the 
allocation of funds for pothole repair.  
 
There being no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:04 p.m.  
 
       
 
 
 
  
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
         
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  September 6, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-09-09 | CLIP_ID: 723 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 723

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 9, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn,  Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent: Bryan Cook.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. White and Mr. Case, agenda item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to sup port received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal, agenda items 
#23 (a) and #23 (b) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve  
remaining items, seconded by Mr . White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 26, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr.  Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
Mayor Orr opened a p ublic hearing for a  transfer of ownership for a retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for AB Cheyenne, WY, LLC d/b/a/ Applebee’s 
Neighborhood Grill & Bar, 1401 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming   
(license currently held by RMH Franchise Corporation d/b/a Applebee’s Neighborhood Grill & 
Bar).  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr 
closed the hearing and referred the agenda item to the Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 - Planned Development 
Regulating Plan, Section 2.4.1 - Zoning Text Amendment, and Section 5.6.2 - Planned Unit 
Development amendment, amending the Sweetgrass Pla nned Unit Development (PUD) 
Regulating Plan for 2,349.54 acre Sweetgrass PUD, City of Cheyenne, Laramie, County, 
Wyoming (located south of West College Drive and east of Avenue C) .  Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr .  
Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4286) 
  
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Submitting to the qualified property owners of real 
property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, excluding real property used exclusively for residential  purposes, at a 
Special Ele ction on Wednesday, October 16, 2019, the following Question:  “Shall a Special 
Assessment of twenty (20) mills be levied upon the assessed value of real property within the 
boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District, excluding real  property 
used exclusively for residential purposes, for a period of four (4) years pursuant to the provisions 
of Wyo. Stat. §15 -9-217(b) for the purpose of (1) administrative costs; (2) landscaping or 
maintenance of public areas; (3) planning or managemen t of development or improvement 
activities; (4) promotion of public events; (5) activities in support of business recruitment and 
development?”  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr .  Luna.  Comments were made regarding the importance of the levy 
taxation of businesses in the downtown area, evaluation of the DDA program, including how funds 
are spent, and how financial records are accessed in compliance with City code requirements.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4287) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis 
to NR-3 Neighborhood Residential – High Density for Lots 1 -9, Block 1, North Gat e Village, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and 
Prairie Hills Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Vacating all of that 15’ alley right of way within Block 
13, Arp Addition, Fifth Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, (located north of 
and adjacent to Leisher Road, east of Cribbon Avenue) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the fiscal year 2019 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16 -4-112 & 16 -4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6009) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the fiscal year 2020 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16 -4-112 & 16 -4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6010) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne to complete the Avenu es Greenway 
Connector Trail between Evans and Airport Parkway and supplement available WYDOT TAP 
Grants with 6th Penny Tax Receipts (6th Penny Greenway Funds).  Mr. White moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. White moved to postpone to September 23, 2019, seconded by Mr.   
Luna.  Mr. White, upon inquiry, advised staff requested a postponement allowing for additional 
review prior to approval.  Motion to postpone carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.   
 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Accepting a grant of $112,500.00 from the Recreation Mill Board for 
the renovation of the Jaycee Park Tennis Courts.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6011) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Accepting a Hazard Mitigation grant from the Wyoming Office of 
Homeland Security, in the amount of $191,430.85 for Dry Creek Drainage Planning.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6012) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction 
Account Grant application to the State Loan and Investment B oard on behalf of the Governing 
Body of the City of Cheyenne for the purpose of replacing Fire Station #5.  Mr. Shanor declared a 
conflict of interest due to his current employ ment, vacated Chambers and abstained from 
discussion and casting a vote.  Mr. Wh ite moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  During 
discussion a point of order was raised by Dr. Rinne regarding discussion participation by the Chair 
on agenda items .  Mayor Orr relinquished duties of the Chair to Council President Case for the 
purpose of participating in debate.  Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, advised the 
grant application deadline is September 19, 2019.  Topics discussed included:  Unidentified grant 
match funding, funding options, ability to not accept grant award, community growth needs 
relating to capital facilities, public health and safety considerations, grant funding application 
annual cycle and concerns relating to the current con ditions of Fire Stations #5 and #3.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – Mayor Orr, Mr. Case, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, and Mr. White.  
Voting “no” – Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Shanor did not cast a vote.  (#6013) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an  exception to the “201 Agreement” for the owner of the 
property located at 7615 E. Pershing Blvd. to allow repair or replacement of an on -site septic 
system located within Laramie County – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. 
Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Sweetgrass 2nd Filing Subdivision for a portion of the north half of the northeast quarter of Section 
16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th Principal Meridian and including a Replat of all 
of Block 3 (Lots 1, 2 and 3 inclusive) of the Sweetgrass 1st Filing Subdivision, City of Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of W. College Drive, east of Avenue C) – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing a street name change from “Snyder Avenue” to “South 
Snyder Avenue” for the portion of a street located between Interstate 80 and West Allison Road, 
City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Water 
Development Commission for grant funding for the North City Zone Improvements Project in the 
amount of $8,040,000.00 and authorizing the Mayor, the City Clerk and the Director of the Board 
of Public Utilities to execute the application and all related documents – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Stat e Loan 
and Investment Board for a loan from the Wyoming Drinking Water State Revolving Fund for an 
amount not to exceed $3,960,000.00 for the North City Zone Improvements Project and 
authorizing the Mayor, the City Clerk and the Director of the Board of Pu blic Utilities to execute 
the application and all related documents – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
 Real Estate Purchase Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and John J. Koehler, for 
the sale of undeveloped public property described as a 1.46 acre portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 
Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 1 st Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for the 
appraised value of $293,000.00 .  Piyush “Pete” Patel voiced concerns relating to the disposal of 
City owned pro perty and provided history of his previous purchase offer made on the adjacent 
Hitching Post property.  Mr. White moved to approve  in an amount not to exceed $293,000.00,  
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mayor Orr upon inquiry, confirmed the property was appraised and that 
the property is vacant with the exception of a Wasatch Railroad Caboose which will be relocated.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#7070) 
 
 Agreement for the trade of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne, described as Lots 
1 and 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1st Filing, and Lot 1, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 
2nd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming, consisting of 419.57 acres an d having an appraised value 
of $1,004,000.00, to Mariah, LLC, for real property owned by Mariah, LLC, described as all of 
Sections 11 and 14, lying north of the Union Pacific Railroad right -of-way, Township 12 North, 
Range 70 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, consisting of 543.76 acres and having 
an appraised value of $1,060 ,000.00.  During discussion , Mr. Shanor raised  a point of order 
relating to State Statute §15-1-108 (a) (i), City Code Section 2.04.080 and Robert’s Rules of Order 
with regard to relinquishing duties of the Chair.  Mike O’Donnell, City Attorney, upon inquiry, 
provided clarification regarding the Mayor’s responsibilities in reference to presiding o ver 
meetings of the governing body.  Following Mr. O’Donnell’s explanation, Council President Case 
relinquished duties of the Chair to Mayor Orr.   Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr.   
Roybal.  Dr. Rinne  moved to amend by substitute dated September 9, 2019, seconded by Mr.   
Roybal.  Discussion topics included:  Belv oir Ranch access concerns including difficulty in 
obtaining UPRR permission for public access and extended staff time frame of efforts in 
attempting to acquire and maintain public access.  Alessandra McCoy -Fakelman, Deputy City 
Attorney, upon inquiry, confirmed staff ’s on -going efforts and dedication in obtaining public 
access and advised anticipated updates to the Belvoir Ranch Master Plan are currently being 
considered.  Ms. McCoy-Fakelman reported documents relating to staff’s efforts are available for 
review by members of the governing body.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present.  (#7071) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) and the City 
of Cheyenne, Transit Program, for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Grant to assist in 
providing a local match for operation funds for the Transit Program for Fiscal Year 2020 (revenue 
to the City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreement between Timothy and Kimberly Cilek and the City of 
Cheyenne, for the purchase of real property described as Lot 1, Block 10, Leisher Black Addition, 
2nd Filing, more commonly known as 101 Co lumbus Drive, in the City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming, for the appraised value of $209,000.00  (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Visionary Communications Inc. for 
wireless antenna tower space located at Fire Station No. 2, 514 W. Fox Farm Rd. , Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and the Cheyenne Public Safety Center, located at 415 W. 18 th Street, Che yenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the  City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization (MPO) and West Edge Collective, LLC, Cheyenne,  for Fiscal Year 2020 
MPO Public Engagement and Web Support Services (MPO Federal Funds) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Inberg -Miller 
Engineers, Cheyenne, for engineering services for design of the 2020 Pavement Management 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and the Wyoming 
Department of Transportation for D.U.I. Mobile Command Task Force enforcement and 
educational activities (Highway Safety Mobile Command Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Consideration of a Bid for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Simon Contractors, 
Cheyenne, for the 2019 Pothole Repair Project (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Publi c 
Services Committee.  James Johnson voiced concerns regarding his perception of a lack of 
communication between the Mayor and members of the governing body and advised he supports 
construction of a new Fire Station.  Joe Dougherty made multiple inquiries relating to the 
emergency procurement and bid process involving the proposed bid.  Dr. Rinne advised due to 
timing, the item was reviewed under other items for the agenda at the September 4, 2019 Public 
Services Committee meeting, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been met.  Dr. 
Rinne reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $249,999.25, seconded by Mr .  Esquibel.  
Mayor Orr and TJ Bart telbort, Purchasing Manager , provided information involving the 
purchasing process utilized relating to the proposed bid.  Eric Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of Staff, 
upon inquiry, provided additional information involving pothole and street management processes 
and advised of the current lack of available staffing and resources to keep up with the maintenance 
demands.  Mr. Fountain further reported funding for the project was previously budgeted for.  City 
Council members voiced various concerns involving the proposed bid , cost negotiation process 
and use of emergency procurement funding.  Mr. Case moved to “Lay the item on the Table ”, 
seconded by Mr. White.    Motion carried.  Voting “yes”- Mr. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. 
Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mayor Orr and Mr. Esquibel.   
 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -1-20 for furnishing eight (8) new four -door 2020 Model Utility 
(SUV) Police Interceptors, for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department  (2015-2018 & 
2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-2-20 for furnishing eight (8) Motorola APX8500 Mobile Radios 
for the City of Cheyenne,  Cheyenne Police Department (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-1-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Scotch Boy, 
Inc., Cheyenne, for the Irrigation Controllers and Equipment Upgrade Projec t (Parks 
Improvements, Maintenance, and R-O-W Operating Cost funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -5-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and STC 
Construction Co., Cheyenne, for the 2019 Wearing Course Project (201 5-2018 1% Sales Tax) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Joe Dougherty requested an update on the progress of the punch list repairs for the Civic  
Center Commons Project.   Wes Bay, Deputy City Engineer, upon inquiry, advised punch list 
repairs are currently underway.   
 
Donn Edmunds expressed opposition to agenda item #18 which was referred to the Public 
Services Committee involving granting an exception to the “201 Agreement” for property located 
at 7615 E. Pershing Blvd.    
 
James Johnson voiced concerns relating to his experience with the Downtown 
Development Authority (DDA) involving lack of accountability , communication and promotion 
of economic development.     
 
Mr. Laybourn voiced opposition of the motion made to “Lay on the Table” for agenda item 
#24(a), spoke to concerns involving overall roadway management by the City and complemented 
current efforts for repairs being made on Yellowstone Rd. and Central Avenue.   
 
Mike O’Donnell, City Attorney, upon inquiry, provided information involving the motion 
to “Lay on the Table” regarding agenda item #24(a) and advised he would be providing a more 
detailed response upon further review.   
  
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:22 p.m.  
 
       
 
 
 
  
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning

 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be

discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two

 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.
