ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating all of that 15’ alley right of way within Block 
13, Arp Addition, Fifth Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, (located north of 
and adjacent to Leisher Road, east of Cribbon Avenue) .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, within 
Title 2, Administration and Personnel, and Chapter 3.04, Fiscal Provisions Generally, within Title 
  
3, Revenue and Finance, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne to complete the Avenues Greenway 
Connector Trail between Evans and Airport Parkway and supplement available WYDOT TAP 
Grants with 6 th Penny Tax Receipts (6 th Penny Greenway Funds)  (Postponed from 9/9/2019).   
Reporting for Finance Committee,  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Luna 
moved to postpone to October 14 , 2019, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  It was noted City Council 
members received an email from the Planning and Development Department advising there is an 
impasse as the location isn’t viable at this time therefore, requesting the item be voted down.   
Motion to postpone failed. Voting “no” – all members of the governing body .  Upon inquiry, 
Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director, reported staff will evaluate different routes 
that can be utilized to connect Evans Avenue to Airport Parkway.  He further explained there are 
issues with using airport property and staff is looking at a possible agreement with Olivet Cemetery 
for utilization of their pathways using current infrastructure.  Comment was made that  moving 
from Pershing is preferable.  Motion failed. Voting “no” – all members of the governing body.   
 
RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the “201 Agreement” for the owner of the 
property located at 7615 E. Pershing Blvd. to allow repair or replacement of an on -site septic 
system located within Laramie County .  Dr. Rinne moved to  adopt with Staff Condition #1, 
seconded by Mr . Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#6014) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Sweetgrass 2nd Filing Subdivision for a portion of the north half of the northeast quarter of Section 
16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th Principal Meridian and including a Replat of all 
of Block 3 (Lots 1, 2 and 3 inclusive) of the Sweetgrass 1st Filing Subdivision, City of Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of W. College Drive, east of Avenue C) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6015) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing a street name change from “Snyder Avenue” to “South 
Snyder Avenue” for the portion of a street located between Interstate 80 and West Allison Road, 
City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6016) 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Water 
Development Commission for grant funding for the North City Zone Improvements Project in the 
amount of $8,040,000.00 and authorizing the Mayor, the City Clerk and the Director of the Board 
of Public Utilities to execute the application and all related documents .  M otion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6017) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming State Loan 
and Investment Board for a loan from the Wyoming Drinking Water State Revolving Fund for an 
amount not to exceed $3,960,000.00  for the North City Zone Improvements Project and 
authorizing the Mayor, the City Clerk and the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to execute 
the application and all related documents.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6018) 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for North Gate 
Village, 2nd Filing, a Replat of Lots 7-9, Block 1, North Gate Village, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and P rairie Hills Drive) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) and the City 
of Cheyenne, Transit Program, for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Grant to assist  in 
providing a local match for operation funds for the Transit Program for Fiscal Year 2020 (revenue 
to the City) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $112,290.00 
(revenue to the City)). (#7077) 
 
[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreeme nt between Timothy and Kimberly Cilek and the City of 
Cheyenne, for the purchase of real property described as Lot 1, Block 10, Leisher Black Addition, 
2nd Filing, more commonly known as 101 Columbus Drive, in the City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming, for the appraised value of $209,000.00  (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $209,000.00). (#7078) 
 
[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Visionary Communications Inc. for 
wireless antenna tower space located at Fire Station No. 2, 514 W. Fox Farm Rd. , Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and the Cheyenne Public Safety Center, located at 415 W. 18 th Street, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7079) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the  City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization (MPO) and West Edge Collective, LLC, Cheyenne,  for Fiscal Year 2020 
MPO Public Engagement and Web Support Services (MPO Federal Funds).  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $44,800.00). (#7080) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Inberg -Miller 
Engineers, Cheyenne, for engineering services for design of the 2020 Pavement Management 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$81,600.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by requesting a work session with 
the governing body and Engineering Department regarding the Pavement Management Plan prior 
to any engineering services being performed on this contract, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Comment 
was made in support of  a work session.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Luna explained th e proposed 
amendment would allow the agreement to be approved, however, a work session would be held 
prior to work being performed on the contract.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, further 
explained the idea is to approve the dollar amount of the agreement but hold off implementation 
until a work session has been held  and advised delaying implementation would not affect the 
agreement. Upon inquiry,  Kris Jones , City Clerk,  clarified the motion  on the floor.  General 
comments were made in support of a work session and concerns were raised regarding the normal 
protocol of requesting work sessions which are to be requested and approved through the Council 
President.  Ms. Jones further clarified the amendment motion and reported agenda items cannot be 
postponed for longer than  2-weeks or until the next regularly scheduled City Council meeting.  
Comments were made on the long-term management program and how it is aimed at developing 
a city-wide system for road management, support for work sessions and  alternative options for 
  
requesting work sessions.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. Laybourn voting “yes”.  Mr. Case moved to 
postpone to October 14, 2019, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mayor Orr,  Mr. Laybourn and  Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and the Wyoming 
Department of Transportation for D.U.I. Mobile Command Task Force enforcement and 
educational activities (Highway Safety Mobile Command Grant).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (amount not to exceed $55,500.00). (#7081) 
 
[CA] Grant A greement between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and the  Wyoming 
Department of Health, Aging Division , to provide support services to person s 60 years or older 
under the Title III-B Program Grant for Fiscal Year 2020 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7026 between the City of Cheyenne and Simon 
Contractors, Cheyenne, for the 2019/2020 Miscellaneous Street Repair Project (2015 -2018 1% 
Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #E -1-20 for furnishing eight (8) new four -door 2020 Model Utility 
(SUV) Police Interceptors, for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, (2015-2018 & 
2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) .  Mr.  Luna moved to accept the bid from Spradley Barr Motors, 
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $455,715.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Police 
Chief Brian Kozak, explained Ford no longer makes  sedans for police vehicles and they prefer 
SUV’s due to various equipment needs.  Mr. Luna moved to accept the substitute bid, dated 
September 12, 2019, from Spradley Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed 
$461,683.00, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Chief Kozak explained the amendment specifically requires 
a light bar with side visibility and confirmed Spradley was still the lowest bidder with this addition. 
Motion to accept substitute bid carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with 
the exception of Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”.   
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-2-20 for furnishing eight (8) Motorola APX8500 Mobile Radios 
for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid from Bearcom/Motorola, Evans, CO, in an amount not to 
exceed $48,882.96).  
 
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #S-1-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Scotch Boy, 
Inc., Cheyenne, for the Irrigation Controllers and Equipment Upgrade Project (Parks 
Improvements, Maintenance, and R-O-W Operating Cost funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (in an amount not to exceed $592,902.96). (#7082) 
 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -5-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and STC 
Construction Co., Cheyenne, for the 2019 Wearing Course Project (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax) .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $199,722.00). (#7083)  
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -4-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and  S & S 
Builders, LLC, Cheyenne, for the Greenway Construction - Powderhouse Segment Project (6th 
Penny Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase time clocks from Paycom, Oklahoma City, Oklahoma for the 
City’s payroll and timekeeping software program (Fleet Maintenance, Solid Waste and 1% Sales 
Tax Funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Transfer of ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s  
Office for AB Cheyenne, WY, LLC d/b/a/ Applebee’s Neighborhood Grill &  Bar, 1401 Dell  
Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by RMH Franchise Corporation d/b/a 
Applebee’s Neighborhood Grill & Bar).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Presentation of Revenues.  Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer , reported 2 
payments were received and recorded in the same month for the Rail Spur Loan and that typically 
only 1 payment is recorded in a month.  Mayor Orr and Ms. Lockman provided information 
relating to the Ticketfly Settlement revenue advising Ticketfly pay s the City for tickets they sell 
for shows at the Civic Center. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Mr. White expressed appreciation to members of City staff and the Police Department who 
both assisted with the recent Cheyenne Marathon.  He also congratulated Mark Ellison and Jason 
Hardy from the City’s Forestry Division on the Rooted in Cheyenne  Program and reported the  
500th tree was recently planted through the program, noting the program is in its 3rd year.   
 
Mr. Shanor moved to take from the table #24  (a) “Consideration of a Bid for a contract 
between the City of Cheyenne and Simon Contractors, Cheyenne, for the 2019 Pothole Repair 
Project.  (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax)” from the September 9, 2019 City Council agenda, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mr. Shanor 
moved to postpone the item until October 14, 2019 and as a condition of said postponement, staff 
is directed to ask the second bidder, Knife River, if they would like the opportunity to submit an 
amended bid under the $250,000.00 amount like Simon Con tractors was offered. Said amended 
bid shall be received by the City no later than 5:00 P.M. on September 30, 2019 and shared with 
all members of the City Council immediately upon receipt.  Staff shall evaluate the amended bid, 
if one is submitted, and a recommendation will be made to the Finance Committee on October 7, 
2019, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor explained he believes the bid process for this project 
has been fundamentally flawed and commented on emergency procurement policy requirements, 
lack of transparency and that only one of the two bidders were allowed the opportunity to amend 
their original bid.  He advised members of the governing body have r eceived additional 
information since the bid was tabled.  Mike O’Donnell, City Attorney, expressed concerns relating 
  
to the proposed conditional postponement, advised a motion to postpone cannot contain conditions 
and that the proposed bid from Simon Contractors was negotiated following the City accepting the 
bid proposal, reporting it is now a negotiated dollar amount.  Motion to postpone with conditions 
failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of  Mr. Luna, Mr. 
Roybal and Mr. Shanor voting “yes”.  Upon inquiry, Wes Bay, Deputy City Engineer, reviewed 
various pothole locations (excess of 200) that are recommended for repair and explained if any 
funding is left over, additional locations will be repaired.  Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Director 
of Public Works, advised she could compile a cost repair breakdown based on the location and 
condition of each pothole, noting  general cost to repair potholes varies.  Discussion continued 
related to declaration of the project as an emergency and concern of the contract cost including 
30% going towards mobilization and traffic management.  Mr. Bay provided an explanation of the 
3 types of repairs that have been presented.   Comments were made related to recent repairs done 
on Yellowstone Road using an improved repair process, support for the improved process an d 
project, the process of repairing potholes , project opposition due to  deviation from current bid 
processes and the importance of process integrity.  Main motion failed.  Voting “yes” - Mayor Orr, 
Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. 
Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.    
 
Mayor Orr provided information on Christmas ornaments available for purchase from the 
Historic Preservation Board and advised proceeds will go towards the restoration of the Airport 
Fountain and the Historic Preservation Board.     
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:47p.m.  
 
       
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
         
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  October 3, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-10-14 | CLIP_ID: 725 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 725

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 14, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case,  Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn,  Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent:  Bryan Cook.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.   Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 23, 2019.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 No action was taken on the vouchers as presented.   Upon inquiry, Michael O’Donnell, 
City Attorney, advised the approval process for vouchers is currently being considered through a 
city code amendment which is outlined in the affiliated agenda item #9  on the  agenda.  Mr. 
O’Donnell reported that voucher reports for the following City Council meetings:  September 23rd, 
October 14 th, October 28 th, and November 12 th, will be approved as to “accuracy only” at the 
November 12, 2019 meeting of the governing body.  Discussion included approval process and 
understanding that approval in the current format is inconsequential.               
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis 
to NR-3 Neighborhood Residential – High Density for Lots 1 -9, Block 1, North Gate Village, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and 
Prairie Hills Drive).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  (#4288) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Vacating all of that 15’ alley right of way within Block 
13, Arp Addition, Fifth Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, (located north of 
and adjacent to Leisher Road, east of Cribbon Avenue).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.  (#4289) 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, within 
Title 2, Administration and Personnel, and Chapter 3.04, Fiscal Provisions Generally, within Title 
3, Revenue and Finance, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Michael 
O’Donnell, City Attorney, advised the proposed amendment involves 2 sections of city code and 
explained in Chapter 2.04 the amendment proposes to reduce from $35,000.00 to $25,000.00  the 
authority of the executive branch through the Mayor to enter into contracts without governing body 
approval.  Mr. O’Donnell advised the amendment in Chapter 3.04 is to outline the approval process 
of the voucher report which will be to approve as to accuracy only.  He further explained contracts 
currently over $35,000.00 are required to come before the governing body for approval prior to 
being included on a voucher report and advised all other expenditures on the report have previously 
been approved.   Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, provided information relating to the reduction 
from $35,000.00 to $25,000.00 requiring governing body approval.  Ms. Lockman advised it 
currently takes approximately 4-6 hours of staff time for the Purchasing Manager to prep are an 
agenda item for governing body approval and would also impose a burden for multiple city 
department staff members relating to additional work that would be required.  She noted additional 
expenses related to the proposed amendment which includes bid advertising and the extension of 
the time it would require to obtain governing body approval.  Ms. Lockman confirmed the cities 
of Rock Springs, Laramie, Sheridan and Gillette currently follow the State Statute threshold of 
$35,000.00.  Discussion included inflation rate adjustments and the bidding process in relation to 
the current purchasing policy.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on 
second read ing, seconded by Mr. Luna .  Mr. White advised at the Finance Committee the 
recommendation was to amend by substitute dated 10-3-2019, however, since then a clerical error 
was corrected.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated 10 -10-2019, seconded by Mr. 
Shanor.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with 
the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.   Further c omments were made 
regarding the ability of a Department Head actively lobbying for or against proposed legislation, 
inflation rates and  growth, current bidding process and dollar amount threshold relating to 
governing body approval. Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
 [CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for North Gate 
Village, 2nd Filing, a Replat of Lots 7-9, Block 1, North Gate Village, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and Prairie Hills Drive) .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6019) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Inberg -Miller 
Engineers, Cheyenne, for engineering services for design of the 2020 Pavement Management 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried ( in an amount not  to 
exceed $81,600.00). (#7091) 
 
[CA] Grant A greement between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and the  Wyoming 
Department of Health, Aging Division , to provide support services to person s 60 years or older 
under the Title III-B Program Grant for Fiscal Year 2020.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(revenue to the city). (#7092) 
 
  
[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreement between John J. LaFond and the City of Cheyenne  for the 
purchase of real property located at 3425 Christensen Road, in the City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming, for the appraised value of $455,000.00 (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee.   
 
[CA] Spectrum Enterprise Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne  and Charter 
Communications Operating, LLC – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and Wyoming 
Association of Sheriffs and Chiefs of Police -Wyoming Department of Transportation -Highway 
Safety Grants Program for selective traffic enforcement grant – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7026 between the City of Cheyenne and Simon 
Contractors, Cheyenne, for the 2019/2020 M iscellaneous Street Repair Project (2015 -2018 1% 
Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $573,892.25). 
(#7026) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6963 between the City of Cheyenne and AVI, P.C., 
Cheyenne, for engineering design and construction management services for the Allison Road and 
Cribbon Avenue Street Improvements Project to change the Time of Performance (2015-2018 1% 
Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -4-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and  S & S 
Builders, LLC, Cheyenne, for the Greenway Construction - Powderhouse Segment Project  (6th 
Penny Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in  an amount not to exceed 
$319,624.50). (#7093) 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase time clocks from Paycom, Oklahoma City, Oklahoma for the 
City’s payroll and timekeeping software program (Fleet Maintenance, Solid Waste and 1% Sales 
Tax Funding).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $40,350.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid Modification #1 to Purchase Order #18 -112666, Bid #E -25-18 
between the City of Cheyenne, and Floyd’s Truck Center, Inc., Cheyenne (Solid Waste Fund) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Request for approval for winery permit holder GrapesNwine, LLC d/b/a Vintages, 309 
North American Road, Units 11 and 12, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to sell other wines obtained from 
the Wyoming Liquor Division for on -premise consumption pursuant to Wyo. Statute §12 -4-112 
(b) (ii) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Appointment of Thomas D. Cobb as City Engineer for the City of Cheyenne.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Case offered supporting comments of the proposed 
appointment.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointment).   
 
  
 Appointments of Miles Henderson and Jason Bogstie to the Public Transit Advisory Board; 
Suzanne Meisner-Esquibel to the Housing and Community Development Advisory Council; Lori 
Schoene to the Cheyenne Housing Authority Board; and Phillip Regeski to the Planning 
Commission.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (mayoral appointment) and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:23 p.m.  
 
       
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
         
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  October 24, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-10-28 | CLIP_ID: 726 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 726

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 28, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff Whi te.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 14, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 No action was taken on the vouchers as presented. James Johnson inquired about  a 
bottomline total voucher figure.  Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, advised the entire voucher listing 
is available for public viewing on the city’s website.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, explained 
the new process is a motion to approve the accuracy of the voucher report that will be adopted 
with the new ordinance. 
 
 Mr. Case moved to suspend the rules to  allow for consideration to reconsider a contract 
modification to Contract #7026 with Simon Contractors for the 2019/2020 Miscellaneous Street 
Repair Project, seconded by Mr. Cook.   Mayor Orr advised the motion is not debatable.   Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”.  Mr. Case advised that since approval at th e October 14, 2019 City Council meeting  of 
Modification #1 to Contract #7026 with Simon Contractors for the 2019/2020 M iscellaneous 
Street Repair Project, additional information has been received. Mr. Case moved to reconsider the 
contract modification, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mike O’Donnell, City Attorney, explained that 
since the contract modification was approved  2 week s ago, concerns have been received by 
citizens.  Tom Cobb, City Engineer, explained the reason for the modification change order is due 
to safety concerns related to oil in the overlay product on Nationway.  He re-evaluated the project 
due to impending weather concerns  and b ased on that , he looked at contingencies on moving 
forward next summer and possibly milling portions of the road.  During re -evaluation of the 
pavement, it was determined the pavement had healed itself and isn’t an immediate safety concern. 
Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell confirmed that if project rehabilitation is needed next spring, a bid 
will be opened in the future.  Mr. Cobb explained rehabilitation will be  necessary as  current 
conditions won’t be long lasting  and advised his recommendation is the project can be delayed.  
  
Gay Woodhouse, Attorney representing STC Construction, provided concerns about the change 
order stating the original work was completed on Nationway in 2018 and in order to do more work 
in the area,  it should be considered a new project which would go out to bid.  Ms. Woodhouse 
provided a handout related to bid requirements in state statutes and reviewed the information. She 
explained particular products were increased in the change order without reasoning, work should 
be done under warranty since it appears the product failed and the additional work is almost double 
the original amount of the project.  Ms. Woodhouse spoke about the bid process and asked that the 
bid be  dismissed and re -opened before the star t of the project.  Gle nn Nicholson, Simon 
Contractors, explained the failure was not related to workmanship and advised it is not a warranty 
issue.  Mr. Nicholson explained  the City Engineer’s office approved a  1.5” mil overlay on the 
surface which is contrary to the normal 2 ” mil overlay.  He inquired as to how the  Civic Center 
Commons parking lot  project for the municipal building was awarded.  Comment was made on 
the chip seal program and maintaining and re-evaluating Nationway repairs.  Motion to reconsider 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Mr. Case moved to postpone to 
11/12/19, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.         
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, within 
Title 2, Administration and Personnel, and Chapter 3.04, Fiscal Provisions Generally, within Title 
3, Revenue and Finance, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Joe 
Dougherty commented on proposed changes regarding the voucher approval process, opposing 
approval as to accuracy only.  Mayor Orr clarified members of the public are allowed to comment 
during public comment periods, however, she will not allow questioning staff from the podium.  
She advised members of the public can ask questions and staff will provide responses in writing.   
James Johnson supported voucher approval as to accuracy and advised the State takes a sample of 
transactions and tests them for accuracy.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. White moved to amend Sections 
D (4) and D (6) by changing $25,000.00 to $35,000.00, seconded by Mr.  Laybourn.  Mr. Shanor 
advised he had previously provided information on the topic  to Mr. D ougherty in an email  and 
spoke against increasing the amount to $35,000.00.  It was requested consideration be given for 
not changing the bid threshold.  Comment was made that members of the governing bo dy have 
been informed that payment of vouchers can’t be disapproved  and the contract process is fiscal 
oversite.  Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, explained the process of placing an item 
on the agenda that the Purchasing Manager must follow.  Ms. Lockman advised it is estimated the 
proposed amendment would  add an estimated additional 26 items per year to the agenda  and 
explained procurement types determine if a contract is needed  and that purchase orders are only 
used in certain instances.  TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager, explained the current process of 
purchasing a piece of equipment and advised that with the new proposal, a formal contract would 
be required.  Mr. O’Donnell clarified that a purchase order is a contract and tends to run below the 
dollar amount threshold.  Mr. Barttelbort provided additional information relating to equipment  
purchases and the bid process.   Upon request,  Kris Jones, City Clerk, read the  amendment 
language.  Discussion continued on the proposed process, relationship between City Council and 
the Mayor, the current legal opinion that the governing body does not have the authority to deny a 
voucher, contract approval process oversite and opposition of the amendment .  A point of order 
was raised by Mr. Laybourn relating to appropriate procedure on postponing an item on 3rd reading. 
  
Mayor ruled the point is well taken and requested  clarification on postponing before the 
amendment is voted on.  Mr. O’Donnel advised the amendment motion must be disposed of prior 
to a motion to postpone. Motion to amend failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” – Mayor Orr, Mr. 
Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr.  Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. 
Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.     Mr. Cook moved to postpone to11/12/2019, seconded by Mr. 
Case.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bartelbort  advised he would provide additional information relating to 
clarification on professional services and contracts prior to the 11/4/2019 Finance Committee 
meeting.  Comments were made opposing the motion to postpone .  Motion to postpone carried.   
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Esquibel and Mr. 
Roybal voting “no”.   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the fiscal year 2020 budget of the City of Cheyenne

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-11-25 | CLIP_ID: 728 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 728

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 25, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, and Jeff White.  Absent:  Dicky Shanor.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by on e motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda .  Upon request by Mr.  White, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr . Case  and Mr.  Roybal, Agenda Item # 12 (a)  was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.     
 
 [CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 12, 2019.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development –
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, amending 
Ordnance No. 3841 by modifying  certain standards of the adopted district, Planned Unit 
Development (PUD), for Lots 1-3, Block 1, Lots 1-4, Block 2, (less that portion platted to Pointe 
Plaza, 2nd Filing), and Lots 1-2, Block 3, Pointe Plaza; a portion of Lot 2, Block 1, Pointe Plaza, 
2nd Filing; Lots 4-23, Block 1, Pointe Plaza, 3rd Filing; all of Point Plaza 4th Filing; and all of Pointe 
Plaza, 5th Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of 
Gardenia Drive and Powderhouse Road) Reporting for  Public Services Committee , Dr. Rinne  
moved to approve on second reading with Planning Commission recommendations, seconded by 
Mr. Cook.  Dr. Rinne provided clarification relating to the Planning Commission’s 
recommendation to approve with allowing the sign exception to be allowed within the entire Point 
Plaza instead of just an exception for the Delta Dental property.  Joe Patterson, Homes by 
Guardian, requested consideration to limit the exception for the Delta Dental property.  Patt i 
Guzman, Delta Dental, adv ised the corporation is mandated by sign requirements and the 
exception would allow them to comply.  Discussion included a possible amendment on third 
reading of the ordinance to restrict the exception to the requesting party only instead of the entire 
Point Plaza property.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Directing all revenue from City of Cheyenne Contract No. 5902 to be 
split evenly between the City of Cheyenne, Solid Waste Enterprise Fund, the Board of Public 
Utilities, and the newly established Belvoir Ranch Recreation Special Revenue Fund.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6023) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Certifying the East Dell Range Boulevard/US 30 Corridor Study – 
referred to Public Services Committee.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  White.  Dr. 
Rinne moved amend page 2, 6 th paragraph to read “That, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body 
hereby certifies the East Dell Range Boulevard/U.S. 30 Corridor Study” dated Sept ember 2019, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.   Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting 
“no”. (#6024) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts in the 
amount of $34,137.07 pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-502 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying the Interim Cheyenne Area Master Transportation Plan – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 Preliminary Plat for Alliance Addition, 4 th Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 
Alliance Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of 
Ridge Road and Jackson Street) . Dr. Rinne moved to a cknowledge the Planning Commission’s 
recommendation to approve with staff condition s #1-3, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 2019-2020 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne 
Firefighters, Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters  (Postponed from 
November 12, 2019). Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve, sec onded 
by Mr.  Luna.  Mr. White moved to amend Section 17, “Hours of Work” and Section 21, 
“Classification of Employment and Basic Wages” as approved by the City Attorney’s Office dated 
November 18, 2019, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. (#7107) 
 
 Land Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate 
Management, LLC, for the purchase of real property described as a portion of Lot 1, Block 1 , 
P.A.S. Subdivision, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for the appraised value of 
$329,630.00, for economic development purposes (General Fund) (Postponed from November 12, 
2019).  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $329, 630.00, seconded by Mr.  
Case.  Mr. White moved to postpone to December 9, 2019, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Joe Dougherty 
requested additional information relating to abatement and remediation expenses and opposed the 
proposed postponement.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. 
  
 Minimum Revenue Guarantee Payment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the 
Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team (CRAFT) for payments to Sky West Airlines for 
providing scheduled flight services between Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Dallas/Fort Worth, Texas  
(General Fund Reserves).  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $623,501.52, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present. (#7108)

grant from the U.S. Department of Homeland Security ( DHS), FEMA, Grant Programs 
Directorate, SHSP Grant Fiscal Year 2019 in the amount of $102,434.00 for the Cheyenne 
Regional Response Team #7 (DHS Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7109)

Laramie, WY, for the Subsurface Methane Investigation at the Happy Jack Landfill (Solid Waste 
Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $39,979.00). (#7110) 
 
[CA] Professional Service s Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, and C2R Landfill 
Equipment & Service Environmental Systems, Huffman, Texas, for the purchase and installation 
of two pumps and control panels for leachate wells NT -3 and NT -4 at the Happy Jack Landfill 
(Solid Waste  Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$39,695.00). (#7111)

Communications Technology, Inc., Sheridan, WY, and the City of Cheyenne (revenue to the city).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7112)      
 
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6625 between the City of Cheyenne and Western 
Research and Development, Ltd., for additional design services for the On -Call Traffic 
Engineering Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax ).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an 
amount not to exceed $36,398.10). (#6625) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification  #1 to Contract #7083 between the City of Cheyenne and STC 
Construction Company, Cheyenne, for the 2019 Wearing Course Project .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#7083) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6885 between the City of Cheyenne and Simons 
Contractors, Cheyenne, for the 2018 Mountain Road and 20 th Street Pavement Rehabilitation 
project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6885)

Tennis Courts, Inc., Loveland, CO, for the Jaycee Park Tennis Court Asphalt Overlay and 
Resurfacing Project (Recreation Mill Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount 
not to exceed $95,000.00). (#7113) 
 
  
[CA] Bar and grill liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Goalz DH 
Cheyenne, WY LLC d/b/a Dog Haus, 3838 Atkin St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. 
 
 Voucher Report. There were no comments or discussion related to the voucher report. 
 
 Revenue Report.   Pat Crank, Representing Cheyenne Frontier Days (CFD), requested 
consideration for the City to stop charging CFD and other entities for additional services by the 
Cheyenne Fire & Rescue and Police Departments during events.  He suggested the city build the 
budget to accommodate the extra expenses associated with additional security and services and 
adjust staff accordingly.  Mayor Orr provided information relating to staffing restraints that have 
been placed on the number of full -time employees the city is allowed to hire by the governing 
body and reported on budgetary issues the city faces with respect to staffing levels.   Mayor Orr 
explained that the city researched how other municipalities handle large events and explained 
multiple events are charged for the necessary additional services.     
 
 Under other business: Kris Jones, City Clerk, explained that the contract modification to 
Contract #7026 with Simon Contractors in the amount of $579,892.25, was reconsidered at the 
October 28 th meeting of the governing body and was back before the governing body at their 
November 12 th meeting for consideration.  She explained that the item, because it had been 
reconsidered, was before the governing with a motion and second and advised a vote was not 
taken.   She further explained that for procedural and record keeping purposes, the governing body 
needs to record a vote on the item.  Mayor Orr called for a vote and the motion to approve failed.  
Voting “no” all members of the governing body present.    
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:42 p.m.  
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-12-09 | CLIP_ID: 730 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 730

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
December 9, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case,  Bryan Cook,  Pete Laybourn,  Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Absent: K en Esquibel.  Also present: Kris Jones, City 
Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.   
 
 Mr. Laybourn expressed appreciation to Jason Sanchez, Community Recreation & Events 
Deputy Director and his crew for cleaning snow in the downtown area for the Christmas Parade.  
Justin Gorman, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce , thanked Mr. Sanchez and his staff, 
Mayor Orr and other city staff for their assistance before and during the parade. Mr. Sanchez 
advised he has the privilege of working with a great group of people , noting e veryone came 
together with positive attitudes to assist.  Mr. Laybourn  complemented the Depot Plaza and 
Municipal Building seasonal decorations.   
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Mr. Luna moved to approve  Consent Agenda items , 
seconded by Mr . Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing bod y 
present.     
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 25, 2019.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meetings of the Governing Body on October 22, 2019 and November 
25, 2019.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development –
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, amending 
Ordnance No. 3841 by modifying certain standards of the adopted district, Pla nned Unit 
Development (PUD), for Lots 1-3, Block 1, Lots 1-4, Block 2, (less that portion platted to Pointe 
Plaza, 2nd Filing), and Lots 1-2, Block 3, Pointe Plaza; a portion of Lot 2, Block 1, Pointe Plaza, 
2nd Filing; Lots 4-23, Block 1, Pointe Plaza, 3rd Filing; all of Point Plaza 4th Filing; and all of Pointe 
Plaza, 5th Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of 
Gardenia Drive and Powderhouse Road) Reporting for  Public Services Committee , Dr. Rinne  
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
by substitute dated 12/2/2019, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne explained testimony was heard 
  
at the Public Services Committee from Joe Patterson, Guardian Companies, and the original intent 
was to change the PUD just for Delta Dental and that the proposed substitute will change it back 
to its original intent.  Joe Patterson, Guardian Companies, explained the original amendment was 
specific for their lot and block and he supports the amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body  present.  Main m otion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4291)  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts in the 
amount of $34,137.07 pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16 -4-502.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6025) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying the Interim Cheyenne Area Master Tra nsportation Plan .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6026) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Providing for the review and approval of a Federal Mineral Royalty 
Capital Construction Account Grant application to the Wyoming State Loan and Investment Board 
on behalf of the Governing Body for the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer District for two Sewer 
Collector Rehabilitation Projects – Southern Interceptor (Priority One) and Terry Road Sewer 
Collector (Priority Two) – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a Land and Water 
Conservation Fund Grant for replacement of the playground in South Lions Park – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving Sanitary Sewer Service, by Outside User Sanitary Sewer 
Services Agreement, for Laramie County Fire District No. 2, for Tract 28, excep ting the East 40 
feet of said tract, Dell Range Addition, 2nd Filing, Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, 
of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Pursuant to W.S. §15-1-404 (b) certifying compliance with W.S. §15-1-
402, for a City-initiated annexation for portion of land generally located between Old Happy Jack 
Road and Happy Jack Road, and west of I -80 – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor 
– Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing a street name change from “Cribbon Ave nue” to “South 
Cribbon Avenue” for the portion of the street located between West Jefferson Road and Pinto 
Lane, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 Land Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate 
Management, LLC, for the purchase of real property described as a portion of Lot 1, Block 1 , 
P.A.S. Subdivision, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for the appraised value of 
$329,630.00, for economic development purposes (General Fund) (Postponed from November 25, 
2019).  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
  
$329,630.00, seconded by Mr.  White.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone to December 19, 2019, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, explained the fundamental purpose 
for the proposed purchase is to gain access to the property including the Ice & Events Center 
property which is critical.  He advised lack of access is holding up  purchase of the front tract of 
Hitching Post property.  Mr. O’Donnell explained  back buildings #3, 4 and 5 are included in the 
proposed purchase including the access at the request of the property seller.  He further explained 
the City doesn’t have to take ownership and commit to abatement and rehabilitation as they could 
sell the additional property and abatement cost would be the responsibility of a future purchaser.  
Mr. O’Donnell advised the reason for the motion to postpone is to allow for more time to acquire 
abatement and rehabilitation cost estimates which have been difficult to obtain.   Upon inquiry, 
Mr. O’Donnell  explained the Attorney’s office, along with additional City staff, are working 
diligently on obtaining estimates as timing is vital for adjacent projects.   Support was expressed 
for a future staff presentation outlining the City’s cost estimates and all potential options.  Brendan 
Ames, Chief Economic Development Officer, upon inquiry, explained an adjacent private property 
project is underway and the access point  to the property (proposed new hotel)  is critical.  He 
advised the interested developer will need appropriate access and without it, the project could be 
halted.  Comments were made in support of the project , importance of obtaining expenses 
associated with the project and how capital construction project expenditures can affect available 
funding for salaries , health insurance , etc.  Discussion followed related to the item being 
postponed, possibly reconsidered or being brought back as a new agenda item if it fails.  Kris 
Jones, City Clerk, and Mr. O’Donnell, upon inquiry, provided information on reconsideration and 
reintroduction of the item if it fails, explaining it can be reconsidered at the next council meeting 
or reintroduced at a later time . They  provided additional procedural information re lating to 
reconsideration and agenda item introduction processes.  Concern was expressed relating to  the 
message that may be interpreted by potential buyers by letting the item fail.  Motion to postpone 
failed.  Voting “yes” – Mayor Orr, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Discussion topics included: Time and effort 
involving the project  (one employee alone has worked in excess of 200 staff hours) , Attorney’s 
office acquiring requested project expenses expediently, i mportance of project and time frame 
relating to potential investors, appearance and perception of continu ed postponements vers us 
reintroduction, support and opposition were voiced regarding postponement, potential for 
additional development on the west side of town and opinion that the roadway should have been 
acquired when the Ice and Events Center was acquired . Ms. Jones, upon inquiry, explained only 
someone voting on the prevailing side would be eligible to make a motion to reconsider the item 
at the next council meeting.  Main motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mayor Orr, Mr. Laybourn and 
Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.    
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Western Research and 
Development, Ltd, for 2020 Signal Upgrade Design Services (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Saddle Ridge, LLC, to 
outline the responsibilities for the donation of land and for the management of a multi -purpose 
non-motorized pedestrian and bicycle pathway, park and landscape amenities in a portion of 
Saddle Ridge, 7th and 9th Filings (Community Facilities Fees) – referred to Finance Committee.   
  
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and WJE, LLC, to outline 
the responsibilities for the donation of land and for the management of a multi -purpose non -
motorized pedestrian and bicycle pathway, park and landscape amenities in a portion of Saddle 
Ridge, 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th, 12th and 13th Filings  (Community Facilities Fees) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming Department of 
Transportation for the 2020 Epoxy Striping Project ( 2015-2018 1% Sales Tax ) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6986 between the City of Cheyenne and TC 
Solutions, LLC, for Owner’s  Representative Services for the Splash Pad Project – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -4-20 for furnishing one (1) John Deere 1575 Terrain Cut with 
Comfort Cab Commercial Front Mower (less mower deck), Rotary Broom, Snow Blower,  and 
Blade, for the City of Cheyenne, Facilities Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Sax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 Voucher Report. There were no comments or discussion related to the voucher report.  
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Christmas holiday schedule, the regularly 
scheduled City Council meeting on Monday, December 23, 2019 will instead be held on Thursday, 
December 19, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in the Municipal Building, City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Bill Gonzales, Street and Alle y Department, asked for support for fair compensation for 
his crew when they have to report to work as essential employees when the City has a weather -
related closure. Upon inquiry, he advised the solution he would like is that his staff be compensated 
as if they are working on a holiday as they are paid their regular rate of pay in these scenarios. 
 
 Kris Jones , City Clerk, advised the following Public Hearing announcement was 
inadvertently left off agenda and read into record:  Announcement of  a Public Hearing to be held 
December 19, 2019 at 6:00pm in City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming 
Statute §15 -1-402 exist for the North ½ of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie County , 
Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard). 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:41 p.m.  
 
 
 
  
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  December 17, 2019

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2019-12-19 | CLIP_ID: 731 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 731

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
December 19, 2019 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor 
and Jeff White.  Absent: Ken Esquibel and Dr. Mark Rinne.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Mr. Shanor abstained due to affiliation with agenda item #9.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, 
agenda item #11 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining 
items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.     
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 9, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for the North ½ of Lot 4, Williams 
Subdivision, Laramie County Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection o f Marshall Road 
and Storey Boulevard).   Tawn Hillenbrand, Planning and Development , provided information 
relating to the compliance of annexation requirements.  Ed Clark, area property owner, expressed 
concerns with variances needed for access , neighborhood safety and property values.  Mr. Clark 
advised he is otherwise not opposed to the annexation.  There being no further comments or input 
and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the North 
1/2 of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the 
intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County LR Low -density Residential 
to City LR Low -density Residential for the North 1/2 of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard) 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Providing for the review and approval of a Federal Mineral Royalty 
Capital Construction Account Grant application to the Wyoming State Loan and Investment Board 
on behalf of the Governing Body for the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer District for two Sewer 
Collector Rehabilitation Projects – Southern Interceptor (Priority One) and Terry Road Sewer 
Collector (Priority Two).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6027) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a Land and Water 
Conservation Fund Grant for replacement of the playground in South Lions Park .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6028) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Approving Sanitary Sewer Service, by Outside User Sanitary Sewer 
Services Agreement, for Laramie County Fire District No. 2, for Tract 28, excep ting the East 40 
feet of said tract, Dell Range Addition, 2nd Filing, Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, 
of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved 
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Rod Stone, area property owner, commented on flooding issues 
relating to the development of Thomas Heights and how it has affected his property.  Mr. Stone 
inquired about a detention pond in the area to help eliminate flooding issues.  Michael O’Donnell, 
City Attorney, upon inquiry, reported the E ngineering Department is working on 3 different 
options to solve issues.  He advised a meeting will be held for residents in the area  to explain the 
options, one of which is a detention pond.  Mr. O’Donnell explained a  cooperative conversation 
with all parties involved is ongoing  and the goal is to reach a stage where people downhill from 
Thomas Heights will no longer experience flooding.  Mr. Stone encouraged the governing body to 
support Laramie County Fire District #2 with this request.  Jason Caughey, Laramie County Fire 
District #2 Fire Chief, upon inquiry, explained prior to the development of Thomas Heights, they 
did not have to service their sewer system in 11 years and since then, they service it twice a month 
at the sum of $13,000.00 for 6 months.  He advised engineers involved explained this is due to 
saturation of the area.  It was noted the saturated area isn’t being considered for a detention pond 
and that Laramie County Fire District #2 would be interested in continuing conversations to find 
a remedy. He further explained their property consists of 2 lots and 10 acres.  Comments were 
made in support of the agreement, concern relating to flooding issues in the area and finding a 
resolution to prevent further property damage.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  (#6029) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Pursuant to W.S. §15-1-404 (b) certifying compliance with W.S. §15-1-
402, for a City-initiated annexation for portion of land generally located between Old Happy Jack 
Road and Happy Jack Road, and west of I -80.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr . 
Shanor moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute dated 
12/16/2019, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor explained the substitute corrects a clerical error 
in the entitlement from I-80 to I-25.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#6030) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing a street name change from “Cribbon Aven ue” to “South 
Cribbon Avenue” for the portion of the street located between West Jefferson Road and Pinto 
  
Lane, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
(#6031) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Designating local banks as deposito ries for municipal funds – referred 
to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Office on 
Violence Against Women OVW-2020-17563 Improving Criminal Justice Responses to Domestic 
Violence, Dating Violence, Sexual Assault, and Stalking Grant Program CFDA Number 16.590 -
Grants to Encourage Arrests Policies and Enforcement of Protection Orders Program – referred to

 RESOLUTION – Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has agreed

and Investments, State Loan and Investment Board, for Countywide Consensus Block Allocat ion 
Grant Funding from BFY 2011/2012 in the total amount of $14,562.00. (Re-allocation of funds) - 
refer to Finance Committee.  Mr. Shanor declared a conflict of interest due to his current 
employment, vacated chambers and abstained from discussion and  voting. Mr. White explained 
the item was reviewed under other items for the agenda  at the 12/16/2019 Finance Committee 
meeting and that provisions of Title 2 have been met.  Mr.  White moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. Luna.  Mr. White explained the Mayor of Albin brought forward the resolution which is time 
sensitive relating to the application submittal and that the Town of Albin desires to submit the 
grant application  to SLIB for funding . Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#6032)  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Western Research and

[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming Department of

[CA] Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Girl Scouts of Montana and

[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between  Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of

[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6986 between the City of Ch eyenne and TC

[CA] Amendment No. #1 to Contract # 7041 to Lease No. 8 -09466 between the State of

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -4-20 for furnishing one (1) John Deer e 1575 Terrain Cut with

Blade, for the City of Cheyenne, Facilities Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -8-20 for furnishing One (1) Bomb Squad Robot, for the City of

to Finance Committee. 
 
  
 Reappointments of Brent Groesbeck and Joe Patterson to the Contractor Licensing Board; 
Nicholas Pickering and Ray Fertig, Jr. to the Building Code Board of Appeals; Lee Filer to the 
Personnel Commission; Patrice Gapen and Stephen Ganison to the Housing and Community 
Development Advisory Council; John Brewster and Jason Bogstie to the Historic Preservation 
Board; Greg Palmquist to the Planning Commission; William Beaman to the City/County Frontier 
Park Improvement Joint Powers Board, Jeffie Wiggins -McMullen to the Fire Civil Service 
Commission and Patti Riesland, Howard Hill Sr., Justin  Whitacre, Michelle Woerner, Julie 
Tucker, Daryl Hensel, Mary Richey, Marsha Dial, Dr. Carole Martin and Steven Bates to the 
Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities.  Mr . Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  
Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present  with the 
exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointment).   
 
Appointments of Brian Casey to the Contractor Licensing Board; Julie Goode to the 
Building Code Board of Appeals; Carla Winkler to the Poli ce Civil Service Commission; Haylee 
Chenchar to the  Downtown Development Authority Board; Linda Burt to the Personnel 
Commission; David Cook and JC Manalo to the Housing and Community Development Advisory 
Council; Cathie Tabor-Douglas and Milward Simpson to the Historic Preservation Board; David 
Janes to the Laramie County Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board; Brian Fenimore to the Board 
of Adjustment and James Bowers to the Planning Commission.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointment).  Mayor Orr expressed 
appreciation to volunteers. 
 
 Appointment of Anthony F. Ross as Senior Municipal Court Judge effe ctive January 3, 
2020.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present  with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote 
(mayoral appointment).   
 
 Voucher Report.   Frank Cole  commented on dirt left by a contractor in the area of a 
Mountain Road project and requested it to be removed. 
 
 Revenue Report.  No comments were made. 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, advised he would be offering an opinion on a petition 
filed by James Johnson, a Mayoral Candidate.  Mr. O’Donnell provided history on the issues as 
follows:  M ayor Orr declared a pothole emergency in August . The Engineering Department 
solicited bids, 2 responses were received, and the low bid was accepted.  Mr. O’Donnell explained 
the high bidder was disqualified as work couldn’t be completed in the required timeframe and the 
Engineering Department then reduced the scope of the bid quantity.  The bid then came before 
governing body and was declined and a contract was never entered into.  The petition was filed 
pursuant to WY § 15-1-113(j) related to malfeasance alleging a bid was entered into and the same 
consideration to alter the bid was not allowed by the higher bidder.  Mr. O’Donnell recommended 
that the petition be dismissed as there was never a contract entered into .  He explained  the 
  
Purchasing Manager does not enter int o contracts but drafts documents , and in his opinion, the 
petition seeks to lure governing body into action  against the statute cited in the petition.  Mr. 
O’Donnell provided additional information relating to the allegations made by Mr. Johnson and 
stated the petition lacks validity and suggests the governing body take action that would be against 
the law.  Mr. O’Donnell, upon inquiry, further advised that in his opinion, the petition should be 
dismissed with prejudice and that no hearing or action is nece ssary, noting the city proper ly 
followed the bid requirement process and that no malfeasance took place.  Upon further inquiry 
from, Mr. O’Donnell advised the petition doesn’t require any action as it doesn’t require a hearing 
and is not required to be placed on an agenda for consideration but could be if that is the desire of 
the governing body.  Mr. Case advised he forwarded the Attorney’s legal opinion regarding the 
petition to Mr. Johnson.  Prior to public comment, Mayor Orr advised those speaking on the issue 
would be allowed 3 minutes to comment.  James Johnson commented on the petition and explained 
that he did not intend it to target Knife River vs. Simon Contractors and it was only filed to address 
the issue during a public hearing  setting.  Mr. Johnson a dded he did not intend to violate state 
statute and the purpose of the petition was to hold accountability for the bidding process.  Mayor 
Orr, a t the conclusion of  the 3-minute response period, asked Mr. Johnson  be removed from  
council chambers as he was not following procedure and continued to comment.  Concern was 
expressed related to the petition being signed by many people and being dismissed on a 
technicality.  Upon inquiry related to potential recourse if the petition is dismissed, Mr. O’Donnell 
reported t his process is an anomaly state statute in Wyoming law  and that there is no revie w 
process required.  He further advised  the governing body may place the item on an agenda and 
either accept the request for a public hearing or can simply dismiss the petition request. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, th e meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:41 p.m.  
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
